# Pilgrim's Progress: notes for Claude

A gentle side-scrolling story walk for kids ages 7 to 10, following Bunyan's *The Pilgrim's Progress*. No build step, no dependencies. **Read `HANDOFF.md` ("START HERE") first in a new chat.** These notes were trimmed in build 140: the build-by-build history of Levels 1 and 2 was folded into the rules and code maps below; the full history is in git (`CLAUDE.md` at build 137).

## Files

- **`index.html` IS THE GAME** (owner, build 119: "let's make main link the real game"): the engine, the title page and Level 1 in the page; Level 2 in `slough.js` (object `SL`); Level 3 in `hill.js` (object `HL`). Pictures, frame packs and voices in `assets/`.
- `story-lab.html`: a few lines that send old links and Home Screen icons on to `index.html`; it keeps a build stamp so an old cached copy notices and reloads. Bump its stamp too.
- `book/pilgrims-progress-part-one.txt`: Bunyan's own text of Part One (public domain, no later editors), section numbers like `{18}`.
- `style-lab.html`: the art-style test page (the layered look; settled, no longer kept in step with the game). `concept-2200.html`: year-2200 concept pictures, waiting on the owner's choice (a quiet, gentle apocalypse; four directions on buttons, `#A3` = direction + version).
- `editor.js` (object `ED`, build 184): the editor's skip arrows, only with `?edit` (see Testing).
- `cross.js` (object `CR`, build 390): Level 5, the Cross (see "LEVEL 5 IN THE GAME"); contents line V; `?level=5&at=cross` starts just short of the cross.
- `rooms.js` (object `RM`, build 261): Level 4's Interpreter's House, the door, rooms 1 to 7 and leaving (see "THE INTERPRETER'S HOUSE IN THE GAME"); contents line IV; `?room=door|1..7|leave`.
- `mannequin.js` (object `MQ`), `assets/mannequin.json`, `mannequin.html`, `tools/mannequin/`: Christian drawn in code from first principles (a skeleton that walks, clothes that follow it), a LAB, not yet used by the game (see "The mannequin").
- `keepsakes.js` (object `KS`, build 176): the pause menu and the two things he carries seen up close (see "Keepsakes and the pause menu").
- `manifest.webmanifest`, `assets/icon-180.png`, `icon-512.png`: the Home Screen app (build 137, see Phones).
- `concept/`: concept pictures shown to the owner (not used by the game): `level4-moodboard-paintings.jpg` (each place beside its painting, cropped from the owner's phone screenshots of Wikimedia, with its measured colours and the one detail to quote as a homage; all public domain but the Chardin photo, CC BY-SA, credited on the sheet); `level4-moodboard.jpg` (build 211 era: a painting, a light and a palette for each place of Level 4, the gate, the narrow way, the house and its seven rooms, rooms 6 and 7 marked as the darker rooms behind a contents-page switch; rough code sketches, waiting for the owner); four rough sketches for Level 4 after Rembrandt (`level4-four-sketches.jpg`: A the gate opened wide with Good-will in the light and the dark castle far left; B inside the gate, the narrow way straight as a rule to the far light; C the Interpreter's house by night; D inside, the Interpreter with a candle and the picture on the wall; drawn in code with stand-in figures, **waiting for the owner's choice**; their walls show a grid of stones, which the owner dislikes: in the game the light must be one soft pool); Level 3's four sketches after four masters (the owner chose John Martin), and two reference paintings from the image model for the hill's fire (the first too loud and red, the second not liked).
- `vision/` (vision chat, after build 393): the plan for the whole game: `README.md` (the vision, ranked fixes for levels 1 to 5, a 16-level shape for the rest, decisions waiting on the owner) and five research reports (how artists and adaptations depicted the book, levels 1 to 5 against the book, levels 1 to 5 art and engagement, levels 6 onward from the book). Ideas only; each departure needs the owner's yes.
- `script.html`: the script chooser (see "LESS WAITING"); kept (owner, build 175) for its plainer version of every line.
- `tools/`: `playtest.js`, `phone-touch-test.js`, `phone-test-3.js` (tests); `stills3.js` (Level 3 stills at any moment, build 239's handover); `stills2.js` (the same for Level 2, build 259: the condition may use `k`, the tick count, e.g. `"k >= 1800"` with `at = bog` is wading); `make-voice.py` (voices); `video-frames.py`, `gesture-frames.py`, `cutout.py`, `flat-feet.py` (turning Google's videos and pictures into game frames). `pack-check.py` (build 362): looks through every pack for cutting faults (filled gaps, holes, ghosts, pale edges, jumps); it only points, look before changing. `clip-cut.py` (after build 360, the Level 4 videos chat): ONE command from a clip of a painted figure on white paper to a room sheet: `python3 tools/clip-cut.py walk|gesture clip.mp4 assets/name.webp --mirror`. It cuts by the rules of builds 289, 320, 324 (paper is only what reaches the frame's edge through near-white; white inside stays, a beard, except shut-in paper low between the feet and small patches of the paper's very colour; the edge un-whitened and pulled in), for a walk finds ONE WHOLE STRIDE (the step is the first lag at which the figure repeats, the stride two steps), holds the torso still, measures the planted foot's slide (`vpx`), scores the loop's seam against ordinary frame-to-frame change (it says LOOK AT THE SEAM when it jumps), prints the META for `rooms.js`, and writes `name-strip.jpg` (every frame on dark and light) and `name-seam.jpg` (last three and first three frames) to look at; delete those two after looking. Tried on the three past free clips: the same loops as the hand cuts (Christian 51..78, the Interpreter 42..69, both 28 frames, `vpx` 6), the beard whole, cleaner edges; about 30 s a clip. Needs `pip install opencv-python-headless numpy pillow`. `video-frames.py` assumes a figure walking RIGHT and at most 488 video px tall: mirror a left-walking video and scale a taller one down first; check its `vpx` by hand.

## The book

- **STAY CLOSE TO THE STORY (owner, build 48: "can you put sticking close to story in the notes").** Bunyan's order of events and his words where a child can follow them. Before adding, moving or rewording a story moment, read it in the book file and keep to it; where the game departs, tell the owner and list it below.
- **CHECK EVERY LEVEL AGAINST THE BOOK (owner, build 106: "please check everything with book to make sure it lines up (can you put this comment in notes for future level building)").** Before building a level and again before calling it done: read its whole stretch of the book, then go through the level line by line and moment by moment: (1) every line is the book's sentence, shortened and made plain, said by the person who says it in the book; (2) the moments come in the book's order; (3) nothing is shown that the book contradicts. List every departure here and tell the owner.
- **The words are Bunyan's, made plain (owner, build 56: "bring the words closer to book").** His sentences, shortened, the oldest words made plain.
- **The storyteller only tells the story (owner, build 108: "narrator should only say what's relevant to story - little side messages can tell user what to do").** Anything that tells the child what to do is a side message: a `hint()` while walking, or a `TIP` line inside a talk (quiet italics, no name, never spoken or recorded).
- **How gently to tell the dark parts** (Apollyon, the Valley of the Shadow, Faithful's death, Giant Despair) needs the owner's ruling, level by level.

### Departures from the book

**Level 1** (`{10}` to `{28}`): the book's first scenes at home (`{11}`, `{12}`) are left out; the level opens with him already turned from his house, his wife in the door; two of Evangelist's lines cut to shorten the talk ("why do you stand still?" and its answer, `{16}`); at his door the child may choose "Stop and look back": he stops, his wife calls, he answers "I cannot stay. I must find the way, for all of us" (ours; in the book he never stops; "Run on" is the book's way); "Go home" at Obstinate's question, and its words (`HOME`), are ours (nothing is lost); Obstinate and Pliable each stand in a lit doorway of their own as he runs by (the book only says they were neighbours); the city wall, the gate, "At the edge of the city stood a great gate" and the end line are ours; the talk with Pliable on the plain (`{25}` to `{28}`) was moved to open Level 2 (owner, build 68). **Build 164 (less waiting):** cut: "So he did not look behind him, but fled on", "In a little time they overtook him", Christian's "Come with me, neighbors! I have seen the light" (choice path); reworded plainer: the opening's second line, Christian's first answer to Evangelist, Obstinate and Pliable named in one line, "That cannot be", "see for yourselves", Obstinate's and Pliable's parting lines, Pliable's "let's walk faster" and Christian's answer (Level 2's opening).

**Level 2** (`{25}` to `{36}`): "Many fears and doubts came over him, and settled in that place" is the storyteller's, while he wades (in the book Help explains it to the dreamer afterwards, `{33}`); Christian's first opening line puts "no more crying, nor sorrow" before the shining garments (the other way round in `{27}`); "in the mist" the steps were hard to see is ours (the book: "against change of weather", `{35}`); the choice to go back with Pliable is ours; he sinks deeper and deeper as he crosses (owner, build 114; the book only says he "began to sink"); Pliable's angry speech shortened; Christian's answer to Help shortened ("that I might escape the wrath to come" dropped); "Go on your way" spoken by Help (the book only reports that he bid him); "It is the Slough of Despond still" (`{34}`, said by Help to the dreamer) is the storyteller's (build 409); the steps' sentence (`{35}`, said to the dreamer) was given to the storyteller, then cut in build 391 (the lights finding the stones show it); the lights settling on the steps are our picture; Help turns and walks away after bidding him go (owner's choice, build 120; in the book he stays and speaks to the dreamer, `{33}` to `{36}`); Help tells Christian the slough's history and the steps before bidding him go, seen as memories of labourers and travellers (build 417; in the book he tells the dreamer, after). **Build 370:** the shining city seen in the sky is ours (his thought; his home in the mist was tried and taken out in build 371); the fears shown as darkness and drum beats are ours (the book: they "settled in that place"; build 380). **Build 368:** cut: "went talking over the plain", "they drew near to a very miry bog", Help's "What are you doing there?" and Christian's answer; Christian faces Pliable in the quarrel and Pliable is seen going into the mist before the choice (ours, staging). **Build 164:** cut: "Because of the burden on his back, Christian began to sink" (shown) and "So he gave him his hand. And Help drew him out" (shown); reworded plainer: Pliable's angry speech ("you can have your fine country all to yourself"), his getting out, Christian's answer to Help.

**Level 3** (`{37}` to `{60}`; checked line by line in build 140): see "Level 3" below.

## Working with the owner

- **Repo `Twhidbey/pilgrims-progress`, branch `main`. Pushing `main` publishes the game** (GitHub Pages, https://twhidbey.github.io/pilgrims-progress/, 1 to 2 minutes). Commit and push after every change, then tell the owner it is live; a phone may show an old copy, so suggest `?v=N`. **Always give two links when a build is live (owner, build 187: "can you output [the editor link] by default and make note in notes"):** the game, `https://twhidbey.github.io/pilgrims-progress/?v=N`, and the editor link, `https://twhidbey.github.io/pilgrims-progress/?edit&v=N` (skip arrows, see Testing); add `&level=2` or `&level=3` when the change is in that level.
- **Build stamp** (`id="build"`, bottom right): `build N · date` in `index.html` and `story-lab.html`. Bump N on **every** push that changes the game, so one number means one push. Keep the form `id="build">... build N` (the page's self-update reads it). **Currently build 469.** The two script tags at the end of `index.html` carry `?v=N` (`slough.js?v=`, `hill.js?v=`): set them to the build that last changed each file, so phones fetch the new copy. Several chats push at once: `git fetch` and rebase on `origin/main` just before pushing, and take the next number after whatever is there.
- **SPEND AS LITTLE AS POSSIBLE (owner, build 106: "can you minimize spending - it's been pretty expensive for me last few days").** Video about 56 cents a try; a picture about 15 cents (`gemini-3-pro-image`); voice about a cent a line. **Before any paid call: say the cost and wait for a yes; keep the running total in `HANDOFF.md`.** Ways to save, in order: (1) do it in code first (breathing, sway, cloth and hair movement, small moves by warping a still, mirroring, tinting, reusing a figure); (2) build first and last frames from frames we already have; (3) draft pictures on `gemini-2.5-flash-image` (about a third of the price), the pro model only for the final; (4) show the owner a still before any video; (5) get two clips from one video (play backwards or back and forth); (6) write video prompts that pass the safety filter first time (no "sunk", "drowning", "thrashing", "desperate", "frightened"; say "thick sticky mud", "heaves", "cross and grumbling"); (7) a shorter video if the API offers one cheaper (not yet checked).
- **Keep `CLAUDE.md` and `HANDOFF.md` updated after every change.** The repo is public: no names, emails or other personal details in notes, code or commits.
- **Players:** kids 7 to 10, mostly on an iPhone. Gentle: no lives, no game over, read-aloud words, short simple sentences.
- **BEFORE AND AFTER FOR EVERY CHANGE (owner, build 259: "give me before and after shots to compare (this is a good idea for every change - maybe add this to notes)").** Before touching a picture, take stills of the moments it affects; after, take the same moments again; send the owner each pair, before above after, labelled (`tools/stills2.js` for Level 2, `tools/stills3.js` for Level 3, `DSF=2`).
- **The owner** looks closely and answers fast; show stills. "What do you think?" wants my opinion; "do it" wants action; "before you code" means only talk. I cannot hear sound or watch motion: say so; their ears and eyes decide.
- **Keep the files trim** (owner, build 26): never add dated or numbered copies; delete test pages once done (ask first).
- Sister project with the same habits: `Twhidbey/math-jump`.

## Art direction

**The look (owner, Nov 14 2026):** scenery drawn in code; **atmospheric layered silhouettes, not video-game looking**: each depth layer basically one colour, overlapping with haze, **pale far layers, darker near layers**; soft rain on the left clearing to warm golden light on the right; simple, natural, calm, **not cartoonish**. The chosen look: `look=50.60.65.29.4.80.30.66.41.19.55.35.0-0.1.1.3` (`LOOK` in the game). Background hills realistic (natural ridgelines). The weather runs across the whole level (rain in the city, gold at the end); **the end is not fully golden** (owner, build 48: "still a tad dreary, though i do want progression"): `GOLD_MAX` caps it per level; raise it level by level.

**ART DIRECTION FOR EVERY LEVEL (owner, build 135): beauty first; each level one stunning picture.** Owner: "Really want to stress the artistic beauty and stunning nature of these levels ... The burning hill can be frightening - but most importantly stunning - it should awe first". **Before building a level, choose its ONE great picture and the master it learns from, and say so in its notes; a frightening thing must awe before it frightens.** Keep the game's own rules (layers of one tone, a flat side view, few things, a far light). Proposed masters: **John Martin** (tiny people under vast rock and fire: the hill, Apollyon, the Valley of the Shadow); **Caspar David Friedrich** (a lone figure from behind before something vast: the Cross, Hill Difficulty, the Delectable Mountains); **Rembrandt** (one warm light in a great dark: the lit doorways, the wicket-gate, the Interpreter's House, Palace Beautiful); **Turner** (light dissolving into gold and mist: the far light, Beulah, the River, the City); **Ansel Adams** (silver tones, one great tree: Level 2); **Van Gogh** (wheat under a moving sky: Level 1's field, if wanted); **Japanese woodblock landscapes** (Hasui, Yoshida: the layered look, Vanity Fair at night); games: *Journey* (a far light as the one goal), *Inside*/*Limbo* (depth by fog and silhouette), *GRIS* (colour returns as the story heals). **AGREED for Level 3:** John Martin for the hill, Rembrandt for Evangelist raising Christian and for the wicket-gate; Friedrich kept for the Cross, Turner for the end of the game.

**People:** monochrome watercolour figures rotoscoped from AI videos ("that walk looks AMAZING, more like this for other characters"). **AI image tools cannot make walk cycles; AI video can.** Frames are grey (`g = .2 + .62 * lightness`) and tinted in the game for the weather (`drawFrame`). **Clothes timeless** (a post-apocalyptic version may come): worn, handmade, layered cloth; no lace, ruffs, buckles, neat buttons, zips, goggles or gadgets. **Faces plain** (no eyes or mouth, a soft wash; say so in every video prompt), **but Christian's side-on profile shows** (nose and chin in the hood; neither black nor blank, owner build 38). **Slow and atmospheric** (owner, build 21): every clip at its own video speed, never sped up. **Feet overlap the ground a little** (a gap looks wrong; overlap reads like perspective), and **people stand and walk only on level ground or a very gentle rise** (put a bank's height into a short face). **FLAT FEET (owner, build 156: "remember 2d flat - NOT one leg higher than other as one would have with perspective drawing ... this has haunted me in the past"):** in every standing or planted pose both soles rest on ONE line; the far foot is never drawn higher than the near one. Painted and video figures often come with the far foot 9 to 14 px high (Pliable's stand, Wiseman's stand, all of Evangelist's old frames). **Measure the soles of every new figure** (the bottom profile of the alpha, frame by frame); fix with `tools/flat-feet.py` (free; image models would not do it when asked), and say "a completely FLAT SIDE VIEW, like a shadow-puppet play ... both feet always land on that same line ... the far foot is never drawn higher" in video prompts, starting from a flattened first picture.

**Picture rules (each from the owner):**
- **Flat, not deep** (builds 40 to 43): a flat side view with a far horizon, like a long lens. Things near Christian sit on depth levels with one rule (`level(p)`: size = p, sliding speed = p, standing line = `HORIZON + 140 * p`); levels near him differ only a little. **Light on the ground is flat:** a low wide pool; never a shape streaming down the picture toward us.
- **Stripped down and elegant** (build 44: "if you have a few things it begs for more"): do not add props; when in doubt take one away. **One special thing in the picture at a time** (build 40). Road things are flat side views (no tops or side faces). Small things go on a further layer, sized for it (Christian must not dwarf them).
- **Three levels of detail** (build 36): people the full watercolour; his house about two thirds of its painting; road things a filled outline with a faint trace (`HOUSE_DETAIL`, `PROP_DETAIL`).
- **Don't cover the scene with words** (twice): the words are a thin strip of light text on the dark road under everyone's feet, no box.
- Sizes on one level must agree (the doorway 146 road units high; people in doorways at `DOOR_FIG` of the door). Trees are plain English kinds only (oak, ash, elm; "don't [do] exotic ones"), painted pictures, no two alike in one scene, softer with distance.
- **Mist goes behind or beside a figure standing in something dark, never between the two.** Anything that goes round a figure (ripples) is drawn in two halves, one behind him and one in front. To keep something out of a mirror, draw it after the mirror. **A surface is never half mirror:** a place is either mud (dull, mirrors nothing) or standing water (mirrors); "more reflection" means more water. Do not draw one thing sharp on top of mist that hides everything round it.
- **Look at close-up stills at twice the size before calling a scene done** (`deviceScaleFactor: 2`).
- **ATTENTION TO DETAIL AT EVERY SEAM (owner, build 318: "you can see roots of tree popping out below transition though (please make note about these attention to detail)").** Wherever one thing fades or blends into another (a tree's roots into the ground, a hill's foot into a plain, a figure into mist), crop the join and look at it enlarged (3 to 4 times) before showing the owner: (1) a fade must end fully clear (alpha to 1 in a cut-out, to 0 in a laid-on wash): a fade that stops at .85 or .9 leaves faint ghosts of what was under it; (2) a blend must never run along one straight level line: vary it along its length (some parts deeper, some higher); (3) an added patch (a swell of earth, a mist, a shadow) must not read as a smudge or a box at the size it is seen; start faint and add only if needed.

## Rules for play

- **No jumps, no obstacles, no score, no counter** (owner, Nov 14: "atmospheric story driven game ... like Riven and Myst ... though I don't want the wandering"). One way to go: walk, a story moment, walk. Hold to walk; a tap only touches.
- **Nothing moves on its own except the words** (owner, build 107). A finger walks him only if it went down after the last words ended (`G.calm`) and was held `HOLD` ticks; when words end, fingers and keys are forgotten. **Any level's start that resets the clock `G.t` must reset `G.calm`** (build 136: Level 2 could not be walked by touch). Still automatic, on purpose: Pliable following, Obstinate going home, Level 2's fall, walking back after "Go back with Pliable", Level 3's veil of mist to the gate.
- **Words stay with what they tell of** (build 107): while words are being said he cannot reach the next place where new words begin (stop points); give the road enough room that the stops hold him for a moment at most (build 110: "more space between things so timing works out"). Positions are fixed numbers on the road; nothing flexes.
- **No skipping by accident** (build 29): a line cannot be tapped away until its recording has finished; a line moves on by itself a moment after it is spoken (build 73), except lines that ask for an act. **Build 330 (owner: "yes take it out"):** the "tap ▸" label in the words' corner is gone (`dMore` stays empty for ordinary lines); a tap still moves a spoken line on sooner, and the corner still shows the gold prompts for acts ("touch the light ✦", "hold to run on ✦").
- **LESS WAITING (owner, build 161: "we need to cut down dialogue it's too much waiting"; "I like some old English but too much and it hurts").** DONE in build 164 from the owner's choices in `script.html` ("everything else as proposed", keeping lines 4, 21, 43, 52, 53): 27 lines cut (mostly the storyteller describing what the picture shows, and the middles of the Wiseman and Evangelist talks), 31 reworded shorter and plainer. **Rules from now on:** about 2 minutes of speech per level; never more than about 5 lines in a row before the child acts; the storyteller never describes what the picture already shows; at most one old word or turn of phrase per line, kept where it is famous ("Flee from the wrath to come", "Life! Life! Eternal life!", "Woe is me, for I am undone!", "Knock, and it shall be opened unto you", "God-speed", "Tush!", "How now, good fellow!"). **Build 175 (owner: "yes to walking lines and quicker hand-offs, keep script.html"):** (1) a spoken line now moves on half a second after it ends (`G.autoT` 30, was 75); (2) talks begin while people are still walking toward each other: Level 1's neighbours call out as they catch him up (`NEIGH_CALL`, captions; they wait a step behind until the words are done, then the talk `NEIGH` begins); their parting after the choice is said as Obstinate stomps home and Pliable follows (`part()` now captions `PART`); Level 3's greeting is said as Wiseman and Christian come toward each other (`GREET`; the talk at the crossing starts with "Who sent you this way?"). **Rule: when people meet or part, say the first and last lines while they walk; stop the child only for the middle of a talk and for its acts.** `script.html` stays (owner): it holds a plainer version of EVERY line (its only copy) for later rewording; its line numbers and "proposed" marks are from before build 164.
- **One text size** (builds 94, 98, 107, 136): every line the same size, from the picture's height only (`TXT_K`, never below `TXT_MIN` 16, at most `TXT_MAX`); a long line lifts the strip's top instead of shrinking. Owner: "too large is bad but too small is hard to read".
- **The hymn is discovered, never given away** (build 31): Amazing Grace is laid along the road as lights, one note per light (`PHRASES`, `BEAT`); the title only plays single soft notes. **Lights are not collected** (build 85: "too video gamey?"): each sings its note and drifts up into the far light. In Level 2 the tune is minor, low and bowed while he wades, and on the steps the lights ring soft tones of one rising chord, not the hymn (build 376: the hymn is saved, whole and in tune, for the Cross); in Level 3 it bends flat on the wrong path.
- Each new level: add its line to the title page's contents (the title no longer shows a level's picture, build 187).

## Lessons (bugs that happened)

- **Never put a `//` comment before other code on the same line** (builds 62, 73, 101: it swallowed the code after it; once every voice went silent, once the page would not start). Comments on their own line or at the true end of a line. **Run a syntax check** (`new Function(source)`) before every push.
- After touching sound, check that recordings really start: wrap `AudioBufferSourceNode.prototype.start` in the headless test and count calls with a buffer longer than half a second.
- Never draw from a canvas that changed this frame into another canvas; bake once and draw from the bakes; draw each layer only in its band; don't touch `imageSmoothingQuality` (build 6: 12 frames a second otherwise). Don't rely on `ctx.filter` (iPhone): paint small and stretch up.
- `VW` is a `let` (the camera draws back at Level 1's gate): never cache it for drawing; anything in `drawWorld` that fills the screen uses `TOPY`; every `setTransform` there carries `OYD`.
- Anything seen in the opening picture loads in the first batch, before "Open the book" (build 89).
- A walking video with a still camera must be checked for sliding feet (build 119: work the travel out again from the planted foot). Videos may zoom though told not to: measure and undo (build 59). A foot drawn too high can be fixed in the pictures for free (lengthen the leg, build 118); **measure both feet of any new standing picture and put the ground at the higher one**. Blank paper caught between figures can be cleaned out of a pack (build 79; `--holes` in `gesture-frames.py`).
- Test walking with real touches, not only keys (build 136).

## Phones

- **The page updates itself** (`freshCopy`): it fetches its newest copy with `cache: 'no-store'` and reloads once onto `?v=N` if the build is higher. Keep that reload inside the same page, or the Home Screen app shows a browser bar.
- **Home Screen app** (build 137): the manifest (`display` fullscreen, `start_url`/`scope` "./"), the icon and the iPhone tags. An icon keeps the settings it was made with: an old icon must be deleted and added again from the main link.
- **The title page is the book (build 187; owner: "when i open pilgrims progress it shows work ... in worldly wise level it flashes between old mountain and new ... better if the opening page was something neutral and didn't reveal the world ... logo of the app should also be something neutral like a book"):** no level's scene shows behind the title any more (`SL.title()`/`HL.title()` are no longer called at boot). On a dark ground with one warm glow stands the title page as first printed in 1678 (Nath. Ponder; the long title, "I have used Similitudes, Hos. 12. 10.", red-ruled frame, IM Fell type, laid paper made in code: `paperPages()`); **Since build 188 (owner: "shouldn't menu be cover of book? Before we open it") the title shows the closed book first**: a brown calf cover like the app icon (blind-tooled frames, a tooled lozenge with a small gold cross, a red ribbon below; made in code, `coverArt()`, `.tcover`, `.tribbon`); "Open the book" (or a touch on the cover) swings the cover open on its spine (`.tleaf`, rotated -180 degrees), and the title page, printed on the cover's inside face (`.tp`, its back face), lands on the left beside "THE CONTENTS." (`.tc`, `body.opened`), with the levels as contents lines (`#levels .lv`, the child's level marked with a pointing hand) and the book's first sentence (`{10}`) below. Choosing a level fades the book into the level. Sizes use `--u` (picture width / 960, set in `fit()`). **Each new level: add its line to the contents.** The app icon (`assets/icon-180.png`, `icon-512.png`, `?v=` on their links) is a closed calf-bound book with blind-tooled frames, a small gold cross in a tooled lozenge and a red ribbon, drawn in code (build 187; the script was in the session scratchpad; easy to redo with PIL).
- **A real leather cover (build 206; owner: "make cover of pilgrim book have a super realistic leather look like an old book (no need for cross, per se) ... find some images online (cheaper!!)"):** a photograph of a real 17th-century English calf binding from the British Library, on Wikimedia Commons under **CC0** (no attribution needed): "Of the consecration of the bishops in the Church of England - Upper cover (c67h3).jpg". Cropped to the leather, its first owner's gilt coat of arms taken out (filled with leather from lower on the same cover, tone-matched, soft-edged; the patch must not reach the gilt fillet at the bottom), the gilt fillet, wear, stains and the spine's raised bands kept: `assets/cover-calf.webp` (900 wide, about 100 KB), laid on `.tcover` once it has loaded (the drawn cover of build 188 stands in until then). The app icons (`icon-180/512`, `icon-edit-180/512`) were made again from the same photo (no cross). **To fetch from Commons the environment now allows `commons.wikimedia.org` and `upload.wikimedia.org`** (the owner approved); thumbnails come from `thumb.wikimedia.org`, which is NOT allowed: fetch originals, slowly (it answers 429 to quick runs). The British Library's "Upper cover (c..)" photographs on Commons are all CC0 and a good source for any old book or leather. **Build 228, quieter (owner: "Book cover is too loud - can we tone down slightly and make look more consistent with rest of game"):** the photograph's blotches and scratches calmed (its middle-size detail at .62, fine grain at .7 about a 60 px blur), a little less colour (.9), a soft darkening toward the edges and 5% darker overall: done once to `assets/cover-calf.webp` (`?v=228`; the original is in git before this build); the page block's edges a muted tan (`#ab9b7c`/`#9a8a6c`, was a bright cream) and the ribbon a deeper red. The app icons are unchanged.
- **The cover tooled as a binding of the 1670s (build 243; owner: "add some decor to cover of pilgrim progress book (main page) keep it in style of books of that time"):** an English calf "Cambridge panel" binding, drawn once in code over the photograph (`tooled(im)` in `coverArt()`): inside the photo's own gilt fillet a double blind fillet (lines pressed into the leather, no gold: a dark groove with a faint lit lower edge); a panel in a triple blind fillet, the leather outside it a shade darker (real ones were sprinkled or stained there); small gilt flower tools (a pointed leaf, two curls, dots; `spray()`) pointing out from the panel's corners and into the frame's corners; a centrepiece of eight such tools round a rosette in a lozenge of small dots. The gold is pressed in (a dark hollow round it), dulled and worn (rubbed away in specks and soft patches). No cross (owner, build 206). The app icons are unchanged. Nothing spent.
- **The ribbon of the closed book, woven silk (build 245; owner: "can we make bookmark more realistic"):** painted once in code, pixel by pixel (`ribbonArt()`, before `coverArt()`): fine lengthwise threads and a faint weft, a soft sheen that drifts across as it hangs with a slight twist (narrower and brighter there), darker edges, a shadow where it comes out from between the leaves, a swallow-tail cut with a few loose threads, a soft drop shadow. A little shorter than before (it touched the "Open the book" button). Deep red, quiet sheen, to sit with the toned-down cover. Nothing spent.
- **Build 276: THE COVER PAINTED IN THE GAME'S OWN LANGUAGE (owner: "book cover is too realistic - need to be more like story itself - i also noticed when i open link it flickers between old cover and new").** The photograph of the calf binding (builds 206, 228, 243) is no longer used: `coverArt()` paints the cover once in code, soft watercolour washes in a dull brown on rough paper grain, a hand-drawn double frame with one dull gold line, and in the middle an arched window of the game's layered hills (pale far, dark near, haze between) under the one far light, a little right. Nothing loads, so the cover never swaps (the flicker was the drawn stand-in giving way to the photograph). `assets/cover-calf.webp` is now unused (kept until the owner says to delete it); the app icons still show the leather photograph. The ribbon and page block are unchanged. Nothing spent. **Build 279 (owner: "edit the hills slightly to make sure the composition looks just perfect"):** the window's hills composed instead of four even wavy stripes: the far ranges bunched low under the light with a soft notch beneath it, a middle ridge falling from the left toward it, one near hill whose shoulder fills the left and sweeps down to the right, the darkest ground low across the foot rising a little at the right edge; the light a little lower (`hills` in `coverArt()`, each range a function of u across the window). Nothing spent. **Build 280 (owner: "i liked the old hills - maybe just the second from the front on the right could be slightly lower as it hits the third layer a bit"):** build 279 taken back; build 276's hills again, the second range from the front lowered toward the right (up to 22 of the cover's 896 rows at the window's right edge, eased from the middle), so it no longer touches the range behind. Nothing spent. **Build 357 (owner: "the logo and iphone screen have the old book cover - can you make sure it doesn't flicker and is consistent across all cases"):** (1) the app icons (`icon-180/512`, `icon-edit-180/512`, `?v=357` in `index.html`, `edit.html` and both manifests) are now the painted cover itself: a screenshot of the title page's closed book (cover, page block, ribbon) at three times the size on one even dark ground with a faint warm glow; the editor's icon keeps its round dark badge with the gold edge and two gold arrows. A Home Screen icon keeps the picture it was made with: delete it and add it again. (2) The book (`.tbook`) is hidden (opacity 0) until its painted cover has been made and decoded (`body.coverready`, set in `coverArt()`, at most 1.5 s), then fades in over .35 s, so the plain brown board never shows first. Nothing spent. **Build 358 (owner, after my two suggestions: "add the tiny pilgrim and clean up the board"):** (1) a tiny dark pilgrim (about 27 of the cover's 896 rows tall) stands on the nearest hill's ridge a third of the way across the window, his burden on his back, leaning a little forward, walking right toward the far light; drawn in code in one near-black tone, no face, his feet on the ridge (`ridge`, recorded as the last hill is drawn); (2) the board cleaner and warmer: base `#5d412d` (was an olive-leaning `#5a4434`), the washes fainter, the dark rings round the 40 larger washes gone (they read as cracks and dirt), the grain warmer and a little finer. The random numbers are drawn in the same order, so the hills are exactly as before. The app icons were made again from the new cover (`?v=358`). Nothing spent. **Build 359 (owner: "that pilgrim is unrealistic - can you use a more realistic shape"):** the drawn pilgrim (circles and a wedge) is replaced by Christian's own outline, traced from picture 14 of his book-free walk (`christian-walk-nb.pack`, mid-stride: hood, burden, robe, the far foot lifting), simplified to 60 points 100 units tall and kept in `coverArt()` (`P`, drawn at .3, about 30 cover rows tall), so the cover still needs nothing loaded. The icons were made again (`?v=359`). Nothing spent. **Build 360 (owner: "maybe make foreground hills much more gradual ... really stress the artistry and simplicity"):** the two nearest ranges are no longer short waves: the second from the front is one soft broad swell, its crown left of the middle, falling away toward the light; the nearest is low ground rising gently all the way across, which the pilgrim climbs (fixed curves in `coverArt()`, the random numbers still drawn in the same order); the nearest range's haze band now ends below its own lowest point (`hb`), so no flat pale line shows above it. The two far ranges are unchanged. Icons remade (`?v=360`). Nothing spent; an image-model composition was offered, not made. **Build 363 (owner: "can you color the pilgrim like the brown layer he's walking on?"):** the pilgrim is filled in the nearest range's own tone (`#2c231d`, was near-black `#17110d`), so he belongs to the land he walks on, his feet grow out of it, and he reads against the paler hazed range behind (the game's rule: each depth layer one tone). Icons remade (`?v=363`). Nothing spent. **Build 382 (owner: "can we remove the open book button and just click on cover to open"):** the "Open the book" button is gone; a touch on the cover opens the book (`openBook()`), once the first picture has loaded (`body.bookready`, set in `boot()`; before that "Getting ready…" shows and a touch does nothing). Under the book a quiet italic line, "Touch the book to open it" (`#sub`, at .62), hidden once it is open. The tests (`playtest.js`, `phone-touch-test.js`, `phone-test-3.js`, `stills2.js`, `stills3.js`) now wait for `body.bookready` and click `.tcover`. Nothing spent. **Build 385 (owner: "when you open book, let's not make it raining since that's level 1"):** no rain sound on the title or the contents (`ambience()`: the rain's gain goes to 0 while `G.state` is 'title'); the low hum and the far single notes stay; the rain comes in once Level 1 begins. Nothing spent.
- **The editor as its own Home Screen app (build 193; owner: "I want an editor link that I can put on my phone Home Screen and it updates with changes"):** the main manifest starts the app at "./", so an icon made from `?edit` used to open the plain game. Now a small script in `index.html`'s head, when the address has `?edit`, points the page at `edit.webmanifest` (start `./?edit`, its own `id`, name "PP Editor") and the editor icon (`assets/icon-edit-180.png`, `-512.png`: the book icon with a round dark badge and the two gold arrows). It updates itself like the game (`freshCopy` keeps `?edit`). **Since build 195 (owner: the skip buttons did not show on the iPhone) install from `edit.html`** (https://twhidbey.github.io/pilgrims-progress/edit.html): a small page whose head carries the editor's manifest and icon with no script, with "Open the editor"; if the phone opens it as the app it goes straight on to `./?edit`. The editor's label now shows on the book cover too ("editor on · the arrows show once a level begins"), so it is plain whether a link is in editor mode. The main manifest now has `"id": "./"` too.
- **Title and end cards** (build 144, owner: on an upright iPhone the title "too big and overlapping"): sized by the picture, not the screen. `fit()` sets `--s` on `#stage` (picture width / 960, times 1.4 upright and 1.12 sideways, at most 1.25); `.card h1`, `h2`, `p`, `.btn` and the build stamp use `calc(var(--s) * Npx)`. "Turn your phone sideways" sits 22 px below the picture's real bottom edge.
- **Upright check (build 178):** every screen of all three levels (words, choices, end cards, title) was checked on an emulated iPhone 13 held upright and sideways for text outside the picture or overlapping: none. The tray of things he carries is small held upright; its touch area is wider than its drawing.
- **First load (build 178):** before "Open the book" only his walk, his standing, the opening's pictures and the scripts load (about 1.5 MB); the weeping (2nd opening line, about 0.75 MB) loads after, then his turn. If the weeping is not ready by its line he simply stands.
- **Voices with the ringer on silent** (`mediaSound`: `navigator.audioSession.type = 'playback'` and a silent looping `<audio>`); every touch restarts sound iOS has left suspended (build 100). Not yet confirmed on the phone.
- **AUTO-WALK (build 309; owner: "can you add an automatically walk toggle (to all levels)"):** a quiet line on the contents page under the darker rooms, "Let him walk on by himself" (◇ off, ◆ on), and the same switch in the pause menu ("He walks on by himself: on/off"); kept on the device (`pp-auto` in `localStorage`; `AUTOW`, `setAuto()`, `KS.autoLabel()`). With it on, `autoHeld()` counts as a held finger 40 ticks after the words end (`G.calm`): `held()` (walking in Levels 1 to 3, Level 1's walk-on), `pushHeld()` (Level 1's gate) and the house's `hold()` (`round`, `walkout`, `follow`). Touch acts (the light, the hill, Help's hand, the knocks, the candle...) and choices stay the child's. Off by default: the owner's rule "nothing moves on its own" holds unless the line is marked. Checked: Levels 1, 2 and 3 reach their end cards with no input; room 4's round and leaving's walk-out run by themselves. **Build 322 (owner: "let's take it off book page and make it separate button next to pause button"):** the contents page no longer carries the line; the switch is a round button with two chevrons (`#autoWalk`, in `index.html`, beside `#snd`) just left of the pause button, shown while playing: dim when off, gold when on; it still sets `pp-auto` through `setAuto()`, and the pause menu keeps its line too. Nothing spent.
- **HELD UPRIGHT, THE WHOLE SCREEN USED (build 422; owner: "on profile on phone it'd be nice to use more screen", then "1 and 2" of my three points; the third, a taller picture, was advised against: every scene is composed wide).** In `fit()`, upright (`body.portrait`): the picture keeps its full width and sits about a quarter of the way down the spare height (`#wrap` padding), and `--below` on `#stage` is the height under it. (1) **The book**: `#title` is fixed to the whole screen with its own `--u` (the cover about 90% of the width or 72% of the height); once open the book stays shifted (`translateX(-160u)`), so the contents page fills the screen where the cover was and only the edge of the title page shows on the left; the contents lines are large enough for a child's finger; the build stamp sits at the screen's foot (the editor's five taps there, `editor.js`). (2) **In a level**: the words (`#dlg`) and the choices (`#choice`, stacked, words hidden while a choice is up) lie in the dark space below the picture, not over it, at a size from the screen's width (`fitWords`: 14 to 19 px); the sound, pause and auto-walk buttons sit in a row at the foot of the screen; the sideways hint is small above them (not on the book). Sideways is unchanged (checked: the same stills, and the playtest). Nothing spent. **Build 445 (owner: "How do we see title page or is it best gone?", then yes to keeping it):** upright, as the cover swings open the book rests first on the title page (`body.tpview`, the book shifted `translateX(+160u)`), filling the screen, for about 3.6 s, then glides to the contents; a touch on the title page (or a swipe left) goes to the contents, a touch on the title page's edge left of the contents (or a swipe right) brings it back (`tpShow(on)`, `tpT`; a level's start clears it). Sideways unchanged. Nothing spent.
- **Text size:** iPhone Safari enlarges text in blocks it judges to be reading text (worse held sideways). `text-size-adjust: 100%` on `html, body`, and `fitWords` measures the drawn words and brings the size down (`fitC`). **Held upright** (build 138, owner: "when phone is in portrait mode can you make text smaller by default so it fits") the picture is only about 220 px high, so the size is `TXT_K_UP` (.052) of it, between `TXT_MIN_UP` (11) and 16; sideways is unchanged. Not provable off a real iPhone: if a line is still too big, ask which line, whether the phone was sideways, and whether Display Zoom or Larger Text is on.

## Making pictures, videos and voices (costs money: ask first)

- **Key:** the owner's, saved in the `pilgrims-progress` cloud environment as `GEMINI_API_KEY` (never print it; never in the repo). That environment allows `generativelanguage.googleapis.com` and `twhidbey.github.io`.
- **Pictures:** `gemini-3-pro-image` via `generateContent` with `responseModalities: ['TEXT', 'IMAGE']`, reference pictures as `inlineData` parts before the text; **a game frame as the style reference matches the watercolour people closely.** Give proportions in door-widths and an aspect ratio (`generationConfig.imageConfig.aspectRatio`). For a back view say "NOT a rucksack: no pockets, no flaps, no buckles, no bedroll". `python3 tools/cutout.py picture.png HEIGHT assets/name.webp` cuts a white-background figure and prints its META.
- **EVERY VIDEO MUST EARN ITS KEEP ACROSS THE WHOLE GAME (owner, build 197: "every video we should be thinking about usefulness down the road (or back to make other levels more beautiful) - we want videos that are super useful").** Before proposing a video, list every level (past and planned, see the 17-level table) where it could be used, and design it for the widest use: a plain white ground, one figure, a flat side view, hands empty, nothing tied to one scene in the picture; a movement that has a clear start and a settled end that can be HELD (a breathing still at the end), so it can be played forwards, backwards (kneel = rise) and looped in its held part; start from a frame we already have, so it joins our existing standing or walking pictures. Say in the offer to the owner where else it will serve. Example: the kneel (build 197) serves Evangelist's feet (Level 3), the Cross (Level 5), and any prayer later; played backwards it is his rising.
- **Video:** `gemini-omni-1.2-flash` through the Interactions API (`POST /v1beta/interactions`, `{model, input: [{type: 'image', data, mime_type}, {type: 'text', text}], response_modalities: ['video']}`; MP4 as base64 in `steps[].content[].data`), about 8 s. **Give a first and a last picture** (two image parts; the text says which is which) for any move with a fixed ending. Put the figure small in a 1280 x 720 white picture. Two people in one video works if the scene is drawn as flat blocks. Then: `tools/video-frames.py video.mp4 out.pack --height H --match assets/christian-walk.pack` (walks: picks the loop, prints the META) or `tools/gesture-frames.py` (one-off movements; takes a video or a folder of frames; `--frames a-b:step`). Check a walk: camera steady, planted foot slides back at a steady speed (`vpx`), stride rises and falls evenly.
- **Voices:** `GEMINI_API_KEY=... python3 tools/make-voice.py` reads every `[N/C/E/O/PL/W/H, '...']` line from `index.html` and `slough.js`, records only missing ones with Gemini TTS in the designed voices (descriptions in `HANDOFF.md`, IDs in the tool), checks each by transcription, deletes recordings of lines that no longer exist. **Change a line's words and you must run it again**, or it goes silent. `--redo C|N|...|all|styled`. **Direct the speech model with tags in square brackets** (`STYLE` in the tool: "[alarmed, breathless] Ah, neighbor ..."); a direction written as a sentence is read aloud. A voice is designed with `POST /v1beta/voices`, `store: true`. Bump `VOICE_V` when recordings change. It reads `hill.js` too (build 143; `WW` = Mr. Worldly Wiseman), `rooms.js` and `cross.js` (build 394; `SO`/`S1`/`S3` = the Shining Ones). The storyteller's voice reads bracketed tags aloud: style only characters' lines. **Google allows 100 requests per model per day** (each line is two: speaking and the check): a long batch stops partway with a 429 "per_day"; run it again the next day and it carries on with what is missing.

## Testing (before every push)

```
python3 -m http.server 8123 &          # in the repo; it dies with the session
node tools/playtest.js 1               # also 1back, 2, 2back, 3 ("back" = going home / going back); a folder as 2nd argument saves stills at twice the size
node tools/phone-touch-test.js         # Level 2 walked by real touch with a stale clock; text sizes
node tools/phone-test-3.js             # Level 3 with real touches only (also: hill | evangelist | gate)
node tools/stills4.js OUT ROOM "COND" [N] [drawn]   # Level 4 stills at any moment (s = RM.peek()); follows on through rooms (build 423)
```

Plus a syntax check of `index.html`'s script, `slough.js` and `hill.js` (`new Function`). `playtest.js` fails on any page error or if the end card is not reached. By hand in a page: wait for `body.bookready`, click `.tcover`, wait 3 s, click `.lv[data-lv="N"]`; then loop `update()` with `keys.r = true` to hold, `next(true)` to pass a line, `pick(false)` at a choice, `lightTouched()`, `keys.push`, `SL.grip()`, `HL.key()` for acts; `SL.peek()`, `HL.peek()` tell the phase; call `draw()`/`SL.draw()`/`HL.draw()` before a screenshot.

**The editor's link (build 184, owner: "a skip ahead button ... link for editor ... jump back and forward ... preview quickly"): add `?edit`** (e.g. `/?edit`, `/?edit&level=2`). Arrows on the picture's left and right edges (or the `[` and `]` keys): ▸ fast-forwards silently to the next story moment (a new line, a choice or the end card), doing what the play-through test does (holds to walk, passes lines, does every act, picks the way on at a choice); ◂ begins the level again and comes forward to the moment before (about 2 s from late in a level). Since build 190 the arrows are round dark buttons with a gold edge at mid-height (owner could not see the old pale edge arrows on Level 3's bright sky). A label at the top shows the level and moment. All in `editor.js` (object `ED`); without `?edit` it does nothing. Moments after a choice follow "go on", never "go back". **Editor mode remembered on the device (build 196; owner, on an iPhone with DuckDuckGo: the Home Screen icon opened without `?edit`, so no arrows):** five quick taps on the bottom-right corner of the book cover (where the build number is) turn editor mode on or off for that browser or icon (`localStorage` `pp-edit`; each Home Screen icon keeps its own store); `?edit` turns it on, `?edit=0` off. The cover's label reads "editor on · tap build no. 5× to turn off".

**Skip-ahead links:** Level 1 `?at=door|neighbours|obstinate|gate` (and `?x=N`); `?level=2`, `?level=2&at=bog`; `?level=3`, `?level=3&at=hill|evangelist|gate`. `?lines` shows Level 1's depth guides. **Since build 467 (owner: "when you give link to location, why does it still take me to home screen - can we jump to game instead?"): a link naming a place (`level=`, `at=`, `room=`) shows "Touch to begin" on the cover, and one touch anywhere opens the book and starts that level at once (in `boot()` in `index.html`; `level=` decides the level, so `?level=3&at=gate` is Level 3's gate, not Level 1's). The one touch stays: phones allow sound only after a touch. Automated tests (`navigator.webdriver`) still open the book and click the level themselves.**

## The engine and Level 1, City of Destruction (`index.html`)

**How Level 1 goes (the book's order, `{10}` to `{24}`; "out, then back", owner build 50: "i really like that out then back"):** the opening picture (`{10}`): rain, he stands turned from his own house, his wife in the lit door; he reads, weeps and trembles (`WEEP`) → hold to walk **left**, out into the wheat field (the first phrases laid right to left) → Evangelist waits in the field, breathing; the talk; the roll drifts into the tray; "Do you see that shining light?": he turns round (his turn video) and only then can the child touch the light → "the man began to run" (`RUNS`), then the child can run → running back **right** past his own house: the door flies open, his wife cries "Come back! Come home to us!", the child chooses "Stop and look back" (the framed picture in the lit door, his back to us, his answer, her second call, hold to run on) or "Run on"; then fingers in his ears ("Life! Life! Eternal life!", the world muffled) → the neighbours in their lit doorways mock him (three video loops) → Obstinate and Pliable in their own doorways; he tires to a walk (`RUN_END`); they overtake him; the choice "Go home" (a few dark steps, he turns back by himself) or "Go on to the light" → Obstinate goes home, Pliable comes → the city wall and gate: the camera draws far back (`ZOOM_GATE`), the child holds the gate to push it open, the tune plays whole, they walk through → end card, "Go on" to Level 2.

**Settled picture of Level 1:** a pale wheat field ("think simplicity and elegant effects": three layers, wind as bands of light, the crop thins into the mist at the field's end); a low 16th-century town (one or two floors, few windows, varied towers and spires); his cottage in a row of painted cottages (only lit doors open: his, the mockers', Obstinate's, Pliable's), each house with its own window style (`WIN_STYLES`, owner build 98); the wide glow at his house comes from its lit windows on the wet road; doorway shadows follow one light at the door's foot; nothing on the road but two tall lamps (lamplight pools radius 300); rain rings where he steps; a realistic city gate (about 10 m tower, 4 m wall, worn coping; the gate is a city gate, not a door), the far light rising above it; far away beyond the town, Level 2's oak in a lowland mist (`drawFarSlough`, using `SL.oakPic`: keep that export).

**Settled small points (each the owner's):** he starts turning round on "Do you see that wicket gate?" (his turn video at 6 pictures a second, about 5 s; build 180), and on "Do you see that shining light?" lifts his head a little toward it (`P.lookUp`/`P.lookA`: the standing picture's head, above y 55 and right of x 86, tilted back up to .1 radian about the neck at (112, 55) in `drawFrame`) until he walks on; after "Run on" at his door he runs on by himself to the lamp just past his house (`G.autoRun`, build 180: the owner's own exception to "nothing moves on its own"), then stops until the child holds; Evangelist faces Christian while they talk, only breathes while waiting (no hand movement), and never disappears (he stays pointing); Christian holds still in a talk until he is in his quiet breathing, then breathes on; at the opening only his wife stands in the door, her hand on the frame; the whole family when he runs back; going into the run there is no thick double picture (`poseChange(n, quick)`); no foreground grass and no leafy boughs (pixelated, blocky); no lit glow in the wheat; Pliable's voice is boyish.

**Code map (search for these names):**

| Area | Names |
|---|---|
| Screen, camera, zoom | `VW`, `VH`, `GY` 440, `LEVEL_W`, `fit()`, `draw()` wraps `drawWorld()`; `G.zoom`, `ZCAM`, `OYD`, `TOPY`, `scenePt` |
| Scenery (baked once) | `buildScenery()`, `SCN`, `bake`, `weatherSegs`/`SEG`/`strip`/`pair`/`fade`, `warm`, `clearAt`, `hGrad`, `GOLD_MAX`, `layerCol`, `mkNoise`, `house`, `bakeFinish`; high skies `hiRain`/`hiGold`/`feather` |
| Depth levels | `LEVELS`, `level(p)`, `lineUp`, `drawLevel`, `HORIZON`, `drawGuides` |
| Field | `WHEAT`, `buildWheat`, `drawWheat`, `wheatPlane`, `FIELD_END`, `FIELD_TREES`, `drawTrees`, `TREE_META`, `bakeTree` |
| His house and the rows | `HOME_X`, `home()`, `doorX`, `HOUSE_META`, `ROW`, `buildRow`, `drawRow`, `NB_META`, `room()`, `WIN_STYLES`, `sills`, `doorFloor`, `DOOR_FIG`, `HOUSE_K`, `mkShadow`, `FAM_SH`, `WIFE_SH`, `WIFE` |
| Gate and wall | `GATE`, `GATE_STOP` (below `DOOR0 - 44`), `DOOR0`, `DOOR1`, `gatePainted`, `GATE_META`, `WALL_META`, `wallTop`, `doors`, `intoGate`, `figure`, `ZOOM_GATE`, `lightPos`, `G.sunUp` |
| Props and lamps | `THINGS` (empty), `PROP_META`, `bakeTint`, `LAMPS`, `lampLight`, `LAMP_Y` |
| Frames | `loadPack(name, into)` (a `.pack` = count, lengths, WebP frames end to end), `PACK_V` (bump when packs change), `drawFrame(im, meta, x, y, face, alpha, sy)`, `stepAnim`, `breath(k)` (stills breathe in code), `talkMove`/`voiceEnv`/`SPK`/`TALKM` (a speaker moves a hair with their voice) |
| Christian | `CHR` walk (38 frames, `CHR_META`, `CHR_K`, `CHR_STEP`, `MAXV` 1.05), stopping and standing `STD` (`QUIET` breathing back and forth, `STAND_FROM_1`; he never stops mid-stride: walk frame 28 or 1 joins the stand), `RUNP` plain run, `RUN` ears run (`G.ears`, `coverEars`, `MUFF`/`CLEAR`), `TURN` (drawn unmirrored), `WEEP`, `BACK`, `poseChange`, `drawChristian`, `walkTick`, `held()`, `keys` |
| Others | `EVA`/`EVS` Evangelist (`G.evan`), `OBS`/`OBS_ST`, `PLI`/`PLI_ST`, `WATCH_PACK` (mockers), `people()`, `walker()` |
| Story | `INTRO`, `FIELDS`, `EVAN`, `RUNS`, `DOOR_WORDS`, `FAMILY`, `LIFE`, `WATCH`, `FETCH`, `NEIGH`, `HOME`, `ONWARD`, `PART`, `GATEWORDS`, `END`, `LEVEL2_OPENING` (kept here for the voice tool); `story()`, `acts()`, `choose()`/`pick()` (`G.choiceKind`), `lightTouched`, `turnToLight`, `G.runZone`, `RUN_END`, `DOOR_SAY`, `DOOR_STOP`, `WATCH_X`, `NEIGH_X`, `AT` |
| Words engine | `talk(lines, then)` (a line is `[who, text, act, fn]`; acts `'light'`, `'gate'`, `'walkon'`, Level 2 `'hand'`, Level 3 `'hill'`, `'knock'`), `caption`, `hint`/`clearHint`, `show`, `next`, `words`, `fitWords`, `TIP`, `G.guard`, `HEARD`, `G.autoT`, `saying()`, `G.capEnd`, `narrStop`, `G.calm`, `HOLD` |
| Voices | `say`, `hush`, `voiceName(who, txt)` (file name in `assets/voice/`), `fetchVoices`, `VOICE_V` |
| Touch | `touchAt` (far light, lamps, birds, the wet road, bells, windows, a roof: chimney smoke `drawSmoke`/`G.smoke`, chimney tops `ch` in `HOUSE_META`/`NB_META`, build 178; birds in the field's trees), `free`, `glints`, the roll in the tray (`drawItems`, `G.roll`) |
| Sound (made in the browser) | `audio()`, `RAIN_VOL`, `note`, `sfx` (steps with the burden's creak, mud, bell, gate...), `hiss`, `sweep`, `opening()` |
| Title, levels, loop | `#title`, `#open`, `#levels`, `LV`, `store` (`pp-level` in `localStorage`), `start()`, `go2()`, `go3()`, `finish()`, `boot()`, `loop()` (60 ticks a second on any screen), `#again`, `#on2` |

## Keepsakes and the pause menu (`keepsakes.js`, object `KS`, build 176)

Owner: "is there a way to get pause and get menu that allows us to navigate away and to the different levels ... if items in upper right corner could be clickable and you can see them up close. a) ... an open bible to a favorite passage (maybe romans 3 in old KJV with sin passages underlined in fountain pen (by christian of course)) and you can flip left and right a couple pages. b) the scroll can just be a piece of paper with a cool seal and the words from flee from the wrath to come written out in cool text".
- **Pause:** a two-bar button beside the sound button (`#pause`), or Escape: "Resting a while", "Go on", or begin any level from its start (the current one marked "here"). The world stands still (`KS.paused`, checked in `loop()`), the sound drops to a whisper; leaving cleans up the words, the choice and the end card, then calls `start()`/`go2()`/`go3()`.
- **His book** (a touch on the book in the tray, top left; `KS.book()`): a Bible open at Romans 3 in the King James text (1769 wording, public domain; `SPREADS`, two spreads: 3:1-18 and 3:19-4:3). **Made like an English Bible of Bunyan's day (build 179; owner: "the book should be like what the[y] had in time of Pilgrims progress")**: dealers' and libraries' descriptions of 1660s-70s copies agree on an octavo or duodecimo, the text in two columns of roman type, ruled in red throughout, bound in brown or black calf. So: two columns with a red rule between (balanced), a double red ruled frame, the long s inside words (`longS`), running heads ("Chap.iij." and "To the Romans."), a catchword at the foot of each page and a signature mark ("Ee 3") on the right-hand pages, a red drop capital, IM Fell English (a type of the 1670s); laid paper with chain lines, cloudy pulp and foxing (`texture('paper')`), a calf binding with blind-tooled lines (`texture('calf')`), red-sprinkled page edges, a red ribbon. **Christian's marks:** the sin passages underlined in iron-gall ink (`<u>`: 3:9, 10, 12, 19, 20, 23), a pen stroke beside 3:23, and beside 3:10 in his hand "What shall I do?" (his own cry, book `{10}`; placed by `marks()` after layout). Pages turn with a 3D leaf (`turn`): the arrows, a swipe, a tap on either page, the arrow keys. One type size for all four pages, the largest that fits (`fit`). **The 1611 chapter summary was left out:** the sites with the 1611 text could not be reached from here to check it word for word. **Departure:** the book never says what he read; Romans 3 is the owner's choice.
- **The roll** (a touch on the roll once he has it; `KS.roll()`): rough parchment (`texture('parch')`, mottled, with faint creases from rolling; a torn edge, `deckle()`) unrolling between its own curled ends: "Flee from the wrath to come." in an engrossing hand (the blackletter of documents; UnifrakturMaguntia), the F in red; a tapered pen rule; "Matt. iii. 7." (Bunyan's own margin reference, `{14}`). **The wax seal is lit like real wax** (`waxSeal()`, owner: "super cool design and realistic wax"): a height map (an uneven puddle with a rounded edge, the matrix's sunken field with a raised lip of pushed-up wax, the device raised in it: the King's crown with a cross above, a ring of beads, the legend SIGILLVM REGIS, "the King's seal", Latin as on seals of the time), softened by floating-point blurs (8-bit blurs left terraces), each pixel lit by one light from the upper left with a sharp gloss and warmer thin edges; deep crimson; two silk ribbon tails under it. Made once, the first time the roll opens.
- **More of his Bible (build 201; owner: "book (bible) can have more pages (more in romans) or another judgment passage (flipping several pages animation to next passage). also more foot/margin notes with more convincing handwriting -script font from that era"):** five spreads now: Romans 3:1-18, 3:19-4:3, 4:4-13, 4:14-25, then a RIFFLE of eight leaves (`riffle()`, `jump` on the spread: copies of the turning leaf, each a little behind the last, their running heads showing the books passing, To the Corinthians ... To Philemon, their text soft and unmarked) to Hebrews 9:18-24 and 9:25-10:3. **Hebrews 9:27 is Bunyan's own margin reference** for "after that to come to judgement" (`{14}`). The new verses were checked word for word against two published KJV texts (the 1769 text in the npm package `es-kjv`, and `bible-kjv`): identical but for Romans 4:18 "nations," (1769) / "nations;" and 4:19 "Sara's" (1769) / "Sarah's"; the 1769 forms are used. Every page fits at the old type size (the Hebrews pages were cut to fit: the size is the largest at which every page fits). **His notes are in Herr Von Muellerhoff**, a quick quill italic (compared with La Belle Aurore, Italianno, Tangerine, Pinyon Script and six others), in brown iron-gall ink: "What shall I do?" (3:10), "even me?" (4:5, "him that justifieth the ungodly" underlined), a stroke by 4:7, "if I believe" (4:24), "no remission" (Heb. 9:22), "I fear it." with a stroke (9:27, underlined whole), "O that it were so" (9:28, "unto salvation"). The notes are ours (a departure: the book never shows his Bible). Handwriting fonts have no long s: write notes with a plain s.
- **The Bible opens at Hebrews (build 208; owner: "Bookmark looks funky - should come out of pages not binding - by the way can we start with hebrews since that was bunyans [choice] (then viewer can flip back to Roman's and also maybe a forward to choice Revelations passage)"):** it opens where the ribbon lies, Hebrews 9 (`MARKED`); back riffles to Romans, forward riffles (James ... Jude) to **Revelation 21:1-7** (Bunyan's own margin gives Rev. 21:4 at `{27}`; checked against the same two KJV texts: identical), with notes "no more crying" and a stroke (21:4, "God shall wipe away all tears from their eyes"), "freely!" (21:6), "O that I may" (21:7). **The ribbon** is sewn in at the head of the spine, lies nearly straight down the inner margin of the marked spread (clear of the text) and hangs out of the foot of the pages with a notched end; at other spreads only its tail shows below the pages on the side where Hebrews lies (`tailR`/`tailL`, set in `render()`). **Build 210** (owner: "the 'dotted' border of page should be covered by shadow of middle crease and also should be below red book mark - on outside bookmark should clearly emerge from pages not cover"): the crease shadow (`.spine`) and the ribbon now reach over the sprinkled page edges (`.edges`) at the head and foot; on the title's closed book the page block is its own layer under the board (`.tpages`, cream with the fine lines of the leaves), and the ribbon (`.tribbon`) lies between the two, so it comes out from between the pages below the board. **Build 211** (owner: "When you flip pages ribbon should clearly come out of stack of pages region not out of page itself"): on the other spreads the ribbon's end starts in the sprinkled edge of the stack of leaves (below the page, not on it) and hangs over the board, with a deeper notch.
- **The roll since build 199** (owner: "when scroll unrolls we need the roll to start at top and get smaller as it goes down - black between roll and paper needs to go. also, font should look more like ancient text from that era and paper texture should look really realistic"): the lower roller starts beside the top one, fat with the rolled sheet, and travels down thinning as the sheet comes off; the sheet shows only down to the middle of that roller (`unroll()`, one clock in JS for both, in pixels; CSS animations of the two drifted apart); the sheet's top and bottom edges are straight and run in under the rollers, torn only at the sides (`deckle(..., sidesOnly)`), and the rollers have no torn outline (the dark gaps were the background showing through torn edges). The words are in **UnifrakturCook**, an English black letter like the 1611 Bible's (compared with UnifrakturMaguntia, German Fraktur; Texturina, too modern; Grenze Gotisch; Pirata One), with a slight ink bleed. The parchment (`texture('parch')`) is made of cloudiness in several sizes, a fine grain, uneven toning toward the edges, about 2600 fine fibres, three faint water stains with tide-lines and a few dark specks (thin wandering veins were tried and looked like a map's contour lines).
- **Fonts** come from Google Fonts (`<link>` in `index.html`'s head): IM Fell English and its small capitals, UnifrakturCook (build 199; was UnifrakturMaguntia), La Belle Aurore (his hand); fetched early (`document.fonts.load`); Georgia stands in if they cannot load. Textures are made in a canvas the first time each is opened (a moment's work).
- **Motion checked, build 240 (small-polish chat; owner asked for the riffles and the unrolling to look smooth).** Filmed frame by frame in Playwright with the page's clocks slowed twenty times (performance.now, rAF, setTimeout and the CSS animations; the script is in that session's scratchpad, not the repo). Found and fixed: (1) **Christian's notes popped in after a page landed** (the back of a turning leaf was laid out without them, since `marks()` measures a flat page): the back is now laid out flat on a hidden page (`.page.scratch`, `putBack()`) and copied, so the page lands with its notes on it; (2) **the ribbon showed on the wrong page during a riffle** (it stayed lying down the middle while the leaves passed, then jumped to a tail): it fades out at the start of a turn and back at the end, only when it has to move (`ribbonTo()`); (3) **the turns ran on CSS transitions started by timers**, which drift apart on a busy phone, and in a riffle a leaf just lifting could drop behind the next leaf as that one started: now ONE clock (`flip()`, performance.now, an eased curve `bez()`) turns every leaf frame by frame; a leaf lifting lies above those waiting, once past upright above those landed; (4) the crease shadow (`.spine`) and the ribbon now lie above the turning leaves (they used to vanish under a leaf and come back); (5) a lifting leaf darkens toward the spine with its angle (`--sh`, `.face::after`); (6) **the roll unrolled while it was still fading in** (the street showed through it): it now waits closed for .38 s, then unrolls. Measured after: no frame-to-frame jumps in a single turn, the riffles or the roll. Nothing spent. **The owner's eyes on the iPhone decide.**
- **No seal on Evangelist's roll (build 242; owner: "Is seal appropriate here?", then "Let's do it").** In the book Evangelist's roll is plain: "a parchment roll, and there was written within, Flee from the wrath to come" (`{14}`). The sealed roll comes at the Cross: the third Shining One "gave him a roll with a seal upon it", to give in at the Celestial Gate (`{94}`; Christian later calls it "a roll, sealed"). So the roll now shows only the words, the pen rule and "Matt. iii. 7.", moved down to sit a little above the sheet's middle. `waxSeal()` and the ribbon tails (`TAILS`) stay in `keepsakes.js` (and `KS.waxSeal` exported) for the Cross's sealed roll in Level 5. Nothing spent.
- **His Bible's ribbon in woven silk (build 247; owner: "Ok let's match ribbon"):** to match the closed book's (build 245), a strip painted once in code (`silk()`, after the overlays are made) and repeated down the ribbon: fine lengthwise threads, a faint weft, darker edges, a soft sheen that drifts from side to side along its length. The ribbon's shapes (lying down the marked spread, the tails below the pages, the notched ends) are unchanged. Nothing spent.
- Not yet: a glint to show the tray can be touched. Any new Bible text must be checked word for word against two published KJV texts (the npm packages `es-kjv` and `bible-kjv` both carry it).

## The mannequin (`mannequin.js`, object `MQ`): a lab, NOT YET IN THE GAME (characters chat, after build 364)

Owner: "carefully build a mannequin of pilgrim which we can draw using graphics not images ... you can use up to $5 to get animations of motion to use for mapping mannequin ... useful for future animations of both pilgrim and potentially other figures"; then "work from skeleton and then build on clothes. Maybe use famous studies of motion from animation studios"; "we should NOT be stretching and shrinking pictures but drawing using vector graphics"; and, stopping a version traced from his painting: **"it seems we are trying to reproduce the exact figure (even down to pixels that may or may not have been clipped right) - we should work more from first principles about how clothes move and skeleton walking natural - this may need some more research of 2d animation principles online".** **Spent so far: nothing.** The game is unchanged (no build number). Lab page: `mannequin.html` (https://twhidbey.github.io/pilgrims-progress/mannequin.html): the bare mannequin and its bones, the clothed figure, his old picture frames beside them only to compare the feel, slow motion, single frames, and the figure walking at the game's size.
- **RULE: nothing is traced or copied from his painted frames, and nothing is stretched. Two exceptions, on the owner's word, because they are DESIGN and not mechanics (owner: "face profile should look more like original (that's just personal design not biomechanical one, right?)"; "look up profiles of hood and trace them"): the hood's outline and the quiet profile of his face.** The figure is DESIGNED: simple shapes built on the joints each frame. His frames are used only for measures (his height, the length of his step, 19 frames a step) and to compare the feel. (Tried first and dropped: his painting cut into parts and traced into tone shapes, the skirt's traced folds warped or cut into bands; it reproduced his frames to .96 of their outline but carried their cutting faults and was a copy, not a design.)
- **1. The skeleton** (`K`, `joints()`, `ik()`, `pose()`), set up as cut-out animators set up a figure (one root at the hip, each part a child of the one before it, each turning about the middle of its joint, far limbs drawn before near ones): hip, spine, head, two legs (thigh 66, shin 67, the ankle 13 above the sole, the foot bending at its ball), two arms (upper 50, fore 40) with hands (palm 13, fingers 10.5, bending at the wrist and the knuckles), the burden. **The shoulder line and the hip line** (owner: "a line from shoulder to shoulder and how shoulders move when one walks"): each runs across the body, away from us (`K.shw`, `K.hpw`: half widths); it can turn (seen from above) and tip (seen from in front), and each arm and leg hangs from its own end (`J.shN`/`shF`, `J.hipN`/`hipF`, `lineEnd()`). A skeleton pose is 27 numbers (listed at the top of `mannequin.js`; the last two are each foot's arch). `MQ.bones()` draws the bones (the two lines in green); `MQ.above()` draws the two lines as seen from above.
- **2. The body** (`BODY`, `body()`; owner: "use realistic profile for clothing and body shape ... look up anatomy studies, like from art schools and the mannequins they use to get proportions right"): by the art schools' canon of heads (Loomis and others: an ordinary man about seven and a half heads tall, the shoulders a head and a half down, the hips at the middle, the knees two heads above the ground; his bones already stood there). Each part has a side PROFILE: along the part, how far its front and its back stand from the bone (`BODY.thigh`, `shin` with its calf and thin ankle, `upper`, `fore`, `torso` with chest, small of the back and buttock), and the head and foot are drawn outlines (`skull`: brow, nose, lips, chin; `foot`). **Hands and feet (owner: "add arches to his feet", "realistic hands and balls of feet"):** the foot is by the anatomists' measures (15% of his height long, a quarter of it behind the ankle, the ankle joint about 4% of his height up: it was 7%, which made boots): the heel's round pad, the arch (higher on the far foot, which shows its inner side: a 4th number on those points), the ball, the crease behind the toes, the toe pad, the toes' knuckles, the instep; the hand (`hand()`) is the back of a relaxed hand as it shows when the arm hangs: three quarters of a head long when open, shorter curled, the thumb lying along the first finger, four fingertips of unequal length. The art schools' arm: the elbow at the waist, the wrist at the fork, the fingertips at the middle of the thigh. **The head (owner: "i wonder if head is too small"; "his face can look more like original face in terms of realism"):** it was a tenth too small for seven and a half heads; now about 38 tall, its profile drawn with the real landmarks (forehead, brow ridge, the root of the nose, bridge and tip, upper lip, mouth, lower lip, the hollow under it, chin, the jaw back to its angle). `sides(a, b, profile, ease)` gives a part's two lines; cloth lying on a part is the same lines with a little ease.
- **3. How he walks** (`classic(u, o)`), from the studies: Richard Williams's four positions of every step, CONTACT, DOWN (the weight comes onto the front leg, the body lowest), PASSING POSITION, UP (the push off, the body highest); a slow or heavy walk takes 16 frames a step or more (his takes 19); gait laboratories: each foot on the ground about 60% of a stride, the heel strikes toe up, the foot rolls flat, then over the ball; arms against the legs; under a load he leans in, knees a little bent, arms swinging little. Each foot is placed first (its touching point never slides), the hip sinks as far as the legs need, and the knees follow from the bones. **The two lines:** the hip of the striking leg is forward at its heel strike, the shoulder line turns the other way (so the arm opposite that leg is forward); the hip of the leg in the air drops a little and the shoulders tip the other way (`turn`, `hipTurn`, `tip`, `hipTip`). **The arms** (owner: "think about how arms move - we need some animation studies"; the animators' overlapping action, "successive breaking of joints"): the shoulder leads, the upper arm follows and is furthest forward on the DOWN, not the contact (Williams), the elbow bends as the arm comes forward and straightens going back, later still, the hand trails the forearm, the fingers trail the hand (`hang`, `arm`, `elbow`, `bend`, `wrist`). **The head** (owner: "see how his head moves when he walks"): MEASURED in his own old walk (`assets/mannequin.json`): it tips forward about a degree and a half as the body rises and back as it drops, so his eyes stay level (the gait laboratories say the same), and he leans in most just after each foot has taken the weight (`head`, `nod`). **THE FEEL, MEASURED (owner: "the original has more swagger and rocking and natural feel"; "his whole torso rocks forward and back when he walks"; "deep dive into animation principles - think disney or pixar level precision"):** `tools/mannequin/signature.js` reads the same measures off the outlines of his old frames and of the mannequin, frame by frame (top and front of the head, chest, hip, hem), and gives each one's swing once and twice a stride. It showed the mannequin stiff in four ways, each now built from its cause: (1) **the torso rocks** (`rock` .022: forward as each foot takes the weight, most a seventh of a stride after the heel strikes, back as he pushes off; his old walk: the front of his head goes 2.5 px each way, the mannequin's went 1.6, now 2.5); (2) **the swagger** (`swagger` .018: once a stride the torso rolls as much again and one step is a little heavier than the other; no real walker's two steps are alike, and the turning shoulders show from the side as a roll; old 2.0 px, was 0, now 1.6); (3) **the legs were crouched** (`sink` 4, now 0): the hip rode 4 px lower than his, so the trailing thigh never went back behind the hip and the skirt's back never swung; now the leg is straight as its heel strikes, gives at the knee on the DOWN, straightens as he vaults over it (the body rises no higher than a straight leg lets it), and the thigh goes back before the foot leaves; each foot is down 62% of the stride (a slow walk); (4) **the back panel was a stiff board** hiding the skirt's swing: it hangs from the shoulders now (see cloth). **Elbows (owner: "his elbow needs to flex some when he walks"):** never straight: 17 degrees as the arm goes back, 31 as it comes forward (`elbow`, `bend`); the arms hang by their own weight, so they do not rock with the torso. **The arch is a spring (owner: "the arches of his feet to adjust as he hits ground"):** it flattens as the weight comes on, is lowest in mid-stance, and as the heel rises and the toes bend back it is wound higher than at rest (the anatomists' windlass; `ARCH`, pose numbers 25, 26). Under his load he leans in more (`lean` .075). Options: `step`, `lift`, `bob`, `sink`, `stance`, `lean` and those above. **The owner on it: "walk looks pretty good".**
- **4.
