How cloth moves** (`CL`, `clothStep()`), from the animators' principles (follow-through and overlapping action, drag: a loose part starts late, keeps going after the body, and settles; the free end latest): the skirt's front and back edge at four heights, and the cloak flap's edge at three, are each a weight on a spring: it hangs at rest (straight down from the belly and the buttock); the legs' own surfaces (`legSurf`, from the body's profile) push it out with a little ease and it cannot pass through them (cloth held out above falls nearly straight down); left alone it swings back, the hem slowest; his speed holds it back (air); when he speeds up or stops it is left behind or swings on. The hem lifts a little as the body drops; the burden sags a beat after the body. `bake()` runs a movement three times round so the cloth has settled, and keeps the cloth's pose per frame (14 numbers). The back panel hangs from the SHOULDERS, so the rocking torso and the turning shoulder line carry it to and fro and it swings on late (`carry`, `CL.fsh`); the burden hangs back as the torso rocks, then follows (`bt`); between frames poses are blended.
- **5. The clothes** (`figure()`), drawn far to near, each lying on the body with ease, **as a monk's habit sits on a man** (owner: "let's look at monk suits and how the[y] sit on frames, also bags on backs"; a habit is a tunic hung from the shoulders, girded with a cord, wide long sleeves, a hood with a shoulder cape, a scapular hanging front and back): far sleeve and hand, hose and soft boots (owner: "his feet look a little skinny and shouldn't he be wearing shoes (that flex of course)"; then "shape foot more like original pilgrims foot and width of calf too": `BODY.shoe` is a soft ankle boot, its shaft one with the leg and about 15 wide as in his painted frames, that keeps the SHAPE OF THE FOOT inside it as the drawing schools build a foot from the side: a round heel, a wedge whose top falls evenly from the instep to the toes, the toes about a quarter of its length ending in a rounded cap, a sole lifting a little at the waist (owner: "shoes maybe less clown like - look up shape of foot": a long low toe drawn to an upturned point, measured from his painted frames, was tried first and read as a clown's shoe: do not bring it back); the leg's own profile is fuller too (calf about 18, ankle about 11, as a real leg from the side) and the shaft stands 1.2 off it; the boot bends at the ball with the foot (`footPt()`), creases showing across the toes as it bends, and the toes straighten again over a few pixels of lift, so nothing snaps), the tunic (on the chest, blousing over the cord, then the cloth's own edges, flaring a little toward the hem (an A-line: `CL.rf`, `CL.rb`), to a hem at mid-shin, as a habit is pinned up for walking; a shaded back, a lit front, the hem's weight; quiet PIPE folds from the cord and a TENSION fold from the knee that pushes the cloth), the cord with its knot and one long end hanging with three knots, lying on the skirt and moving with it, the back panel (the scapular's back, showing below the sack), the burden, IN TWO TIERS as in his painted frames (owner: "The top of bag also needs some parameters - it looks weird - I think it's supposed to be two ti[e]red backpack"): a sack bound high and close on his back, as loads are carried (hung from its tie behind the shoulders, its weight low, flat against him, a rope round its girth), and a smaller bundle tied on top of it behind his hood, bound with two cords, its underside resting on the sack, jolting a little more than the sack as he walks (the gathered mouth that used to stick up behind the hood like an ear is gone), the near sleeve and hand, his head, and **the hood (owner: "it should basically start above eyes and follow contour of head")**: it starts at the top of his forehead, above his eyes, and follows his head, over the crown with a little ease (about 3 px), standing off the BACK of his head a little more (about 9 px, and hanging there; owner: "not quite so close to back of head (extends off back a tad) like the assassins creed character"), then falls to his shoulders; at the front its top comes forward over his brow to a small BEAK above the eyes and the rim turns back under it (owner: "look up assassins creed character - he's a good model": that game's hood is known for its beak-shaped point at the centre front; ours takes only that general shape, a peaked hood, nothing of the character); its rim runs from the forehead back past the temple and the cheek to under the jaw, so his whole profile shows in front of it (forehead, nose, lips, chin); its cape ends on the shoulder (owner: "should not go past his shoulder down") and rides with the near shoulder; the back sags a beat after the body. His head is BOWED (`head` .27), as in his painted frames. His face: a quiet profile like the painted one (a small nose, lips and chin stepping back), lit, the hood's shadow on his brow and down the cheek; no eye and no mouth drawn. **HOW TO READ HIS OLD HEAD (owner: "the 'start' of the hood in my original sprite is actually his nose ... i think that's where things went wrong"):** in his painted frames the long upright line at the front of the head (x about 128, y 15 to 40 in the walk pack) is HIS FACE, forehead to nose, the head bowed; the hood starts at the top of that line. `tools/mannequin/hoodtrace.py` traces that outline (all 38 frames averaged with the head held still); I first misread the line as the hood's rim and built a hood towering over a head set too low (owner: "looks awful - it goes way higher than top of his head"). His old head is about 47 tall (a sixth of his height); the mannequin's is 38 (seven and a half heads): offered to the owner, not changed. **Hoods tried and turned down, do not bring back:** a short hood hugging the skull with a long cape; a deep rounded cowl with a bulging back ("looks funny"); Zurbaran's Capuchin cone ("looks nothing like a typical one"); the tall hood from the misread tracing ("awful"). The sack's rope goes over his shoulder, down his chest and back under his arm. **A sleeve** is a wide tube hung from the shoulder: its underside sags, the arm rests against whichever side of it the arm is pushing (the cloth trails behind a swinging arm), and the cuff reaches the knuckles. A front scapular panel was NOT added (it would change his known outline): offered to the owner.
- **The look (owner: "let's go more realistic now but dark quiet silhouettes"):** `TONE` holds close dark tones (about .10 to .26), so he is a silhouette first with only a trace of form inside; `LOOK.detail` scales that trace (0 = one flat tone; the lab's "Inside" button tries none / quiet / more); `shade(tone)` will carry the game's tints.
- **Movements measured from frames stay possible** (the owner's first idea): `tools/mannequin/fit.py` follows his frames with cut-out parts and bone legs; `anims.py` keeps only what frames show reliably (hip, lean, nod, ankles, foot pitch) in `assets/mannequin.json` (3 KB); `fromTrack()` plays it on the same bones, the knees worked out from the bones. `tools/mannequin/sheet.js out.png classic "[0,4,9,14,19]" 2` renders stills; `tools/mannequin/signature.js` compares the feel of a walk with his old frames in numbers; `tools/mannequin/hoodtrace.py out.png out.json` traces the outline of his old head and hood.
- **HIS MEASURES AS SLIDERS (owner: "Can you make sliders for me to select my favorite hood"; then "At least three parameters. Same with other key body dimensions please"):** every key measure of him is a number in `MQ.DIM`, ten parts (his hands were added later, see "A NINTH LINK, AND HIS HANDS") with at least three measures each: `hood` (`beak` how far its top comes forward over his brow, `droop` how low the beak's tip hangs, `top` room over the crown, `peak` a point at the back of the crown, `back` room behind his head, `hang` how low the back hangs, `rim` how much of his cheek shows, `cape` its lower edge: above 0 a cape down over the shoulder, below 0 the hood stops on his traps as a collar round the base of his neck (-12 = at the neck's base; to -14), `head` the size of his head, 1 = seven and a half heads, `bow` how far it is bowed; and six more (owner: "add some more hood parameter like angle of hood maybe like 5 more - think creatively"): `tilt` the whole hood tipped forward or pushed back, `rake` its opening slanted back toward the jaw, `size` the whole hood larger or closer, `tail` a tail hanging from the back of the crown (an old hood's liripipe; it swings with the back panel), `roll` a turned-back rim whose inside catches a little light, `bib` how far its front falls on his chest; and five for the TIP above his forehead (owner: "more parameters about tip of hood above forehead"): `sharp` round or a sharp point, `hook` its end curled down or flipped up, `thick` how thick it is at its root, `sweep` its top scooped or bulging, `cut` how far the rim cuts back under it; all five at rest by default, so the tip is as it was until they are moved), `face` (`nose`, `chin`, `brow`, and `tip`, `bridge`, `lips`, `jaw`, `forehead`), `shoe` (`toe` its length past the ball, `point`, `lift` the tip turned up, `bulk`), `leg` (`calf`, `ankle`, `shaft` the boot's thickness), `build` (`legs`, `arms` lengths, `girth` how deep his body is, `lean`), `robe` (`hem` longer or shorter, `flare`, `full`), `sleeve` (`width`, `drop`, `len` over the hand), `sack` (`size`, `high` how low it is tied, `round`, and the bundle on top: `top` its size, 0 = none, `set` back or forward, `flat` a flat roll or a round ball; and `back`, the whole pack shifted back from him), `gait` (`step`, `bob`, `rock`, `swagger`, `arm`, `bend`). The drawing reads them as it draws (`headPt()`, `skullPts()`, `shinP()`, `shoeV()`, `st()` for the girth); after changing `build`, `robe`, `gait` or the hood's `bow`, `MQ.refresh()` makes his bones (`K` from `K0`), the cloth's rest (`CL` from `CL0`) and the walks again. The lab page has a slider for each (53), in parts that open and close; the close-up stays in view and shows the part being changed (head, feet or the whole of him); each part has "As it is now" and some have starting points (ten hoods; shoes "as in his painting"); the lines under the sliders name only what was changed, e.g. `hood=8,0,3.6,0,9,0,0,0,1,0.27` and `shoe=1,0.6,0,1` (the numbers in the order above), and the page's address carries them, so a link carries the choice. Checked on an emulated upright iPhone: every slider at both its ends gives no error and no broken pose, and a link restores its choice. **When the owner sends lines or a link, put those numbers into the defaults at the top of `mannequin.js` (`HOOD`, `FACE`, `SHOE`, `LEG`, `BUILD`, `ROBE`, `SLEEVE`, `SACK`, `GAIT`), and say so here.** **THE OWNER'S FIRST CHOICE, now his defaults (sent as a link):** `face=0.75,1.1,1.5` (a smaller nose, a little more chin, a stronger brow), `shoe=0.86,0.35,0.4,0.88` (a shorter toe, a little pointed, its tip barely lifted, lower over the foot), `leg=1.08,0.92,2.1` (a fuller calf, a slighter ankle, a thick boot shaft), `build=1.04,1.08,1.11,0.135` (longer legs and arms, stouter, leaning in much more), `robe=-2,1,0.98`, `sleeve=13,1.1,0` (narrow sleeves ending at the wrist), `sack=1.43,-0.06,1.05` (a much bigger burden, tied higher). **Then the hood (a second link):** `hood=2.6,-3,2.9,0,6.8,8.2,-2.8,0,0.99,0.38` (a short beak tipped up a little, close over the crown, less room behind but hanging low, more of his cheek covered, his head bowed further: .38), then a third link, `hood=2,-2.8,2.1,0,6.8,8.2,-2.8,0,0.99,0.38,0.1,0.8,0.96,0,0,21` (a shorter beak, closer over the crown, tipped a little forward, its opening raked back, a touch smaller, its front falling 21 on his chest), with "Can you have hood not go over shoulder": those are the hood's defaults now, except `cape` (-12) and `bib` (see next). **THE HOOD STOPS ON HIS TRAPS (owner, of -12 as first built, where the edge still sat on the shoulder's top: "Hood should stop at his traps well before shoulders", with a fourth link, `hood=...,0.1,-1,4.6,0,0.2&build=1.04,0.98,1.11,0.135`):** below 0 the lower edge is now a collar round the base of his neck (`up`, `ce`, `EDGE`, `hoodEdge()`, `pouch` in `figure()`): higher at the back of the neck, lower at the pit of the throat, as the base of a neck is; it no longer rides with the near shoulder; the back no longer bags out over his upper back; the robe has a neck of its own under it (a small patch round the neck's base, so no gap shows). Measured with his body showing through the clothes (the neck's base, then clear robe, then the shoulder's top about 4 lower): the sleeve's cap stays below the hood's edge through the whole stride. **THE FRONT COMES DOWN ONTO HIS CHEST (owner, shown the hood ending under his chin with `bib` 0: "Can you move down front to chest"):** `bib` is the owner's 21 again, and the fall is shaped as a hood's front lies: from under his jaw the cloth hangs, bellying forward a little, to a rounded foot on the chest; its seam with the robe runs from the neck's side down to that foot; and **the near shoulder and arm are drawn again OVER the hood** (they are nearer to us than his neck and chest), so the shoulder stays clear and the fall shows on the chest in front of the arm. (Tried first: the fall kept wholly in front of the arm with the hood drawn over it; it was a thin tongue like a strap.) As `cape` goes from 0 down, the arm over the hood fades in (full by about -5), so the slider does not jump. **A FIFTH LINK, now his defaults, and the first with THE WALK set:** `hood=...,20,0.1,-1,4.6,0.2,0` (the front fall 20, the tip's top bulging a touch, no cut under it), `build=1.04,0.98,0.98,0.135` (slimmer: girth .98, was 1.11), `robe=-12,0,1.17` (a shorter robe, no flare, a fuller skirt), `sack=1.5,-0.06,1.14,0.8,6.5,1.1` (a bigger, rounder burden; the bundle on top set forward 6.5 and a little rounder), `gait=96,3.6,0.044,0.018,0.31,0.24` (a slightly longer step, the torso rocking twice as much, .044, and the arms swinging much more, .31 against .12). No points were dragged by hand in it. Checked: opened, the link now lists nothing as changed; stills of eight frames of the stride show no broken pose. **A seventh link, the walk again, now his defaults:** `gait=96,3.6,0.036,0.02,0.02,0.16`: the arms hardly swing (`arm` .02, was .31), the elbows flex less (`bend` .16), the torso rocks a little less (`rock` .036), a touch more swagger (.02): a man bowed under his load, his arms hanging. From the fourth link, now defaults too: the tip `sharp` .1, `hook` -1 (flipped up a little), `cut` .2, and `build` arms .98 (shorter). The walk is still as it was. Before the first link they were: face 1, 1, 1; shoe 1, 0, 0, 1; leg 1, 1, 1.2; build 1, 1, 1, .075; robe 0, 1, 1; sleeve 23, 1, 7; sack 1, 0, 1. `load()` now calls `refresh()`, so his measures are in force from the first moment.
- **THE HOOD SHAPED BY HAND (owner: "Maybe draggable Bézier curves"):** under the hood's sliders, "Shape it by hand: drag its points": he stands still, the hood fills a taller close-up, and the 22 points of its outline show as gold rings on a gold line (the hood's own edge: a smooth curve THROUGH the points, which is easier under a finger than Bézier's off-curve handles). A touch or the mouse takes the nearest ring within a finger's width and moves it by as much as the finger moves (so the finger need not cover it); a touch away from every ring still scrolls the page. Undo, "Points back as they were", Done. Each point keeps an offset of its own in ITS OWN FRAME (his head's, or his neck's), laid on top of what the sliders give, so a hand-drawn shape goes with him as he walks and the sliders still work under it: `MQ.HAND.hood` (`r0`..`r5` the rim from under the jaw up, `tip`, `tt` the tip's top, `cf` the crown's front, `c1`..`c4` over the crown, `b1`..`b3` down the back, `po` where the back meets the collar, `e0`..`e4` the lower edge to its foot on the chest); `hpt()` and `pl()` in `figure()` apply them, and `MQ.rec(array)` makes `figure()` note each point's place (and the outline) for the lab; `MQ.curve` is exported for drawing the line. The link and the lines carry them as `hand=tip_6_-1,b2_-2.7_3.1` (name, across, down; `hand=none` = none). **When the owner sends a link with `hand=`, put those offsets into `HAND.hood` at the top of `mannequin.js` and say so here.** With no point moved the figure is the same pixel for pixel. Checked on an emulated upright iPhone with real touches (drag, undo, a touch in the corner takes nothing, the link restores the shape) and with the mouse. Only the hood so far; the same could be done for the burden, the shoe or the face if wanted. **THE OWNER'S OWN SHAPE, now the hood's defaults (a sixth link, `hand=b2_-1.3_-5.2,b3_-2.3_-3.8,e0_-0.9_2.2,e1_6.8_1.1,e4_4.1_-3.3,po_-5_1.7,r0_2.3_5.1`):** the back of the hood drawn in, its collar level and further forward, its foot on the chest a little up and forward, the rim lower under his jaw. In the lab "Points back as they were" now returns to this shape (`hand=none` in a link gives the sliders' shape alone).
- **THE NEAR SHOULDER BELONGS TO HIS BODY (owner: "His shoulder doesn't seem cohesive with his torso (rotates like a separate piece)").** Found in close-ups across the stride: the shoulder was a round cap ON TOP OF THE ARM (a disc 10.5 at the joint, standing as high as the hood's collar), in the arm's lighter tone, so the whole arm read as a paper doll's pinned-on limb, worse since the owner set a big arm swing. Now, as cut-out animators hide a pivot: (1) the cloth over the shoulder is a YOKE fixed to the body (`yoke()` in `figure()`): from the back of his neck it slopes down and rounds over the shoulder (the trapezius' slope), and the same in front under the hood's fall; it is anchored to the neck above and the joint below, so it gives as the shoulder line turns, and the arm turns under it; (2) the sleeve fits closer at the shoulder (`upperSleeve`: ease 1.3 at the top, 3.2 from two fifths down; the joint's disc 8.8), so its top is lower than his collar; (3) the near sleeve has the ROBE'S OWN TONE at the shoulder and grows lighter only down the arm (`nearTone`, a gradient from his neck to about 30 below), so the arm comes out of the body instead of lying on it. The far arm has the closer fit too. **Rule for any figure on this skeleton: a shoulder is drawn with the body (a fixed yoke in the body's tone), never as the arm's round top.**
- **FOUR POINTS FROM THE OWNER ("Can the pack be adjusted to sit back horizontally a little - also can I adjust the shape of the top. Also a few more face feature change parameters. The strap of his bag cuts through his arm"):** (1) **the pack sits back:** a new measure `sack.back` (the whole pack shifted straight back from him; slider -4 to 16), set to 4 as "a little"; (2) **the bundle on top shaped by hand:** `MQ.HAND.top`, nine points `u0`..`u8` round it (`hptG('top', ...)` in `figure()`), dragged in the lab like the hood's ("Shape the bundle on top by hand" under the burden's sliders; `hand=` in the link carries both shapes, a name `u` + digit is the bundle's; "Points back as they were" and "As it is now" reset only the shape being worked on); (3) **five more measures of his face** (`FACE`, in `skullPts()`): `tip` the nose's tip turned up or hooked down, `bridge` a hump on its bridge, `lips` thinner or fuller, `jaw` lighter or deeper, `forehead` sloping back or forward; all at rest, so his face is as it was (59 sliders in all now); (4) **THE STRAP** used to run down his chest and back to the sack, hidden only where the arm lay over it, so it showed on both sides of the arm as if it went through it. Now (`strap()`, `strapOn()`): from the sack up over the top of his shoulder, down in front of the shoulder, and in under his arm, where it ends out of sight (its last points go with the arm, so the arm always covers its end); drawn under the arm, and again over the shoulder itself (clipped to the yoke and the joint's disc) once the arm is drawn. **Rule: a strap, belt or cord that passes behind a limb must END behind it, never show on both sides of it.**
- **THE BUNDLE'S DECOR FOLLOWS ITS SHAPE (owner, having dragged the bundle taller and fuller: "Can you adjust decor on top part of burden now that size has changed", with an eighth link).** What is drawn on the bundle used to sit at fixed places, so on the owner's bigger bundle the cords stopped short and its underside and shadow cut across its middle. Now all of it is placed from the bundle's nine points wherever they are (`BL`, `mx`, `cord()` in `figure()`): each cord runs from a place on the top edge to a place on the bottom edge, bowed a little; the underside is the lower part between the back and front points; the shadow on the sack lies under the lower edge. The sack's tie at its mouth is drawn BEFORE the bundle (the bundle sits on it; it lay across the bundle). And a chink of sky that opened between his neck, the sack and the bundle once the pack sat back is closed by a small piece in the sack's tone drawn first, under everything (the sack's mouth drawn up to his shoulders). `tools`-free check used: every frame rendered on magenta and searched for background shut inside his outline. **The eighth link, now his defaults:** `sack` back 7 (was 4), `gait` bob 3.3 and rock .032, and the bundle's own shape, `HAND.top` (`u0_3.5_-2.5,u1_2.5_-1.6,u2_1.5_-3.8,u4_1_0.5,u5_1_2.7,u6_-2.8_6.4,u7_-3.6_3.3,u8_-1.1_-2.2`). **Rule: anything drawn ON a shape that can be reshaped must be placed from that shape's own points, never at fixed places.**
- **A NINTH LINK, AND HIS HANDS (owner, a link, then: "can you make hands look more normal too").** **The link, now his defaults:** `face=0.75,1.1,1.5,-0.5,0.3,0.65,0.2,0.2` (the nose's tip turned up a little, a slight hump on its bridge, thinner lips, a deeper jaw, the forehead a little forward), `build=1.07,0.98,0.98,0` (longer legs, and **`lean` 0: he no longer leans in**; he walks upright under his load, only his head bowed), `sack` high -.05, `gait=95,2.7,0.032,0.016,0.02,0.16` (a slightly shorter step, less up and down, a little less swagger). **His hands** (`hand()`, `FINGERS`, a new group of measures `HANDS` / `MQ.DIM.hands`: `size`, `width`, `fingers` their length, `curl` 0 = straight, `thumb`; the link's key is `hands=`, not to be confused with `hand=`, the hand-dragged points): they were a broad mitten with small teeth for fingertips, and the fingers curled FORWARD, toward the thumb, which no hand can do. Now, by the drawing schools' measures: the back of the hand and the fingers about the same length (`K.palm` 14, `K.fing` 11.5), the whole about a third as wide as long (a hanging hand is seen a little turned), narrow at the wrist; it hangs from the wrist by its own weight, more upright than the forearm (pose numbers 21, 22: -.14), the back of the hand toward us; the thumb lies in front along the first finger, drawn first (it is on the palm's side), its round tip standing a little away at the knuckles; four fingers of unequal length (the middle longest, the little one shortest and set higher), each a line thinning to a round tip, bending at its three joints and curling BACK, toward the palm, which faces his thigh, the little finger most (pose numbers 23, 24: -.24); a fine dark line between the fingers and where the hand's edge lies over the thumb (scaled by `LOOK.detail`). The space between the fingers and the web beside the thumb are filled, so no hairline of sky shows inside the hand (checked frame by frame). The lab has five sliders for them (64 in all) with a close-up of the near hand (`view` 'hand'), and three starting points (open, loosely closed, big working hands). **Rule: a part that bends must bend the way the real joint does; check which way before drawing it.**
- **HIS CAPE (owner: "can you draw cape over his should[er] and between his back and pack and down", then, stopping my first try: "see how it looks on original sprite?").** My first try was a mantle on the shoulder and a wide panel below the pack, with the part between HIDDEN under the pack. His painted frames (`christian-walk-nb.pack`, looked at enlarged with the contrast raised) show it as **ONE LONG BAND of cloth**: it lies over the top of his near shoulder, runs down behind his arm, where it shows as a strip between the arm and the pack, and below the pack hangs on outside the skirt almost to the hem, ending in a point behind, with long folds running its whole length. So in `figure()`: (1) its body (`capeBody`, after the pack and before the arm, so the strip lies over the pack's front edge as in the painting): from the back of his neck down his back standing a little off the robe, then the cloth's own swinging edge (`fl`, the old flap's springs: it flies and settles later than the skirt) to the point (`o2`), a hem slanting up to its front edge (`cf`), which comes round his side to about the line of his arm (`cX`); its back in shade, its front edge turned a little to the light, two long folds from the shoulder to the hem (`fold()`), its shadow on the skirt (clipped to the robe); (2) `capeTop()`, the part over the near shoulder, drawn over the arm (and again after the arm is redrawn over the hood): from the base of his neck over the shoulder, its lower edge sweeping from the front of the shoulder down and back across the upper arm (a cloak thrown back, the arm free in front of it); its top is fixed to the body, its fall lies on the arm and goes with it; the pack's strap lies over it. It took the place of the old narrow back panel. Tones `cape` .16, `capeSh`, `capeLt` (a shade darker than the robe, so it reads). Measures `CAPE` / `MQ.DIM.cape`, five sliders in the lab (69 in all): `fall` how far it falls over his arm (0 = none on the shoulder), `len` how low it hangs behind, `full` how far it stands out behind, `wrap` how far round his side it comes, `slant` its hem level or slanting to a point (`cape=` in the link). The same link, now his defaults: hood `beak` 1.6, `droop` -2.6, `top` 2.3; hands `curl` .3 (nearly open). **Rule: before designing a part of him, LOOK at how his painted frames show it** (the mechanics stay our own; what he wears and how it lies is design, and the painting is the design).
- **THE STUTTER IN HIS STEP (owner: "he has a little stutter to his step").** MEASURED first (I cannot watch motion): every joint's place frame by frame, and how suddenly its speed changes. Three causes, each fixed at its cause in `mannequin.js`: (1) **the hip's height had corners.** The hip rides by its own bob but must sink wherever a leg could not otherwise reach its foot; that was worked out afresh at each frame, so he dropped 4 units in two frames at every heel strike and then wavered, and his knees (a nearly straight leg bends a great deal for a small change of height) jumped about: 15, 25, 28, 24, 29 degrees. Now `hipLine(o)` works out the whole stride's line first (304 places, `stride(u, o)` giving the feet, the two lines, the bob and the height the legs `need`), smooths it over about a frame each way, holds it down again wherever a leg needs it, a few times over, and lowers the whole line by the little still wanted: smooth everywhere, every foot still reached (`o.hipLine`, made in `walk()`; `classic()` reads it). The worst sudden change of the hip's speed went from 2.8 units a frame to .5; the knee now bends and straightens four clean times a stride (strike 17, the weight coming on 27, mid-stance 20, 34 as the heel rises, 67 in the air, 16 before the strike). (2) **the foot's roll paused at every key** (`key()` eased each stretch between two keys by itself): now one smooth line through all the keys, keeping its pace through each (the harmonic mean of the stretches on both sides) and resting only where the value turns back or lies level, so it never overshoots; the heel comes a little higher before the toe leaves (`PITCH`: -46 degrees at toe-off, was -40; the foot flat at .09 of the stride, was .07), so the knee bends steadily into the swing instead of straightening first. (3) **the foot in the air came down still travelling forward and stopped dead at the strike:** its path now joins the planted foot's at the landing, in place and in speed (it reaches a little past its landing place and is drawing back with the ground as the heel comes down: the gait laboratories' "swing leg retraction"), and it comes down softly (`lift` by sin to the power 1.4). Tried and not kept: the same joining at toe-off (a foot that trailed on for a moment after the toe left made the knee straighten and bend again, a small hitch of its own). His poses are nearly the same as before (stills compared); what changed is how he gets from one to the next. `GAIT0` holds the walk's own defaults. **Rule: any value worked out afresh at each frame from a limit (a `max`, a clamp) has corners; work out the whole movement's line first and smooth it. And keys must never be eased one stretch at a time.**
- **"NOW IT KINDA LOOKS LIKE HE'S TIP TOEING" (owner, of lab 25's stutter fix).** I had over-softened the step; four causes, each put right (lab 26; what the bullet above says of the hip's line, the heel at toe-off and the landing is replaced by this): (1) **no weight came down on the heel:** the smoothed hip line was lowest exactly AS the heel touched, so he let himself down before each step and set the foot on the ground, as a man creeps. A walker FALLS onto his front foot (Williams: CONTACT, then the DOWN two or three frames later, the body lowest as the weight comes on and the knee gives, then up over the leg to the PASSING position). `hipLine(o)` now BUILDS the line from those places instead of smoothing what the legs need: highest at the passing position (`pHi` .27 of the stride), as high as a nearly straight leg lets it; still coming down at the heel strike, where it is just low enough for both legs to reach; lowest a little after (`pLo` .07, about two and a half frames); then up, quicker than it came down (cosine eases between); the owner's `bob` deepens the DOWN (.15 a unit); the swagger makes one step heavier; last the whole line is lowered by whatever any leg still wants (now about a tenth of a unit). (2) **the foot was SET down:** joined to the ground's own pace it hovered and drew back before landing. Now half of that (`m1` .5: it slows before it lands, but it lands), and it comes down at a steady rate (`lift` by plain sin in the second half of the swing; still soft at lift-off). (3) **he was on the balls of his feet too long and too high:** the heel leaves later and lower again (`PITCH`: flat until .36, only -12 degrees when the other heel strikes, -40 at toe-off). (4) **the foot in the air was pointed down through mid-swing and its toes came up only in the last two frames:** now it is level by .87 of the stride and toe-up 14 degrees two frames before the strike, 18 at the strike (`PITCH` .69 -52, .78 -22, .87 2, .94 14), so the heel plainly leads. Measured after: the hip's worst sudden change of speed .4 units a frame (2.8 before lab 25), the knee 20 at the strike, 29 as the weight comes on, 16 at the passing position, 30 as the heel rises, 58 in the air. **Rule: smoothness is not the whole of a walk. Weight shows in WHEN the body is lowest (after the heel strikes, not at it) and in the toes being up before the heel lands; check both whenever the walk is changed.**
- **THE LAB PAGE UPDATES ITSELF (owner: "I don't see new sliders": the live page had them; the phone showed an old saved copy).** `mannequin.html` carries a number, `const LAB = N` in its head, shown at the top of the page ("lab N"); on opening it fetches its own newest copy (`cache: 'no-store'`) and, if that copy's number is higher, reloads once onto `?v=N` keeping the measures in the address; `mannequin.js` is loaded as `mannequin.js?v=N`. **Bump `LAB` on every push that changes the lab (currently 27), and give the owner the link with `?v=N`.** A copy saved before this was added cannot update itself: it needs the `?v=` link once.
- **WHY HE DOES NOT YET LOOK AS GOOD AS THE PAINTING (owner, of lab 26: "i really like all the work you've done here - just don't feel it looks better than the orginal - something about that original drawing looks so good - why can't we reproduce it with first principles").** Answered, nothing built, nothing spent. Four moments of the stride were laid side by side: his painting as painted, his painting toned near-black, the mannequin. What the sheet shows: (1) the painting still wins when toned near-black, so most of the gap is the SILHOUETTE: one sweeping gesture from the hood down the cloak to its ragged flying tail, a tattered hem, an uneven hand-made edge; the mannequin is well-made PARTS (pack, hood, body, skirt, legs) with smooth even curves; (2) inside the outline the painting has about three times the range of tone and about sixteen times the drawn variation (washes, lost and found edges) against flat close tones and thin even lines; (3) the measures chosen one at a time have drifted from the painting: his robe there reaches the ankle (ours stops below the knee and shows long legs), his pack there is about as wide as his body (ours is larger than his body and set back), his hood and head are larger (about 47 against 38). First principles give how he MOVES and how cloth hangs; what makes the painting good is taste (which edge to lose, which fold to draw), and the rule "nothing traced or copied" keeps that out. **Lesson: mechanics from first principles, the look from the painting; a figure built from rules comes out correct before it comes out beautiful.** Offered: (A) one free still of a design pass beside the painting (its proportions, the cloak as one ragged sweep, a hand-made edge, washes for tone); (B) the mannequin as the pose-maker (true poses, level feet, steady size) with the paint coming from the image and video models over its stills, free sources first; either way the painted walk stays in the game. **The owner chose A ("A let's go"): see the next point; B is held.**
- **THE PAINTED LOOK (lab 27; owner: "A let's go"; nothing spent). A second look for the mannequin, OFF by default: the dark look and the owner's measures are untouched, pixel for pixel (checked).** Made by looking at his painted frames enlarged four times: light washes in a mid grey with lighter islands and a few darker pools, a few long dark drawn lines, a thin dark outline that comes and goes, a ragged hem, and the cloak as one broad band sweeping back from the shoulder to a ragged tail at the knee. In `mannequin.js`: (1) `LOOK.paint` 1 makes `figure()` draw in the painting's own key (`PTONE`, about .29 to .67, the key of his pack frames) with a worn hem (uneven, a tatter or two; set along the hem by `hs()`, so it goes with the cloth), the cape's tail in two ragged lobes (`hq`, `tail`), shading bands as soft washes (`edge()` in ten faint passes), two fold lines on the cape and two on the skirt of unequal weight, and without the pack's girth rope, the tension fold across the skirt and the hood's back band; (2) a new measure, `CAPE.sweep` (0 = its front edge straight down, as before; 1 = sweeping back to the tail; a slider; `cape=` in a link now has six numbers; 70 sliders); (3) `MQ.PAINTING`, his painting's measures as a starting point laid over `MQ.DIM` (head 1.22, legs 1, robe hem 13 / flare 1.5 / full 1.3, sleeves 22 / 1.1 / 6, sack size 1.08 / round 1.3 / top .9 / set 4 / back 2 / high -.02, cape fall 30 / len .78 / full 1.8 / wrap .62 / slant .35 / sweep .7), measured off the frames (his hem there is about a sixth of his height above the ground, his pack about a fifth of his height wide); (4) `MQ.paintFrame(S, Q, z)` (and `MQ.painted()`, drawn like `figure()`; `PBOX`, `paintFields()`): the flat shapes painted over pixel by pixel: every edge wanders as a hand-drawn line does (the picture read through two slow fields of drift), washes (islands, pools, a faint tide-line), a drawn line where one shape meets another at a clear step of tone (read two thirds of a unit each way, so its weight is the same at any size; lost in places), pigment gathered along the outline, the paper's grain. **The paint goes with the cloth:** the fields are read where each row of him has come from (above the hip his spine as he rocks and bobs, below it the skirt as it swings), and washes and drift fade below the knee, so paint does not slide over him and swinging feet do not swim. About 22 ms a picture at the game's size (z 1), 90 ms at 1.5. **MEASURED (I cannot watch motion):** inside the outline the painted mannequin changes 7.7 (of 255) from one frame to the next with a tonal spread of 31; his painted frames change 10.0 with a spread of 25: it should flicker no more than the walk the owner likes. **In the lab:** "Look: dark / painted" (`look=paint` in the link; each picture of the walk is painted once and kept, `PC`, freed and made again when a measure changes; while a slider moves he shows in the painting's flat tones) and "Measures: yours / his painting's". **Tried in this pass and changed, do not bring back:** four evenly spaced dark folds on the cape (a striped curtain); hard-edged shading bands with lines drawn at every tone step (piping and doubled lines: shading must be soft where lines are found by tone); thin spike tatters (claws); a second small layer of washes (camouflage); a thin tension fold crossing the pipe folds (a strap across the skirt); a line read one pixel each way (its weight changed with the size). **Where it stands, honestly:** he is now of the painting's family; the painting is still looser and bolder in its line, ours tidier. Stills for the owner must be made at the size he is seen (z 1 to 1.5), not larger: the line reads thinner enlarged. **Next, on the owner's word:** the game's tints over this key (it is the key of his pack frames, so `drawFrame`'s tint should fit), baking the walk's pictures for a level, the sleeve's folds and a bent arm as in the painting, bolder calligraphic line; or B.
- **Where it stands:** the owner on the hood that follows his head: "Hoods look better"; since then the hood stands off the back of his head a tad and has a small beak over the brow, and the owner has set his hood, face, shoes, lower legs, build, robe, sleeves, burden (its top bundle too) and now his walk with the sliders; the hood now stops on his traps at the base of the neck, its front falls onto his chest, its outline is the owner's own (dragged by hand in the lab), and his near shoulder is drawn as part of his body: waiting on the owner's eyes (I cannot watch motion). Still short of his old walk by the numbers: the skirt's swing at the hem and the back panel's once-a-stride swing. Known rough spots: the far sleeve is a dark tube when that arm swings forward; fold lines are thin drawn lines. The robe's outline is still designed, not traced from a reference (no clean side view of a habit was found: Commons answers slowly; the paintings found are from in front). Open question to the owner: his old head is about 47 tall, the mannequin's 38.
- **Next, on the owner's word:** the look (toward the game's quiet silhouettes); standing, stopping and turning; the run; kneeling; arms that act (reach, pray, cover his ears); the game's tints and a switch to try him in a level; other figures as other clothes on the same skeleton. Paid videos only where a movement is unknown (ask first; up to $5 allowed in all).

## Level 2, Slough of Despond (`slough.js`, object `SL`)

**The picture (Ansel Adams; owner: "ansel adams style twisted oak ... beautiful and stunning especially composition wise"):** nearly black and white with a breath of colour; two-thirds sky; one great black twisted oak on the far bank (five great limbs sweeping smoothly out of a short thick trunk; the long limb over the water nearly level and well above Help's head), pale ghost trees; the camera draws back in the mire. The slough is one map of mud and pools (`mudMap`, `poolAt`): all mud by the home bank, pools opening and joining into clear water by the far bank; where someone flounders a pool is stirred to mud for a few seconds (`churns`). Nothing is mirrored in the mud; Christian in the mire is drawn after the mirror (`chrLate`).

**How it goes (`{25}` to `{36}`):** the plain in gold, the two talking (`OPEN`) → mist and grey come in → they walk out over the mire, deeper at each step (`MUDDY`), and fall in (no input; a slow sink, `sunk`) → they wallow in bursts, apart (`burst`); Pliable's anger; he struggles back and climbs out on his home side, hurries off into the mist → the choice: "Go back with Pliable" (a few steps back, his real turn, nothing lost) or struggle on → hold to wade, each step a note of the minor tune (`WADE_TUNE`), sinking deeper (`DEEP`) → at the far bank holding only strains for a few seconds; "could not get out" → Help hurries in from the right out of the mist bank and kneels at once (`HSEQ`, `fogBank`); Christian's arm is already up → "Give me your hand" (act `'hand'`: touch Help's hand) → the grip, a major chord, the lift (`LIFT`) → he looks back (his turn video); his lights fly to the hidden steps, each a soft tone of one rising chord (`LAST`; the hymn's last phrase until build 376); gold returns → "Go on your way"; Help turns and walks back into the mist (`HTURN`) → hold to walk on into thickening mist with the far light shining through ahead (`END_X`, `ahead`) → end card, "Go on" to Level 3. Strained, breathless voices for the six lines said in the mire (owner likes them, build 125). Help is a strong plain working man, one of the King's labourers (`{34}`): bare-headed, short beard, sleeves rolled, nothing in his hands; he hurries ("not run but quick"). The fears are sound and one storyteller line only.

**Code map:** words `OPEN`, `NEAR`, `FALL`, `BACK`, `ALONE`, `FEARS`, `STUCK`, `HELP`, `DRAWN`, `STEPS`, `ONWAY`; phases `ph` (`plain`, `fall`, `pli`, [`back`, `backtalk`], `wade`, `stuck`, `help`, `drawn`, `onway`, `look`, `on`); figures `C2` (`turnC`, `C2.tv`, `C2.rt` reach clock, `C2.wa` wade clock), `pli` (stop video `META.stop`, `STOP_ENTRY`, `plCen`, `pli.wal`, climb `META.climb`), `help` (`HWALK`, `HSEQ`, `HTURN`, `mv`); `lift`; places `HOME0`, `BANK0`, `WADE0`, `STUCK_X`, `GRASP`, `bankY` (level shelves); depths `SINK`, `C_SINK`, `PLI_SINK`, `DEEP`; `cam` and `look` eased by phase; painting `bakeTree`, `oak()`, `skyMist`/`skyClear`, `nearC`, mirror strips, `mudMap`, `mudC`, `wetAt`, `ripples(far)`, `mound`, `FIG_DIP`, `low()`, `threads`, `splash`/`drops`, `prints`; sound `bowed`, `sfxS`, `footfall`; `AT2` skip places; `SL.title()`, `SL.grip()`, `SL.peek()`, `SL.oakPic()`.

**Pliable's turn in the bog (build 140):** there are no frames of him turning, so it is a heave round, not a slow dissolve: he ducks into the mud with a splash and a ring, the picture swaps over a few ticks at the bottom of the duck under the splash, and he comes up facing his own side (`pli.tq`, `pli.duck`, `pli.from`/`pli.tgt`). A real turn would need a video (about 56 cents): only if the owner asks.

**No blank space under a sinking figure (build 141):** Christian's floundering pictures (`christian-wade.pack`) end at the video's own mud line, the waist (63 units above his feet). They used to come in by the clock while he was only knee-deep, so paper showed under him ("a flickering blank space"). They now come in only once he has sunk nearly that far (`wk` follows `C2.y`, 44 to 57); until then his walking figure sinks, cut at the surface; any small rest is filled with the body's own width in its dark tone (`lastRow`, in `figure()`, only with `FIG_DIP`). **A picture that a video cut at its own mud line must never be shown higher than that line.**

**THE MUD ANSWERS THE FIGURES (build 147; owner, build 141: "it doesn't stick to character [or] act like coordinated response to their movements"; the owner said yes to the plan).** The random timers are gone (`burst` only paces the men's own heaving now; `footfall` keeps only its sound and the tune's note). ONE mud surface is fed by the figures' own pictures: every picture of a man in the mire is recorded as it is drawn (`MREC`, `RECS`, in `figure()`), and `sense()` reads, in that picture's see-through mask (`amap`, half size, measured once per frame; `runsAt`), where the body and each arm cross the mud line, and compares with the last picture. (1) A slow heavy height field along the slough (`fh`, `fv`, `fStep`, `fPush`, `fAt`): a body edge moving out heaps the mud ahead, one drawing back leaves a sag, sinking lifts it, rising drops it. (2) A collar of mud hugs every crossing, heavier the more he moves (`coll()`), drawn as ONE shape on a strip canvas (`bandC`) faded along its length by a one-row picture (`profC`): narrow strips side by side showed as a comb. (3) A new arm crossing = a plop (`plop`: a dip with a swell either side, a slow ring, a few heavy drops, a thick sound). (4) A crossing gone = a string of mud drawn up after the hand (`pullOut`, `strings`), followed in the picture itself (`handAt`) until too long, then it breaks into drops; when the whole body leaves the mud (the lift) strings come from both sides. (5) What has been under stays dark and wet (`FIG_WET` in `figure()`; `C2.D` how high the mud reached on Christian, drying very slowly after the lift; `liftWet()` for the lift picture, from Christian's head height by frame, `LIFT_HEAD`, `LIFT_SPLIT`; Pliable wet to the waist once out). Pliable in the mire is now drawn late like Christian (`pliMire`), after the mirror, with the churned mud, the far half of each ring, the figures, the surface, the strings, the drops and the near half of each ring, in that order. **The two halves of a ring are now one ring** (owner: "upper half ... and lower half should really tie together"): same curve, weight, tone and light lip, ending at the same two points, far half only a little fainter (.82), and both laid over the same mud (the dark churned patch used to cover the far half); the far half is clipped off the banks. `SL.peek().mud` reports strings, field heights, drops, rings for tests. Nothing spent. **I cannot watch motion: the owner's eyes decide** (stills at twice the size look right; a probe run showed strings and plops at real arm movements through the fall, the wallow, the wading and the lift).

**MOTION REVIEW OF THE MIRE (build 182; owner: "reanalyze mud in depth. make sure motions look clean and not jerky or drawn out and mud interaction looks good").** How it was checked: a tick-by-tick log of the whole level (positions, the body's crossings, the collar, the field; the page's own loop frozen with `KS.paused` so only the test steps it), filmstrips of every key moment (`SL.peek()` reports `mud.recs`, `c2`, `pl` for this), and close strips at twice the size every second tick. Found and fixed: (1) **a long double picture as each man fell in:** Christian stood stiff and upright for about two seconds, then walk and struggling pictures crossed over another second; now they plunge to the hips at once under the splash (`sunk`: .8 of the way in 42 ticks, then slowly on to the waist) and the struggling picture takes over in about a sixth of a second (`wk`), never shown higher than its own mud line (drawn at `max(C2.y, 58)`; filling the gap below it showed as a flat dark box); Pliable's swap is quicker too (`pli.wk` +.11 a tick). (2) **Drops flew higher than their heads** (about 120 units, like confetti): heavier now (gravity .21, slower throws; the fall's splash smaller), so they rise a few hand-widths at most. (3) **Pliable's struggle loop reversed at full speed** at each end (a jerk): eased with a cosine (`pong`). (4) **The collar jumped** when an arm joined or left the body at the surface (up to 20 units in one picture): its shape now follows the body softly (`S.sb`), an arm's collar grows in as it enters (`S.armRuns` with their age) and fades as it leaves (`S.ghost`). Checked and fine: Christian's own motion is continuous everywhere (the steepest is stepping down the home bank's short face, a third of a second); the wading collar and plops; Pliable's climb; the lift's wet darkening. Nothing spent.

**Build 186 (owner: "show a bit more of Christian at the far bank... also still feels a little jerky ... i'm not sure christian should have book in his hand").** (1) He sinks less as he crosses (`DEEP` 8, was 22): by the far bank his head, shoulders and chest show; the camera stands a little closer there (`z` .66, was .6; Help's coming draws it back for the oak). (2) **Why it was jerky, found in the pictures:** his struggling video (`christian-wade.pack`, 120 frames, 12 a second) was played at up to 1.3 times its own speed in bursts and dropped to a fifth in an instant; now it never runs faster than its own speed and its pace eases (`waStep`, `C2.waV`; Pliable's `pli.wtV` the same), and each picture melts into the next (`blendIm` in `drawChr`; Pliable's climb too). (3) **The book in his hand jumps about in the mire:** in the struggling video it flips from hand to hand, appears and vanishes; in the reaching video it is at his hip and then simply gone. Not fixable in code; proposed to the owner: new videos made from book-less pictures (see HANDOFF).

**NO BOOK AFTER LEVEL 1 (build 189; owner: "i'm not sure christian should have book in his hand" ... "Everywhere please" ... "That's expensive! Any way w[e can] do it without spending that much - be honest").** New videos for it (about $2.95) were proposed and turned down as too dear; instead the book was painted out of the existing pictures, free: in each frame it was found (by following it from frame to frame, or by searching for a few hand-picked pictures of it) and filled with the robe round it, the outline rebuilt where it stood out past his body (scripts kept outside the repo: OpenCV's inpainting). Level 1 keeps the book (Bunyan's opening: "a book in his hand"). After it his walking, standing and turning pictures are the book-free copies (`christian-walk-nb`, `-stand-nb`, `-turn-nb` packs; the shared `CHR`, `STD`, `TURN` arrays are filled from one set or the other: `bookless(on)`, `bookSync()`, `NOBOOK`, `BK`, `wantNB`; `start()` turns it off, `go2()`/`go3()` on); the moment he lifts the book while standing became a soft settle into his breathing; the struggling video (`christian-wade.pack`, Level 2 only) was cleaned in place; in the reaching video the book is under the mud. In Level 3 he no longer opens his book and weeps at Evangelist's feet: he bows low and trembles (his standing picture leaned forward from his feet, in `drawChr`). **Build 197 (owner: "Let's do 1 and 3 - I think bowing will show up later at foot of cross - can you make this versatile"): a real KNEEL** (one video, about 56 cents, right first time; `assets/christian-kneel.pack`, 96 frames at 12 a second, `KNEEL`, `KNEEL_META` in `index.html` so any level can use it). Made from his book-free quiet standing picture (frame 0 joins it; `ax` set so the body does not jump), facing right on white, burden on his back, hands empty: he goes slowly down on both knees, clasps his hands, bows his head, then only breathes (frames 50 to 95, `hold`, played back and forth eased). Played backwards it is his rising. In Level 3, "fell down at his feet" plays it forward (`C3.kf`, `C3.kp`), "stood up trembling" plays it backward from wherever it is; the code bow remains only as a stand-in until the pack loads. The video drew a thin ground line: wiped from the frames before cutting (by the line's fitted slope, only where nothing of him is above it). For later: the Cross (Level 5) needs him WITHOUT the burden once it falls: paint the burden out of these frames as the book was (free), or a second video then. Still with a book or a pouch at the belt, left as they are: the plain run and the lift (it reads as a pouch). Nothing spent.

**Build 200: THE MIRE RECOMPOSED (owner: "we need to spend money on some animations for when they are in slough - it just doesn't look right - think very carefully about composition maybe before spending $"; my diagnosis and plan, agreed: "Yes please").** Found by filming the level tick by tick: (1) the struggle was a twentieth of the picture's height, jammed in the bottom-left corner on the words, the right two-thirds empty; (2) every mire picture came from a video of a man NOT in mud (Pliable's climb is a puddle; Christian's struggle is cut at the waist by code), so the bodies have no weight: only new videos made in deep mud can fix that; (3) the mud was nearly the sky's own pale and read as mist; (4) the frame blending of build 186 left a ghost outline. **Step 1, free (this build):** the camera pushes in as they fall and holds close through Pliable's part (`z` 1.05, `cx` -740, `wl` 330: the men about a fifth of the picture, the home bank at the left edge, a band of mud between them and the words); wading, it follows him on the left third and draws back only over the last half to `z` .9, the oak's trunk and limb above him; "could not get out" is close under the oak (`ph === 'help' && !help`), and only Help's coming draws it back to the whole oak (`z` .6, kept); the mud a clear step darker (`tone` .64 / .5 / .3, was .86 / .7 / .44); the mud map starts a little left of the home bank and fades in over 260 units (a hard seam showed close up); each picture melts into the next only in its last third (`smooth(.65, 1, ...)`). **Step 2, waiting on the owner's yes (about $1.12):** two videos made in deep mud from the first frame (his book-free picture with a flat dark band of mud at his chest): (A) wading, a loop: leaning into the mud, shoulders heaving, arms shoving it aside, rising and dropping with each pulled step (also the River, Level 17); (B) stuck, heaving, then lifting his head and reaching up, ending on his current reaching picture so it joins Help's grip (also the River). Optional (C) Pliable heaving round and dragging himself out (about 56 cents; Level 2 only).

**Build 204: no grey discs under the men (owner: "The grey circles (shadows) below Christian and pliable don't look right").** On the darker mud of build 200, three things showed as grey shapes under each man: (1) the low mist lens round them (`low()`) reached below the surface as a pale disc: now clipped to above the surface line; (2) the churned dark patch under each struggle (`mudCh`, 44 units deep): now a faint shallow darkening (a third as strong, 16 units deep); (3) the rings stood up as tall ellipses: flattened (`ry` .08 of their width, was .17), lying on the surface as seen edge-on. And the mud map now starts where the water does (`MUD_X0` = `WATER_L`), all mud by the home bank with no mirror fading in (a paler column showed under Pliable). Nothing spent.

**Build 212: CHRISTIAN ALWAYS SOLID, MORE CONTRAST (owner: "it seems the sprites for Christian in 2 and 3 still have clear parts !! Can you make sure he's always opaque and bring in more contrast if necessary so he doesn't look too flat").** Four causes, all fixed, nothing spent: (1) **gaps open to the outside** (between arm and robe, along the hem), which `solidOf` cannot reach: every Christian pack (walk, stand, turn with and without the book, wade, reach, kneel, plain run, weep) and the lift were made solid once, offline (OpenCV: the outline closed by a 5 px disc, 3 px for the lift; everything inside filled, the new pixels painted from the robe round them; only more than 3.5 px inside the outline, so the soft edge stays soft; the script is not in the repo, the originals are in git before this build); `PACK_V` 28; (2) **the frame melting of build 186** laid the next picture over the last, so a moving arm showed twice, half see-through (Pliable's pale double outline): taken out for both men (`bk`, `blendK` 0); (3) **the right-hand mist veil in the bog** stood between us and Christian once the camera came closer (build 200): it now starts to his right (`v0`); (4) **contrast**: `solidOf` now spreads every figure's tones 1.3 times about its own middle and caps the lightest at a little above that middle, so the watercolour's paper-white patches no longer read as holes (Levels 2 and 3, all figures); among the rocks the fire's warm and cool light is laid with 'soft-light' (it was a flat wash that made him pale and ghostly), and the dark tint is a shade deeper ([70,62,70], was [78,72,82]).

**Build 214: TWO VIDEOS MADE IN THE MUD (owner said yes to A and B, build 212; about $1.12, both right first time).** Both from his book-free quiet standing picture with a flat band of mud (grey-brown, `#5c5650`) drawn across a 1280 x 720 white picture at his waist (the band's top at video y 447, his feet at 600, 122 pack pixels = 63 units above the feet). (A) `christian-wade.pack` (replaced the old, 48 pictures from video frames 28 to 122, a clean loop by picture difference): wading to the right, leaning in, shoulders heaving, the body rising and dropping with each pulled step, arms shoving into the mud; the model drew the band as ground and his robe over it, which does not matter as everything below the line is cut. (B) `christian-stuck.pack` (new, `STUCKP`, 120 pictures): chest-deep, heaving and straining on the spot (6..58, played back and forth), then lifting his head and his right arm, mud dripping from the sleeve (60..119). **The B video zoomed in slowly to 1.5 times over its first five seconds** (measured by matching the burden, which keeps its shape, frame to frame; anchored on the mud line) and was undone frame by frame before cutting. Each frame was cut at the band's top (everything below made white), then `tools/gesture-frames.py --height 361 --to 288`. In the game: `META.wade` {ax 85, ground 298}, `META.stuck` {ax 120, ground 299}; A while wading, B for every struggle on the spot (the fall, Pliable's quarrel, going back, stuck); **both are drawn with their own mud line on the surface (`wy` 64), since the videos carry their own depth** (the old sinking deeper as he crosses would have hidden him); the reach is B's 60 to 78, held with a breath at 76..80, placed at `HX - 110` where his hand meets Help's (`handPt`: 34 units above the mud); the old `christian-reach.pack` stays only as a stand-in. Pliable's frames are unchanged (the optional video C was not made). For later: the River (Level 17) can reuse both.

**Build 217 (owner: "Close to his hand in first pic seems like there's a notch cut out in red"; "The top of the rocks in the mountain scene have light lining - doesn't seem appropriate here"; "really dial in 'silver' sun lining in level 3 opening").** (1) **The notch** was real space between his hand dipping into the mud and his robe, right at the mud line, in the new wading video (and a few in the stuck one): both packs were made solid as in build 212 (treating the figure as going on below its bottom row) and then narrow gaps just above the mud line were filled as a low heap in the body's own tone, widest at the line (30 pack pixels) and gone 26 pixels up; `PACK_V` 30. (2) **The warm line along the crag tops** (build 191) is off (`edge = 0` in `crags()`): the fire is hidden behind them. (3) **THE SILVER LINING, Level 3's opening** (`figure()` in `hill.js`, "LIGHT ON THE FIGURE"): the old rim was the figure less itself moved 1.6 picture pixels, under one screen pixel, so it read as a faint smudge. Now a crisp line along the edges facing the sun and a little upward (the figure less itself moved 2.6 pixels toward the sun), the thin parts (hood fringe, hat brim, cane, ragged hems) glowing through at .4 (a pixel lit where every neighbour 2.2 pixels away in four directions is outside), in a silvery warm white strongest on the head and shoulders and fading to a trace at the feet, with a bloom at half strength. Nothing spent.

**Build 220: no cuts into him while he wades (owner: "His belly / hand is getting cut out when he walks leaving cut into his body - it looks bad").** In the new wading and stuck pictures his hand goes into the mud a little ahead of his body, so at the mud line the paper between hand and robe showed as a bite out of him. Now, in both packs, just above the mud line everything from his back to his hand is solid, tapering up quickly (a heap of mud pushed up round him: 22 pack pixels high, as wide as needed at the line), in the body's own tone, the soft edge pixels included so no pale seam shows; then made solid again as in build 212; `PACK_V` 31. `tools/gesture-frames.py` takes `--white N` now (paper lighter than N is cut away; default 232; 243 keeps a pale-lit robe when the paper is 250 or more). The scripts used (solidify, heap) are not in the repo. The hunched front under his chin and the dip between his burden and hood are his real outline and are kept. Nothing spent.

**Build 226: his stop repaired (owner: "when he stops Christian hand swing back behind his body and the sprite gets a cut out of his midsection - that's wrong").** Leftovers of painting the book out (build 189) in `christian-stand-nb.pack` (Levels 2 and 3): a V-shaped notch in the front of his robe at the waist in nearly every stopping and standing picture, black smudges where the swinging hand had been (pictures 12 to 20), and a faint see-through ghost of the hand round his outline. Repaired, free: soft pixels more than 2 pixels from the solid body cleared; the front edge closed wherever it dips in for up to 26 rows with robe above and below it (not in the bottom 60 rows, so not between hem and toes), painted from the robe round it; near-black smudges repainted the same way; every soft edge sharpened (alpha `(a - 90) * 255 / 140`), so no grey halo. `PACK_V` 32. The scripts are not in the repo.

**Build 230: every Christian pack checked (owner: "Can you check other levels with same sprite please").** A check script measured every picture of every pack of his (walk, stand, turn with and without the book, kneel, plain run, ears run, reach, weep, lift, wade, stuck) for notches in the front or back edge, see-through ghosts and black smudges, and the flagged pictures were looked at as dark silhouettes. Real faults found: his walk, with the book (Level 1) and without (Levels 2 and 3), had a bite or a small hole at the waist between his swinging hand and his robe in several pictures; repaired as in build 226 (front edge closed, edges sharpened), then made solid and pinholes filled; the book in Level 1 is untouched. Everything else flagged is his real shape (arms held out, hands clasped, the dip under the burden). `PACK_V` 33. Nothing spent.

**Build 259: MUD THAT LOOKS LIKE MUD, WET SHADOWS, SOFTER SHORES (owner: "bog mud seems to look good but I don't like that it's all grey underneath them - maybe we should put in some shadows or more bog look like the wavy edges closer to tree - it's too plain - also the dark shore on two sides is too thin and high contrast - maybe make it reflect or shadow down and maybe some mist to blend in").** (1) **The mud's own tones** (`mudDk`, `mudLt`, baked once beside `mudMap`, laid on the mud only with 'source-atop'): a dark wet rim round every pool, so the pools' wavy edges are drawn into the mud everywhere; broad flat patches of wetter mud; a few long thin bands of dull sheen, flat as seen edge-on; and near the home bank a few pools now open in the foreground (`poolT`: lower in the picture only, the mud right under the men stays mud). (2) **Wet shadows** (`shC`, drawn small and stretched, so soft): under everyone standing in the mud (every `figure()` drawn cut at the surface is recorded in `SHN` and drawn the next frame from `SH0`) and under the home bank (it stands on mud, so it had no mirror), the outline turned down, squashed (.42 for figures, 1.5 for the bank) and blurred, dark at the surface and gone about 58 units down. A dull smear in the body's own shape: not a mirror, not a disc. (3) **Mist along both shores** (`mistC`): one long flat layer on the water line, thickest over the bank's foot, faded in and out along each shore; round puffs were tried first and read as pale ovals (taken out). `hum` (the shores' swells) moved up beside `bankY`. Nothing spent.

**Build 271: DEEP IN THE MIRE, ONE CONTINUOUS REACH, AND FIVE CRITICS (owner: "He doesn't sink enough in the slough - when he reaches toward help, animation has to jump to put him at right level. I want Christian pretty far down - can you run through level with subagent critiques - looking at pure beauty, simplicity, cohesion, animation consistency, and storytelling").** Found by filming the far bank tick by tick (`tools/stills2.js` now takes `STEP=n` and writes `log.json` with each picture's phase, place and clocks, for filmstrips): (a) the reach cross-faded from his heaving picture to the old reach (a pale see-through double for half a second), and (b) the reach ended him upright, chest out, while the lift's first picture has him shoulder-deep with his arm raised high, so he dropped at the grip. **Now:** (1) the wading and stuck pictures are drawn deeper than their own mud line (`C2.md`: `MD_IN` 16 where they fall, going down as he wades to `MD_FAR` 28, eased), and 28 is exactly the lift's first picture's depth: found by matching the stuck video's frames to the lift's frame 0 (best: stuck frame 88, 77% overlap, `REACH_F`, at `REACH_X` = `HX - 82`); (2) the reach is ONE play of the stuck video from the picture he is in (`C2.rq0`, recorded from the heaving loop `C2.q`) to `REACH_F` (its high diagonal arm, out of the mud), at or below its own speed, then held breathing; no cross-fade; the lift follows on. Five critic subagents (beauty, simplicity, cohesion, animation, storytelling) then reviewed stills and filmstrips of the whole level; fixed where they agreed: (3) **pools** softer and fewer small islands (`poolN` less fine noise, a wider edge), so less "camouflage"; (4) **the mud map runs on under the far bank and fades** (`mudEnd`; it ended in a straight upright seam at `BANK0`); (5) **the mirror** in strips one picture-pixel high (two-pixel strips, each upright inside, made every slanting edge of the oak's reflection a staircase) with a slower, gentler wander; (6) **the horizon**: the far shore is a soft haze, not a band with a hard top (it read as a double line across the sky); (7) **Help steps out of the mist whole and dark**: the fog bank (`fogC`) is cut away softly from his back while he walks in (it lay over him, so he came in as a pale ghost on the trunk); (8) the home bank's pale footprint specks (`prints`) are no longer drawn; (9) the plain's sun has a soft edge. **Proposed to the owner, not done (their words or staging):** cut Help's "What are you doing there?" and Christian's "Sir, a man called Evangelist sent me ..." (seven lines before the act); mirror Christian in the quarrel so the two face each other; lay the lights flat across the mud as visible steps, with one wide look from bank to bank; time Pliable walking off into the mist on "away he went"; the far light's shafts in the ending, which cross the trunk where Christian walks past it (the owner asked for them in build 124). Nothing spent.

**Build 361: THE LIFT, NO BLOCK BETWEEN THE TWO MEN (owner, in the Level 4 videos chat: "when help pulls christian out of water, i think i saw a clipping isse").** Two causes, both fixed, nothing spent: (1) build 212's making-solid of `lift.pack` had closed the outline round BOTH men where their hands join and filled the open air between them (7 to 9 thousand pixels in pictures 42 to 51, smaller fills in 13, 14, 55, 60 to 62, 69) with a grey smear that hid Christian's knee and Help's front leg: those areas were put back from the pack as it was before build 212 (git `fd685e6`; only added areas over 150 px, so the small filled paper spots stay filled); `PACK_V` 38; (2) in the game `solidOf` (in `index.html`, used by Levels 2 and 3) filled the same gap again, because the hands above and the strip of ground below shut it in: it now leaves any shut-in clear space larger than 2500 pixels clear (open air, not a spot of white paper). **Rule: a pack with TWO figures must never be made solid as one outline; a large space shut in between figures is air.** Checked by stills of the lift before and after (`tools/stills2.js ... "s.lift > 76" 8 1 bog`, `STEP=12`).

**Build 362: EVERY PACK CHECKED FOR FILLED-IN GAPS (owner: "Can you check other animations through out game").** `tools/pack-check.py` (new) went through all 28 packs frame by frame (fills against the packs before build 212, shut-in holes, ghosts, pale edges, jumps); the flagged frames were looked at on red. Found, the same fault as the lift: (1) build 212's making-solid had also filled the open air BETWEEN CHRISTIAN'S LEGS wherever the feet closed the gap below the hem: his walk with and without the book (8 and 7 pictures), his turn (11 and 9), the kneel (2), one standing picture, and the gap behind the back leg in three pictures of his plain run; put back from the packs before build 212 (only added areas over 150 px in the lowest third of the figure; the waist repairs of builds 226 and 230 are untouched); `PACK_V` 39; (2) in Levels 2 and 3 `solidOf` filled every shut-in gap up to 2500 px, so Evangelist had a web between his boots and between hand and staff, and Help, Pliable and Wiseman between their legs in some pictures: measured, the true white-paper spots are all under 30 px and every larger hole is air, so `solidOf` now fills only spots up to 50 px. Checked and left: pale edges in Level 1's `christian-stand`, `evangelist`, `obstinate`, `pliable` (the watercolour's own light outline, every frame alike, not seen as a fault); soft pixels round `pliable-climb` (its painted puddle rings); no jumps in any pack. **Seen and NOT fixed:** with the skip link `?level=3&at=evangelist` the very first frame shows a white cloudy patch in the middle of the rocks (in older builds too; not seen in a play-through). Nothing spent.

**Build 365: QUICK TURNS AND THE STEPS SHOWN (owner: "it'd be nice for help turning to be a quick turn and go instead of drawn out slow turn with feet walking look (don't like that - much prefer a pivot on one foot or something quick). (2) the stepping stones don't show up any more - how does this fit with story in the book? (3) christian also turns slow").** (1) **Help's turn** (`help-turn.pack`, 80 frames) played at .2 a tick took nearly seven seconds, his feet walking round; now it plays through in `HTURN_TICKS` (44 ticks, under a second), eased in and out, and his walk takes over at once (`lv.p`). (2) **Christian's turn in Level 2** (`turnC`, `C2.tv.p`) is played through in `TURN_TICKS` (50 ticks), eased, at every turn in this level (going back with Pliable, looking back at the steps, turning to go on): the owner's own exception to "every clip at its own video speed". Level 1's turn on "Do you see that wicket gate?" is unchanged. (3) **The steps:** the stones were no longer drawn anywhere (only the mist over them thinned, and the lights stopped in the air above them). Now each is a low flat stone at the surface (`steps`, drawn in the near picture so it mirrors where there is water), hidden until its light finds it; the light settles ON its stone (`ty` = the stone's top) and leaves a warm edge along its top that fades a little. In the book ({33}, {35}) the steps are real, "good and substantial ... through the very midst of this slough", laid by the King's labourers, but hard to see in foul weather, and Christian never looked for them: "Fear followed me so hard that I fled the next way, and fell in." The lights finding them are our picture (listed in the departures). Nothing spent.

**Build 368: LESS WAITING IN THE SLOUGH, STORY MOVES INSTEAD OF GAME (owner, from `level2-cuts.html`: "Yes do your changes - your can shorten walking path too - and speed up going across mire. Less game and more intentional story building moves").** (1) **Words:** four lines cut (the storyteller's "went talking over the plain", "Just as they ended this talk ... a very miry bog", Help's "What are you doing there?" and Christian's "Sir, a man called Evangelist ..."); ten reworded shorter and plainer (Pliable's opening question, "We shall shine like the sun", "I cannot go fast", "they fell into the bog", Pliable's "You can have your fine country all to yourself!", "He struggled out on his own side", "So Christian was left alone.", "But at the far side he could not get out ...", Help's "Why did you not look for the steps?", the steps' line). The first three are `LEVEL2_OPENING` in `index.html` (Level 2 only). Ten new recordings, all first takes (about 10 cents); `VOICE_V` 9. (2) **Shorter walks:** `START` -2100 (was -2750; the four opening lines at `OPEN_AT`, the first said as the level opens), `END_X` 470 (was 660); the plain's gold and mist follow `PLAIN_GOLD`/`PLAIN_MIST` (shared with the `?at=bog` skip). (3) **A quicker crossing:** .9 a tick (was .55: about 9 s of holding, was 14; the tune still has a note a footfall), and the far bank's strain gives way sooner (`strain > 120 || slipT > 220`, was 230 / 420). (4) **Story moves** (ideas C, D, E; A, tapping to heave, and B, touching the lights, were left out as too game-like): (E) in the quarrel Christian faces Pliable (`heave(-1)` at the start of `pli`: the picture mirrored under a splash and a ring, as Pliable's heave round), and heaves back toward the far bank when he struggles on (`wadeOn`); (D) the choice waits until Pliable has climbed out and gone into the mist (`pliGo`, at most 15 s), and as he goes the camera comes in close on Christian alone (`z` 1.2); the choice now reads "Go back, as Pliable did"; (C) while the lights find the steps the camera draws back from bank to bank (`cx` -480, `z` .62), so all seven stones are seen across the bog. Nothing else spent.

**Build 370: THREE THOUGHTS (owner, of my offer of flashbacks and flash forwards: "Yes sounds great - it should be like what he sees in his mind - thoughts - the shapes in the bog can look a little like monsters or live blobs - very subtle but alive looking").** Each is baked once, small and blurred (`thoughts()`, `THK`; `soft()` averages 16 offset copies, `vign()` fades every edge like a memory), comes by itself, lingers and goes; no words added. (1) **The place he tells of** (a flash forward): on Christian's "We shall shine like the sun" (`cityT`, from the caption of `LEVEL2_OPENING[1]`) a far city on a hill with a tall spire (its five shining figures were taken out in build 372) stands faintly in the gold sky over the far light, wavering, a second fainter copy drifting over it, for about 12 s, gone before the mist (the book, `{27}`, `{28}`). (2) **Home** (a flashback, ours): while the choice is offered (`homeA`, `ph` 'pli' and `G.state` 'choice'), his cottage shows far off in the mist on the left, only a faint roof and wall, the door lit, his wife's dark shape in it, its foot lost in the mist; it fades once he has chosen. (3) **The fears** (`fears()`, `FEARS_AT`, `fearT2` from the FEARS caption, about 15 s, wading or stuck): five dark soft shapes heave up out of the mud round him, each six lobes that swell and turn, now and then two dim eyes opening and closing; drawn before him (never between us and him), cut at the surface. `SL.peek().thk` gives `cityT`, `homeA`, `fearT2`. **Tried in this build and changed:** hard-edged first drafts (the shining ones read as cones, the house as a board-game piece, the fears as smudges); the house is now mostly its lit door. Nothing spent.

**Build 371: the fears as bulges of mud, home taken out, a slower crossing, Pliable out sooner (owner: "The shapes need to rise up as bulges from bog. Didn't like home-please remove. He [flits] a little fast across. Can you make pliable get out faster. A little less wiggling-more struggle once then rise out").** (1) **The fears** (`fears()`) are now the mud itself swelling up: a low mound with spreading flanks and a rounded crown, its outline lumpy and moving as if something breathes under it, in the mud's own tones (lighter on top), a faint wet sheen along the crown, its edge softened by five slightly larger, fainter passes; it heaves up and sinks back slowly, and now and then two dim eyes open near its top. (2) **Home in the mist** (build 370's second thought) is gone (`homeA`, the cottage bake). (3) **The crossing** .7 a tick (was .9 in build 368; about 11 s of holding). (4) **Pliable:** less wriggling while they wallow (his struggle loop at .05 + .09 of a burst, was .06 + .14), and once he has said his piece he struggles back once and rises straight out (the struggle back 1 a tick, was .32; the climb .34 a tick, was .2): from his line to out of the mire about 6.6 s (was 10.5). Nothing spent.

**Build 372: the city alone (owner: "The city of light is great - light figures please remove").** The five shining figures round the city in the sky (build 370) are taken out; the city on its hill, its spire and its glow stay as they were. Nothing spent.

**Build 376: no hymn on the steps (owner's rule, passed on by the Level 3 chat: don't overplay Amazing Grace, it is saved to come whole and in tune at the Cross; owner: "ok sounds good").** The lights settling on the steps no longer sing the hymn's last phrase in the major: each rings one soft tone of a single rising G major chord (`LAST`). The minor wading notes stay. Nothing spent.

**Build 377: THE FEARS RISE IN PLACE, ONE AFTER ANOTHER (owner: "the bumps rising up should be just from map area and stay in the same place horizontally just come up - what's a particularly intense way to do this that's not too detailed").** Each bulge is fixed at its own place in the mire (`FEARS_AT`: world x -880, -330, -770, -470, -620, between the banks; they no longer follow him) and only rises straight up and sinks back (`fearRise`). The intensity is in the timing, not in detail: they come one after another about every 1.2 s from the FEARS line (`s` 20 to 300 ticks), each a little bigger, the last and biggest (1.5) just behind him; each lands with a deep low thud and a ring and a heave of the mud's surface (`fPush`), and the whole picture darkens for a breath as it swells (`FP`, a .2 dark wash). Once risen each breathes and two dim eyes open and close; they sink within about 6 s, and quickly once Help comes (`fearHelpT`). Nothing spent.

**Build 380: THE FEARS AS DARKNESS AND DRUMS, AND QUIET BETWEEN THE STORYTELLER'S LINES (owner: "ok remove bumps but darken whole scene dramatically - that would make it intense, some drum beats ... make sure there's a pause between narrator 's talking so things space out nicely").** (1) The bulges of mud (builds 371, 377: `FEARS_AT`, `fears()`, their rings and heaves) are gone. On the FEARS line the whole picture sinks into the dark over about 1.5 s (`fearDark()`, `FP`: a radial wash of `DARK`, .9 at the edges, .74 a little out, .5 round Christian so he still reads), holds while eleven slow low drum beats come in heartbeat pairs, louder and closer together (`DRUMS`, `drum()`: two falling sweeps about 80 to 40 Hz and a dull low thump of noise), the dark swelling a breath at each beat (`beatT`), then lifts over 3 s from `FEAR_END` (560 ticks), or within about a second once Help comes (`fearHelpT`). (2) **Quiet between lines:** `quiet(n)` = nobody has spoken for n ticks (`quietT`, set while `saying()` or a talk is on). FEARS waits for 110 quiet ticks after "So Christian was left alone." (and may come from a third of the way across); STUCK waits until the drums have played and the dark has begun to lift, then 90 quiet ticks; "Then a man came to him, whose name was Help." waits 75 ticks after STUCK (`helpSayT`). Headless, timed by reading: about 1.2 to 1.8 s of quiet between each. Nothing spent. **I cannot hear the drums: the owner's ears decide their weight.**
