**Build 384: DARK MIST, NOT A CIRCLE OF LIGHT (owner: "don't like the circle of light around him - the darkness can be a mist around perimeter of scene and then as a mist layer over parts of scene"; then "yes do it").** Build 380's radial wash (lighter round him) is gone. Now (`darkMist()`, baked once, small so it is soft when stretched): an even dimming (.34 of `DARK` at full); two ragged frames of dark mist (about 150 soft blobs each, thickest in the corners and along the top), drifting slowly against each other and creeping in a little at each drum beat; and one low band of dark mist (wrapping sideways, drifting) lying flat across the far mire well behind him (from `wl - 190 z`, drawn before the figures in the mire, so never over him); he reads dark against the paler near mire. Tried in this build: the band right behind him (from `wl - 118 z`) swallowed him. Nothing spent.

**Build 387: PLIABLE STANDS AT THE START, CLIMBS OUT SMOOTHLY, NO SLAB AT THE BANK (owner: "when i first start level 2, pliable tries to walk as pilgrim stays still and it looks funny ... pliable exiting bog looks jerky and mud against land looks funny").** (1) At the level's start Pliable's stand (`pli.stop`) was dropped at once (Christian had not yet stopped, so `halt` was false) and he walked straight back into his stop video; now he leaves his stand only once Christian has really moved (`pli.stop.cx`). (2) **His climb out:** it eases in from the struggle and plays a little slower while he stands up out of the mud (`pli.cv`: .24 there, .34 elsewhere); the code's lift onto the bank is spread wider (`smooth(.6, .93, p)`, was .68 to .85); and while each picture of the clip is shown he slides toward the next picture's height (`plTop(f)`, the top of each frame measured once; `slide` in `pliMire`), since the clip rises up to 7 px between pictures: measured, the head's largest jump per tick is now 3 px (was 8); once out he sets off from standing (`pli.ov`) instead of at full pace. (3) The heaped mud surface (`bandC`) ended in a flat grey slab with a hard square edge against the home bank where he climbed: it now fades out over 58 units from each bank. Nothing spent.

**Build 388: HELP COMES OUT OF THE MIST CLOSE BY (owner: "can help appear out of the fog and then a very short walk then kneel ... since the long walk doesn't look so good. maybe he's doesn't walk quite so fast").** He no longer walks three extra strides in from the picture's edge: he starts at his walk video's own first frame (`HW_START` 48 in `HSEQ`: frame 32, about 360 units from where he kneels, was about 690), in the mist right of the oak (the fog bank moved 180 nearer, `X(HX + 820)`), fades into sight out of it (`help.a` +.022 a tick, about 3/4 s), and walks at his video's own pace (`help.w` .2 a tick, was .3, build 121's "half as fast again"); the kneel is unchanged. Tried: starting nearer (frames 52, 44) made him appear out of the oak's trunk. `SL.peek().hw` gives his place in the walk for tests. Nothing spent.

**Build 391: LEVEL 2'S WORDS CUT AGAIN, 18 LINES TO 12 (owner: "let's shorten dialogue (way too 'busy')"; chosen in `level2-cuts.html`, round 2: "everything else as proposed (0 changes)").** Measured from the recordings, the main way's speech is about 0:47 (was 1:21). **Cut** (the picture shows each): Pliable's "I'm glad to hear it! Come on, let's walk faster!" and Christian's "I cannot go fast, because of this burden on my back." (`LEVEL2_OPENING` in `index.html` is two lines now; `OPEN_AT` two places, the second at -1820); Christian's "Truly, I do not know."; the storyteller's "He struggled out on his own side, and Christian saw him no more." (Pliable's climb now starts as the talk ends, in `talk(FALL, ...)`); on the go-back path "Christian turned back toward his own house. The mist grew thick and dark."; "So Christian was left alone." (`ALONE` empty); "There were good steps through the middle of the bog, but in the mist they were hard to see." (`STEPS` empty). **Shortened** (four new recordings, all first takes, about 4 cents; `VOICE_V` 10): "They fell into the bog. Its name was Despond."; Pliable's "You can have your fine country all to yourself!"; "He could not get out, because of the burden on his back."; "Fear followed me so hard that I fell in." Kept: the shining-like-the-sun line (the city in the sky), the fears line (the dark and the drums), Help named, his question, "Give me your hand", "Go on your way", and on the go-back path "No. I must struggle on, toward the gate." **Departures added:** the steps are no longer spoken of after Help's question; Christian's answer to Help shortened further. Spent: about 4 cents.

**Build 395: THE MIRE'S LOOK, FIRST PASS (owner, of my look review: "Let's do it" to A, B and C).** (A) Two faults: (1) the veil right of Christian (`look.veil`) was a pale upright band with a hard left edge laid down over the near mud too; it now comes in over a long soft stretch (`v0` to `v1`, at least 620 wide) and thins away below the water line (made on a small canvas `VEILC`, 96 x 54, stretched, so its edges are soft); (2) as each man fell in, his walking picture and his struggling picture were both shown half see-through for a moment (a pale double of Pliable): now one swaps for the other at once under the splash (`wk >= .5`), for both men. (B) A full range of tone: the near mire darkens toward the foot of the picture (`nearDk`, eased in during the bog phases, laid before the figures in the mire, up to .58 of `DARK`); the far light brighter in the mist (`disc` .55, was .3); the far mist over the horizon a little thinner and one soft pale line along the horizon. (C) The oak kept: less veil in the fall and the quarrel (.5, was .84) and the camera a little right there (`cx` -680, was -740), so the oak's long limb reaches in over them. Nothing spent.

**Build 396: THE MIRE'S LOOK, SECOND PASS (owner: "Let's do it" to D to G).** (D) **Calmer mud:** in the bog phases the gaps between the rows of mud (many small pale dashes of water) are mostly filled over the middle and near mire, more toward the foot of the picture (`MCOV`, a small mask stretched, fading out from `X(-440)` to `X(-180)` so the still water under the oak keeps its mirror), and the old pools' dark rims and wet patches (`mudDk`, baked since build 257 but unused) lie on it as broad dark wet ground, both by `nearDk`, which now eases by the game clock (`ndT`), not per drawn frame. (E) **The dark mist** of the fears (`darkMist()`) is blurred once as it is baked (`soft4`: shrunk to a quarter and drawn back up, twice), so its blobs no longer show as streaky bands. (F) **The steps** (a neat row of even ovals each with a light on top read as runway lights): every stone a different width, height and gap, its top in two low humps, a small stone beside about half of them (`st.sub`), and a settled light melts into its stone (smaller and fainter, `fl`) with a soft flat warmth on the stone instead of a gold line. (G) **The plain a third shorter:** `START` -1740 (was -2100), the second opening line at -1590, the gold and mist moved in with it (`PLAIN_GOLD`, `PLAIN_MIST`); the plain's veil is .5 like the bog's (build 395). The wide look across the bog after "Go on your way" was already there (build 368's (C)). `SL.peek().nd` gives `nearDk`. Nothing spent.

**Build 398: THE DARK STAYS UNTIL HELP COMES (owner: "The darkness should come on slowly and leave only when help arrives").** `fearDark()`: the dark gathers over about six seconds from the FEARS line (`smooth(0, 360, fearT2)`, was 90 ticks), no longer lifts by the clock (`FEAR_END` now only marks the drums being done, which STUCK still waits for), and lifts only once Help is on his way, quickly at first so he is seen stepping out of the mist (`(1 - t / 110)^3`), gone in about two seconds. `fearHelpT` is now set in the update, not in the drawing (it waited for a picture to be drawn). Nothing spent.

**Build 406: A SLOWER CROSSING, THE DARK GATHERING AS THINGS GET WORSE (owner: "draw out his movement across slough a tad and have the darkness come on more and more slowly (like things getting worse)").** (1) He wades .58 a tick slowing to .42 by the far bank (was .7 all the way; about 15 s of holding, was 11), as the going gets heavier. (2) The dark begins before the fears line: while he wades it creeps in with how far he has come (`preDk`, up to .4 of the full dark by the far bank, eased), then from the FEARS line it deepens over about 10 s (`smooth(0, 600, fearT2)^1.6`: slow at first, quickening), never lighter than it already was; it still lifts only when Help comes. Nothing spent.

**Build 409: TWO BOOK LINES FOR THE LONGER CROSSING (owner, of my three offered: "How about 1 and 3"; about 2 cents of voices).** The storyteller says "But still he tried to struggle on, toward the side nearest the gate." ({31}) a tenth of the way across after a quiet breath (`STRUGGLE`, `struggleSaid`); the fears line now waits for it and comes from .38 of the way; then, five seconds or more into the dark and after another quiet breath, "It is the Slough of Despond still." (`STILL`, `stillSaid`; {34}, where Help says it to the dreamer), said near the far bank or once he is stuck; "He could not get out ..." waits for it. Both recorded first take; `VOICE_V` 14. **Departure:** the {34} line given to the storyteller. Headless, timed by reading: struggle, fears, still, stuck about 9, 9 and 6 s apart. Spent: about 2 cents.

**Build 417: HELP TELLS OF THE SLOUGH'S HISTORY, SEEN AS MEMORIES IN THE MIRE (owner: "When help explains the history of the slew can we do a flash to the efforts of the king and other travelers interaction with it - history etc"; chose all three lines; about 5 cents of voices, two second takes).** After the lift, on the far bank, Help speaks before "Go on your way" (new phase `hist`, the camera drawn back to see the home bank): "It is not the King's wish that this place stay so bad. His labourers have worked here for sixteen hundred years." and "Twenty thousand cart-loads of good teaching have been swallowed up here." ({34}, `HIST`), while dim grey ghosts of the King's labourers (Help's own walk, `ghost()`) come one after another along the home bank behind hand-carts (`cart()`), tip their loads into the mire at the edge, and each load sinks with a slow ring (`memories()`, `histT`, `histA`). Then he turns and looks back as the lights find the steps, and Help says "The King laid good steps through the midst. But in foul weather men step beside them, and sink." ({35}, `STEPS`), while three ghost travellers (Christian's and Pliable's walks) wade among the stones, step beside them and sink (`travT`, `travA`); then "Go on your way" (`onway` now comes after `look`), and Help turns and goes back into the mist. The ghosts are each figure's own picture in one dim grey ([104,98,94], `MEM`; pale ones did not read against the pale mist), kept at half size and laid twice, a little off, wavering, cut at the mud's surface. `SL.peek().mem` for tests. **Departures:** Help tells Christian what in the book he tells the dreamer ({33} to {35}), and before bidding him go, not after; the labourers, carts and travellers seen are ours; "the scum and filth that attends conviction" and the surveyors left out; Help's steps line made short. `VOICE_V` 15. Spent: about 5 cents.

**Build 418: GIANT WAGONS FILL THE SLOUGH (owner, of build 417's hand-carts: "Need giant carts to fill slough").** The labourers' memory is now a steady line of great wagons (`cart()`: a deep plank box heaped with earth, two great wheels, a long shaft; about two men tall and two and a half long; one silhouette filled once, faint plank lines and spokes), one every 7 s, each with a team of three labourers walking alongside (nearer than the wagon); at the edge it tips forward about its front wheel (.5 rad), a stream of earth and tumbling clods pours from its lip into the mire, the heap spreads wide across the mud and slowly sinks, and the empty wagon fades as the next comes up. The camera for this (`hist`) stands at `cx` -560 (further left shows the plain's dark edge where the mire begins). Nothing spent.

**Build 421: SOME DARK KEPT UNTIL HELP REACHES OUT; CHRISTIAN REACHES ONLY AFTER HELP DOES (owner: "Can you lighten darkness a tad when help arrives but keep some darkness till he reaches out - also Christian doesn't reach for help till help reaches for him").** (1) In `fearDark()` the dark lifts as Help comes out of the mist only to `HELP_KEEP` (.62 of itself), and the rest goes as Help's hand goes out after "Give me your hand" (`1 - smooth(82, 150, mv.t)`, about 3 s). (2) Christian's reach (`C2.rt`) begins only once Help's hand is most of the way out (`mv.t >= 130`), at the stuck video's own pace; it used to begin with Help's. Nothing spent.

**Build 453: CHRISTIAN WAITS UPRIGHT, BREATHING; HE REACHES ONLY WHEN HELP'S HAND IS TAKEN (owner: "he shouldn't reach out his hand till after help says and then it can just be the animation (the still arm/hand seems a little long) - while he's waiting for help and also talking with help can we make his head move a little to suggest he's breathing and then later talking").** (1) Once Help is on his way, Christian stops heaving: the stuck video plays on forward at its own pace to frame 66 (chest-deep, upright, arms down, looking toward Help) and holds there, easing between frames 66 and 72 as he breathes (`C2.hf`, `C2.hb`). (2) Help's hand can be taken only once it is out AND "Give me your hand" has finished being said (`helpSaid()`, from the recording's length; `handReady()`); only then does the "take his hand" prompt show. The touch (`grip()`) starts his reach (`C2.reachGo`): the stuck video plays on to `REACH_F` at its own pace, about a second and a half, and the lift begins the moment his arm is up (`liftGo()`): no still pose with the arm held out. (3) **His head moves** in those calm frames (62 to 80): the hood and head (right of pack x 76, above y 86) turn a hair about the neck (98, 88) as he breathes, and with the rise and fall of his own voice while he says "Fear followed me so hard that I fell in." (`HEADM` in `figure()`: the body drawn with a few rows of overlap under the head, so no seam). Fades out as he reaches. Checked: the playtest reaches the end card; stills before and after; no seam at the neck at twice the size. Nothing spent. **I cannot watch motion: the owner's eyes decide the size of the head's movement** (`-.022` for breath, `3.2` / `1.4` for the voice, in `drawChr`).

**The shores: clean lines, gently contoured (builds 152, 155; owner: "the shore on the right side looks a little flat and unnatural").** Build 152 added grass, reeds, clods and a crumbled edge; the owner: "The grass makes it too busy - I liked old clean lines - I meant right shore was just too flat (vs contoured slightly)". So all of that is gone again: the land is one clean dark silhouette; beyond where people stand it swells and dips in a few long, low rises (`hum`, in the `SHORE` block after the oak, drawn over the ground, which is unchanged: `bankY` still gives level ground to stand on), and the oak's trunk flares smoothly into the ground. **Rule: keep this level's shapes as clean silhouettes; no small busy detail (grass, clods, roots).** Nothing spent.

## Level 3, Mr. Worldly Wiseman and the hill (`hill.js`, object `HL`): played and liked by the owner ("Looks fantastic", build 238); see `HANDOFF.md`, "HANDOVER FROM THE LEVEL 3 ART CHAT", for its present shape

**The picture (agreed: John Martin for the hill, Rembrandt for the gate).** The owner on the hill (build 139): "I like hill - stunning ... lots of amazing potential here"; keep its breaking-wave profile. The camera draws back until Christian is small beneath it; the far light is hidden behind the hill while he stands there.

**THE SUMMIT THE OWNER CHOSE (build 154, sending a phone picture of build 146: "This seemed to capture it better - something about your current plum[e]s is off"): ONE small soft light at the peak, no flame shapes at all, and the smoke rising straight out of it as a full dark billow lit from beneath, with a few sparks; the cloud canopy of build 151 stays. Tried and taken out (do not bring back): tall golden flames (builds 148 to 150: they made the mountain look small), five small points of fire with a thread of smoke (build 153: candles and a chimney). What follows about strands of flame describes those removed tries.** **LINES OF FIRE (owner, build 157: "The lava lines were nice - more of those and glowing. Not a fan of new reference image"): six lines of fire run down the rock from the one light at the peak, wandering, branching, thinning, each with its own slow pulse (`SEAMS`, built by `run()` in `build()`; hair-thin gold core, a soft amber glow, a little red at the edge). The second reference study was NOT liked: do not rebuild the plume from it; its notes follow only as a record.** **What that study showed:** the smoke is ONE continuous ribbon, not scattered puffs: narrow, dense and nearly opaque where it leaves the peak, with tight small billows; it widens steadily and its billows grow as it travels; its upper edge is crisp and curling, its lower edge soft; it thins to nothing at the far end; it is a mid grey, a little warm, lit only at its very foot. The fire is one tiny flame. The sky is calm and clean (the plume is the only event in it). The rock is a pure black shape, and one cleft in its face glows from inside (fire seen through an opening), which reads better than seams drawn on the surface. **SCALE RULE (owner, build 153: "Fire makes mountain look smaller - really want to not downplay grandeur of this massive rock - should the plumes be smaller because they are far away and almost like small cracks in the peak"): anything drawn ON a great thing is a measure of it. The fire on the hill is a few SMALL points in clefts at the peak, hair-thin seams, tiny sparks, and threads of smoke that only grow as they drift away; the fire's greatness is carried by the wide glow behind the hill and the light in the cloud, never by big flames. (The sizes of flames given below were made small in this build.)** **The hill's fire: RESTRAINT (owner, build 145: the reference painting "seems pretty intense - ai can go overboard ... I want it more subtle and nuanced and beautiful and awe inspiring in its balance and restraint, like a good artist, not video game punch in the gut"; earlier: "flame feels toyish, not sure about veins").** The fire itself is hardly seen; what is seen is its light (`draw()`, all scaled by `burn` = how near he is; `fire()` adds a flash `F`, which only makes these brighter): (1) a wide amber-rose glow BEHIND the summit, so the hill is a black cut-out; (2) **a real fire on the summit, in GOLD** (owner, build 148: "I do want more fire that would be nice - just not the intense video game red"): a soft bright heart and five strands of flame of different heights, each a chain of soft lights along its own wavering line (`flames`; no hard-edged tongues), leaning with the wind, drawn IN FRONT of the smoke's base (the smoke painted over them at first and they read as two dots); white-gold, gold, amber, and only a little red at the flames' outer edge (owner, build 150: "Little red is ok just not the crazy of tha[t] concept art. Maybe a little more": the strands were made taller, about a fifth of the hill's height); (3) ONE seam of fire (`SEAMS`, two strands from the same source) running down inside the overhang, thinning and dying away, breathing slowly; (4) a faint lit edge on the overhanging face; (5) **the smoke in the sky** (owner, build 146, of the reference painting: "the red ... was too much but I did like smoke in sky - that was cool"): one great slow plume of soft round billows (`PUFF`, three shapes baked dark and again as underlight) that rises from the summit, bends with the wind and rolls away across the sky over his head, growing and melting away; warm grey-rose, the near billows lit amber from beneath, never red; the camera stands a little further back (`z` .4) so there is sky for it; (5b) **a canopy of cloud, the middle way** (owner, build 151, after pros and cons): the sky behind the hill's outline stays clear and glowing so the black shape reads; cloud gathers only in the top of the sky and on the far left, where the plume flows into it; dark billows (`PUFF`), those near the summit faintly lit from beneath, the lightning's light inside them; drawn before the hill; (6) a few slow sparks; (7) lightning as a pale light inside the cloud, the bolt barely drawn; (8) a soft sheen on the plain. **No hard-edged cartoon tongues, no scattered burning cracks, red only as a faint outer edge, no hard flashes. More fire is welcome; loudness is not.** The hill itself: `FACE` (the overhang, with a second lower one), `backX`, a nearer buttress a shade darker, fallen rocks at the foot. Ideas offered, not built: the fire dying down as Evangelist speaks of forgiveness; firelight on Christian; the pillar answering a touch.

**Mercy, after Rembrandt (build 159, built while the owner slept; not yet seen):** when Evangelist takes Christian by the right hand, a small warm light gathers on the two men and lies in a flat pool at their feet (`mercy`, `tintM`), the only warm thing under the black hill; when he says the man at the gate will receive him, the hill's fire sinks low and its flashes stop (`calm`). **Both are ours, not the book's** (in the book fire comes out of the mountain after the hand-taking, `{57}`, and that flash is kept; nothing says the fire sinks).

**The wicket-gate, after Rembrandt (build 142).** Owner on build 139: "I like light on gate - bricks not doing it for me - something about the light - door: not a fan - mountains ... their elevation compared to wall is distracting - need artistic analysis especially regarding composition - think Rembrandt". What was wrong and what is kept now: (1) three lights fought (far light, lantern, door): ONE at a time: a small lantern until the door opens, then the light from within, which is the far light he has followed (behind the wall it only glows over the top; no disc in the sky); (2) a low wall with the ridge just above it made two level lines: the wall is high and runs out of the picture both ways; (3) bricks, or stones each lit as a tile: the light is ONE soft pool on a rough surface, the big stones (`STONES`) only faint differences in it with dark joints; (4) the door is planks, iron straps and a ring in a deep arch, and opens only a hand's breadth on a soft-edged light; (5) the gate stands in the right third; lantern, face and doorstep make one slanting line. Evening has come ("in process of time": **a departure, the book gives no time of day**). **Rules for any lit scene: one light at a time; light is a soft pool on a surface, never a pattern of lit tiles; never stack two level lines; let most of the picture be dark.** Not yet seen by the owner.

**The opening (build 158; owner: "the tree at beginning needs redo - the color scheme too - like the green but we need beautiful and intriguing entry - think about the artist we are using - maybe a generated tree like slough but with leaves"; my proposal, agreed: "Let's go"):** John Martin's quiet side (*The Plains of Heaven*) before his terrible side: late golden afternoon (a sky from soft gold to a quiet blue-green, the field green-gold, the far hill pale violet-grey and harmless, a pale thread of smoke over it), and **a great oak in leaf where the ways cross**, grown by the Slough's own tree code with another seed (`SL.grow(27)`, `SL.ribbons`: exports added to `slough.js`, keep them; `bakeOak()`, `OAKL`): kin to the bare black oak of Level 2, but alive. **The owner on build 158's crown of big dabs in three greens: "That's an awful tree" (a cartoon). Build 160: ONE dark tone, as everywhere in the game: the oak is a silhouette like the Slough's, its leaves only a fine broken edge of small dabs in the same tone with sky showing through the crown, the low sun touching the far edge very faintly; the field and far hill were quietened too. Never paint a tree in several bright greens.** **Build 162 (owner, of the one-tone tree: "Tree bigger, leaves slightly sparser more varied, sunlight twinkling in leaves, think classic artistic rendering of tree from master painters - also color palette for opening scene can be tweaked to be more beautiful"):** the crown is painted as Claude Lorrain, Constable and Corot painted trees: many separate feathery pads at the ends of the boughs, each a cloud of small touches, sky between them and the limbs seen through; each pad dark beneath and a little lighter toward the low sun; pads differ in size and depth of tone; all close dark olives (still never bright green); a few hundred sunlit touches twinkle, each in its own time (`OAKL.tw`). The tree is bigger (`.88`). The palette is Claude's evening: gold at the horizon, peach above, a soft blue high up; a blue distance (ridge and far hill); a warm olive field. **THE PAINTED OAK (build 163; owner: "Getting better maybe adjust how trunk interacts with ground to be more realistic - more detail on trunk - can we have google image have a go at it?"):** one picture from the image model (about 15 cents, right first time), given a still of the code-drawn oak as its shape and asked for Claude Lorrain / Constable / Corot: a gnarled trunk with a hollow, roots spreading into rough grass, the long limb reaching right, feathery pads of leaves in dark olives. `assets/oak-leaf.webp` (cut from its white paper by distance from white, the paper's white taken out of the edge pixels), `OAK_META` (`ax`, `ground`, `k`, `tw` = sunlit leaves that twinkle), `loadOak()`, `OAKP`; toned a little darker in the game; the code-drawn oak (`bakeOak`) stands in until it loads. **Build 165 (owner: "Tone it down to slightly more silhouette, rerun to get whole tree"):** a second picture (about 15 cents, right first time) given the first and asked for the same tree whole, with white margin on all four sides (the first ran off its paper on the left); and the painting is pulled part of the way toward one dark tone (`OAK_SIL` .42: 0 = the painting, 1 = a flat silhouette), so the trunk's character and the leaf pads still read but it sits with the game's quiet shapes. **Build 166 (owner: "can you make it back into drawn tree using Google tree as template (remove that branch that sticks way off to the right) and made it slightly bigger so it doesn't interfere with heads of characters"):** the painting is now only the TEMPLATE. Its long right limb was taken out of the picture file; in `loadOak()` its outline is laid in as one dark tone with a faint trace of bark and roots (`OAK_SIL` .8), and the leaves are DRAWN over the crown in code: one small touch wherever the painting has a leaf (a jittered grid over its pixels), in close dark olives, lighter where the painting's leaf was sunlit; the lightest twinkle (`OAKP.tw`). (Build 168, owner: "fix cut lines in tree - they do not look good": where the limb was taken out, the picture file's crown now ends in scattered leaf-sized clumps instead of straight cuts. **When cutting anything out of a painted tree, never leave a straight edge through foliage: break it into leaf-sized clumps.**) **Build 170 (owner: "The tree roots need to reach brown ground or they didn't look right"):** the oak stands at the road's own edge now (drawn after the road, sliding with it, its foot a little below the road line), and the grass the painting put at its foot is turned to the road's dark earth, so the roots go down into the ground. Bigger (`OAK_META.k` .86, about 520 units tall) so the crown is well above the men's heads, and the camera stands a little further back on the plain and at the meeting (`z` .84 to .9) so the whole tree shows. a flat shade under it; a few leaves drifting down; an all-dark copy laid over it as the sky darkens. Its long limb reaches over the place where the two meet. Not yet seen by the owner.

**The hill is not shown at first (owner, build 167: "Maybe don't show mountain at first, just some foothills, the reveal will be cooler later"):** across the plain and the talk only a line of foothills stands in front of the far ridge (`reveal` 0); at "Do you see that hill?" the camera draws back and the hill, its village and its thread of smoke come out of the haze. **The hill far off looks like a great mountain across a plain (owner, build 169: "Having it blue like hills far away distant look right but I'm not sure what else to do? Brown? Maybe put some low fog to give it scale as it rises above. I'm thinking like mt Kilimanjaro"; my answer then was to keep it blue; **the owner, build 171: "I meant to say blue doesn't look right"**, so the hill is now a warm dusky brown-grey, rock in evening light, standing in front of the blue far ridges; the owner on the oak's roots in the road's earth: "roots look great now!!"):** deepest blue at the top, paling downward until its foot is lost in the plain's haze; a little warm light on the side toward the low sun; a long level band of low cloud drifting across its lower slopes, the mountain rising clear above it (`HILL[0]`'s gradient in `build()`; "LOW CLOUD ACROSS ITS FOOT" in `draw()`); the band thins away as the sky darkens. **ONE LIGHT, BEHIND EVERYTHING (owner, build 173: "Sun thru leaves would also be a nice touch. Rock has sun behind it. Should it cast shadow and be more dark? Think about any big picture things like this").** The low sun stands at the horizon in the middle of the picture, beyond the oak, the men and the hill, and the whole opening now follows from that: (1) everything between us and it is dark with a bright edge toward it: the oak, the people (`RIM`), and the far hill (a darker dusky mass, no light on its face, a bright rim down its sunward edge: `HILL[0]`); (2) the land is brightest far off where the light skims it and falls into shadow toward us, with a glare of haze along the horizon; the ridges are a warm violet-grey; the road is warm dark earth; (3) shadows lie on OUR side of things and are flat: the oak's shade, the hill's long shade on the plain; (4) the sun breaks through the oak's crown when it stands behind it: a small flare and a few soft spokes that turn as he walks (dapples of light in the shade were tried and read as stepping stones: taken out). (5) build 174 (owner: "I would like to see your other suggested changes"): the clouds are grey-violet above and bright along their lower edges (`CLOUDW` laid a little lower, `CLOUDG` over it), and the oak's trunk has its bright edge toward the sun, on whichever side the sun is (`OAKP.rimR`/`rimL`). **For every level: decide where the one light is, then make every tone, edge and shadow follow from it.** **The people against the low sun (owner, build 172: "Characters should be darker matching tree also maybe outline of glow from sun behind them"):** on the fair plain the figures are dark like the oak (`tint` fair [64,60,58]; they keep their watercolour detail faintly) and each has a thin warm edge on the side toward the far light (`RIM`, in `figure()`); the edge fades as the sky darkens and is off at the gate. **One light on everyone (owner, same build: "Characters are different tints: is that ok?"; my answer: no, the game's rule is one tint for all figures; Wiseman's warm tan was left over from when he was a stand-in):** Wiseman is now in nearly Christian's tone, only a little warmer (`wt`).

**Build 177: the owner's six points after playing it ("i like this level but don't love it"; all agreed as I proposed them; "do it here").** (1) **The light on the figures** ("the light around edges looks like a copy ... like i'm wearing 3d glasses"): the rim is now the figure's own outline, a hair thin, only where the edge faces the sun or the sky, strong on head and shoulders and gone by the knees, with a soft bloom (`figure()`, "LIGHT ON THE FIGURE"). (2) **The Wiseman talk acts itself out** ("lacks punch ... kinda drags"): a sweet, too-sweet tune under his voice (`sweet()`), the burden groans at "no more dangerous way" (`groan()`), and when he names the village it glows warm and small far off on the plain while the camera drifts to it (`vil`, `vilTo`); the village now stands at the hill's near foot ("the first house you come to is his") and fades as he comes close. (4) **The figures less flat**: each body is lit across, the sun side a little lighter and warmer, the far side a little cooler (in `figure()`), and the fair tint is a shade lighter ([72,66,62]). (5) **A painted mountain** ("the mountain cracks feel artificial. i need more realism"): one picture from the image model (about 15 cents, right first time) painted over the hill's own outline as its template, so the breaking-wave shape is kept: dark fractured rock with fire deep inside a few clefts. Cut into `assets/hill-rock.webp` (the rock) and `assets/hill-fire.webp` (only the fire); `paintHill()` tones the rock into the fair and dark hills, `LAVA`/`LAVAB` lay the fire on, restrained and breathing; the drawn seams and burning edge are gone once it loads. To redo: `HL.hillPics()` gives the outline for a new template. (6) **The road bends toward the mountain** ("i'd like for christian to get much closer to mountain and really feel the fire and rocks and trembling"): as he walks out of his way the hill slides more with the camera, grows to 1.35 times and its foot comes to the road (`appr`, `HL.appr`), until the overhang hangs over his head; beneath it stones and dust fall from the overhang (`ROCKS`, `shed()`, more at each flash), the ground trembles (a low rumble and a jolt at each flash, the picture drawn a hair larger so no edge shows) and he shivers (`trem` in `drawChr`); the camera stands at `z` .35 there so the summit stays in the picture. The tree (3) stays as it is.

**Build 181: THE MOUNTAIN'S SIZE, ONE RULE (owner: "when you first see the hill it kinda appears out of nowhere - it should be there already and to the right. hill compared to house is so big. but then when christian gets close it's no where near as big ... need to think deeply about good way to view enormity of hill but still have character control and visibility"; my proposal, agreed: "yes build it").** (1) **It is there from the start, veiled:** on the right from the first picture, a great dusky mass in the haze, its summit lost in cloud (`HILLV`, baked once in `paintHill()` at a quarter size so its edges are soft, its tones following the sky at each height, a trace of the rock showing through); at "Do you see that hill?" the veil lifts (`reveal`) and the rock and fire show. This replaces build 167's "foothills only". (2) **Its size only ever grows:** far off it is already huge (`HS` 1.6); the camera never zooms out to fit it while the child steers (Christian keeps his size, `z` .86 to .8), and the hill grows as he walks out of his way (`NEAR` 2.6 times at its foot) until it runs off the top of the picture and the overhang closes over him. (3) **The camera looks up:** beneath the overhang, while nothing is steered, the camera climbs the cliff to the burning summit, drifting toward the peak (`cam.lift`, `cam.liftX`, by `stillT`: up from 70 to 340, holds to 520, down by 770), then sinks back to him; Evangelist comes only after it is down (`stillT > 780`). (4) **One far wide shot, after John Martin:** at "Do you see that hill?" the camera draws far back (`z` .3): the two men tiny dark figures with their thin edge of light, the mountain filling the sky. The village is a fixed small size now (not scaled with the hill). **Rule: show a vast thing's size by what does not fit and by moving the camera along it, never by shrinking the person the child steers.** **Build 183 (owner: "Gotta increase resolution, no?" ... "Let's go!!"):** the painting was made again from the first one at the image model's highest size (`imageSize` '4K': 6336 x 2688; the same outline to 99.7%; one picture, roughly 25 cents), cut the same way into `hill-rock.webp` (3000 x 1280, twice the hill's units; 148 KB) and `hill-fire.webp` (1500 x 640); `paintHill()` keeps the rock at the picture's own size (`R`). The smoke and the summit's cloud keep the size they have from a distance (`kS`): stretched to the near hill they were one huge blur. **Lesson: a painted picture that the camera comes close to must be made at the size it is seen at its closest; ask for `imageSize` '4K' and keep it, and do not scale soft code-drawn shapes past the size they were made for.**

**Build 185: INTO THE ROCKS (owner: "i don't like that hill looks initially - it looks fake. let's hide it entirely till worldly wise tells him to look and then screen pans right ... pilgrim goes away from viewer and down the hill descending so his body disappear ... then scene shift to him walking a mountain path rocky ... when he reaches foot of mountain and narrator speaks we zoom out super wide ... then back to zoomed in where evangelist meets him"; then "we need a surrounding of rockiness, not just ground then big rock"; yes to the nearest crags passing in front of him). Replaces build 181's veiled hill, its growing hill and its camera climb (all gone).** (1) **Hidden until Wiseman points:** no hill on the plain; at "Do you see that hill?" the camera pans right (`HILL_F`, `z` .55) to it far off, small (`HSF`), hazed like a far mountain (`HILLV`, baked small from the dark rock and washed toward the sky's tones); the village stands on its own place on the plain (`VIL_W`). (2) **Over the brow:** past `BROW0` the road swells into a low rise (`crest`, peak at `CREST`); he walks up onto it and down the far side out of sight (`C3.dy`; the near ground drawn again over him), feet, body, head; then the picture goes dark (`cut`, phase `down`, automatic). (3) **The rocky path** (phase `path`, `PATH0` to `FOOT`): every layer is rock (`crags()`, outline from `crag()`: straight runs with sharp breaks, one tone a layer): far ridges (parallax .35), crags behind the path (.75), the stony path, the nearest crags along the bottom (1.3, in front of everyone, sometimes hiding his feet); they rise as he goes (low boulders and open sky first; at the foot only a strip of sky); edges facing the hidden fire ahead catch a thin warm line; the fire's glow in the sky ahead; holding does less and less; low rumbles. The mountain stands at its true size (`HSN` 8 times the far hill's units, its foot at `HX0`), hidden behind the crags but for the overhang above at the foot. (4) **The pull back** (phase `still`, `zs` 'out', 'hold', 'in', 'done'; `Zw` 0..1): at the foot the storyteller says HIGH and STOOD; the camera pulls back from him (`Z_NEAR` .8) to the whole mountain (`Z_WIDE` .085) in about 3.5 s, eased in the scale's logarithm (`ease5`); lightning as it settles; he is a tiny dark figure with a faint warm light round him; it holds until the fire's line is said, then comes back down to him (about 3 s) and Evangelist walks up. (5) **Back:** he hurries back along the path to `PATH0`, then the veil of mist to the gate as before. Skip links: `?level=3&at=hill` now starts on the rocky path; `at=evangelist` at the foot after the pull back. **Rule kept: the child never steers in a shot where he is too small to see; the wide shot is a moment, not a walk.** Nothing spent.

**Build 191: THE CRAGS ARE THE MOUNTAIN'S ROOTS (owner: "The crags might be a little sharp and too geometric - need a little more natural flow to them - also more cohesion with mountain"; my six points, agreed: "Yes please").** Each crag outline (`crag()`) is now a row of overlapping SPURS at uneven places, each a small copy of the mountain's breaking wave: a steep face on the left, a short flat brow, a long fall to the right broken by a ledge; tall spurs are wider, so no thumbs or horns; a broad slow swell runs under them and fine roughness on top. The layers are filled with the painted mountain's own rock (`ROCKTEX`/`ROCKPAT`: a piece from inside `hill-rock.webp`, made grey about a middle grey, mirrored into a seamless tile, laid over in 'overlay', moving with each layer) in its warm brown-black; only the highest faces turned toward the fire catch the warm edge (by height against the layer's envelope); at the foot the crags behind him run up into the mountain as one long slope of fallen rock (`scree`, about 560 units), which in the wide shot is the band of rock along the mountain's foot. **Tried in this build and taken out:** smooth domes (the fall after the top was a smoothstep), then spikes (the fall began at the top with no brow). Nothing spent.

**Build 192: a crisp edge on the mountain up close (owner: "The faded edge of the mountain in background is kinda bothering me").** Among the rocks the painting is shown about three times its own size, so its soft edge (with a pale rim the image model painted along it) read as a blurred halo. Now the overhanging face is drawn from the hill's own outline (`facePath`: a band 30 hill units wide inside `faceX`, in the rock's own tone, matched by sampling the picture) and the painting is clipped 2 units inside it; only the face, because elsewhere (the summit, the back) the painting's outline differs a little from the drawn one and clipping cut it. Nothing spent.

**Build 194: the figures solid on the fair plain (owner, through the small-tweaks chat: "I noticed pilgrim sprite is a little clear in third level - problem?").** Not transparency (every draw is at full alpha): low contrast. The fair tint was a mid grey-brown close to the olive field, and the across-light wash lightened the whole body. Now the fair tint is [50,46,44] (was [72,66,62]; the dark tint among the rocks stays [78,72,82], where a lighter figure reads against the black crags), the wash .07 / .09 (was .16 / .2); Wiseman's tone follows ([54,48,44] fair). The small pale patch at his shoulder is white paper in the original watercolour, not a leftover of the book's removal. Nothing spent.

**Build 198: THE CRACKS BREATHE (owner: "Should smoke come from multiple cracks (but not obstructing any of the view of the super cool mt)? I love how cracks light up so cool"; my proposal, agreed: "Yes").** Faint steam seeps from the four brightest cracks of the painted fire (`VENTS`, found in `hill-fire.webp`, hill units), not as columns (build 153's threads read as chimneys): wide soft breaths (`STEAMP`, `steamPuff`, two baked blobs `STEAMC`), warm amber as they leave the crack and grey as they rise and drift left with the wind; faint when young, at their strongest once lifted off the crack, gone within a short height; a flash makes every crack puff (`fire()`). They fade before the painting's own edge (`inRock`, from the rock picture's alpha at a fifth of its size, `ROCKA`; the drawn outline is narrower than the painting on the back side). **Tried and changed in this build:** bright round white glows piled up at each crack (drawn in 'screen', too many young puffs): now fewer, larger, fainter, rising further, laid on normally. Nothing spent.

**Build 202: the owner's eleven points ("alright some changes").** (1) **Rocks only round his path** ("the rock around him only need to be around that little area not extend to the whole base of the mountain. looks strange as a line"): every crag layer is faded by where it stands in the world (`tl` in `crags()`: from a little before `PATH0` to soon past the foot); the fallen-rock slope is shorter. (2) **More lightning, boulders in the background:** flashes every 1 to 2.5 s while the whole mountain is seen (3 to 6 s at the foot); rough boulders fall from high on the mountain right of the path (`boulder()`, from the rock picture's alpha `ROCKA`, never onto him), with a thin dust trail and a cloud where they land, more at each flash. (3) **The flicker going over the brow:** his feet sank into the near slope for a moment before the near ground was drawn over him, then were cut off at once; now the near ground covers him from the moment he passes the top and he goes down in one smooth curve (`C3.dy`). (4) **The kneel looked too big:** its video zoomed in about 1.2 times as he went down; `kneelK(frame)` undoes it (`FK`, a size factor in `figure()`; .12 from frame 0 to 30). (5) **See-through spots in Levels 2 and 3:** the figures were cut from white paper by whiteness, so white paper inside the watercolour was cut away too (2 to 7% of a figure); `solidOf(im)` in `index.html` fills, once per picture, every spot not joined to the outside through clear pixels with the figure's own middle tone, keeping a 4 px soft edge; used by `figure()` in `hill.js` and `slough.js` (Level 1 untouched). A pale ghost seen once beside him in Level 2's turn was a different moment of the turn, not the fill (checked: the same moment drawn with and without). (6) **Colour on him near the mountain:** `FIRE` in `figure()`: the side toward the fire a warm ember glow, the other a cool violet, a little warm dust at the feet; a flash lifts the warm side. (7) **The oak's bright edge flickered:** it jumped from one side of the trunk to the other the moment the sun passed behind it; now the two edges cross-fade, and the line is softened a little. (8) **The flicker at first sight of the mountain:** the far ridges' outline was sampled at fixed screen places, so it wobbled as the camera panned (`ridge()` now samples fixed places on the land); the village is baked at about its shown size with soft lit windows; the painting's flat bottom edge under the far mountain is covered by the plain laid again below its foot. (9) **One more look:** the wide shot at the foot holds about four seconds longer, and when he is back at the start of the path the camera pulls back once more to the whole mountain with lightning (`farewell`), then the mist takes him to the gate. (10) **More smoke and cloud far off:** `farK` (.4 of the reveal): a faint glow, a fuller plume and cloud gathering over the far mountain. (11) **Dust:** soft drifting clouds (`DUST`, `DUSTC`, `dust()`) instead of flat ovals; stones are rough many-sided shapes. Nothing spent.

**Build 203: SMOKE IN LAYERS (owner: "The clouds of smoke should be more like the mist at the base of the mount - maybe a layer instead of clear puff?").** The round billows of the canopy are gone: a short rise of soft billows leaves the summit (the plume's billows now fade by a third of their journey, softer and wider), then the smoke lies out in five long thin layers (`SMOKEL`: three ragged streak textures baked dark and again as underlight) that stream left with the wind, one above another across the top of the sky, like the low mist at the foot; each layer is drawn twice half a journey apart, each copy fading in and out as it drifts, so it never jumps; the lowest are lit from beneath by the fire and at a flash. Nothing spent.

**Build 205 (owner: "When we zoom over to mountain while worldly is talking it still flickers - can we make it solid. Also more lightning bolts and smoke at top of mountain. Some larger boulders too. Finally the layer on front of Christian as he walks toward mountain can be higher in point and fully go in front of him - maybe make nice shapes like the layer behind him").** (1) **Solid in the pan:** the mountain no longer fades in while the camera pans to it (half-seen, the ridges and sky showed through it as it slid): `reveal` is 1 the moment Wiseman points, and the far fire has no fast pulse. (2) **Lightning:** each flash is one to three jagged bolts out of the cloud over the summit with a branch or two (`bolt` is a list of lines), drawn as a white-gold core in a soft glow; distant flashes over the far mountain too while Wiseman points and as he turns aside. (3) **More smoke at the top:** the rise of billows from the summit is denser (70) and darker before it lies out in the layers. (4) **Larger boulders:** most 6 to 15 hill units, one in five 16 to 25. (5) **The nearest crags:** the same leaning spurs as the layer behind, wider (`seg` 250), much taller now and then (`hHi` 580), so a tall one passes wholly in front of him partway along the path; near the foot, where he and Evangelist stand and talk, they stay low (`clear` in `crags()`). Pebble dust is small and flat (a round brown blob showed at the foot). Nothing spent.

**Build 207: LIGHTNING TRACED FROM PHOTOGRAPHS (owner: "Lightning looks too crazy and unrealistic - maybe look for lightning shots on web (free pics) and trace them").** Two public-domain NOAA photographs from Wikimedia Commons ("Lightning NOAA.jpg", a great branching cloud-to-ground strike; "Cloud-to-ground lightning stroke - NOAA.jpg", two single strokes) were traced: the bright channels found by their contrast with the sky round them (a channel must stand 28 levels above a 19 px local average, so glowing cloud is left out), thinned to one-pixel lines (Zhang-Suen), followed into lines of points and simplified; each point keeps how thick the channel was there. Stored in `hill.js` as `BOLTS` (`big`, `a`, `b`, and `side`, a stroke through cloud; about 10 KB). Each flash takes one, sized, sometimes mirrored, and lands it on the rock's own top along the shoulders and the long right slope (found in `ROCKA`), where there is sky above to see it; the in-cloud stroke runs beside the summit. Drawn as a white core in a violet-white glow, thicker channels brighter; it flickers as real strikes do: a stroke, a dim moment, and often (60%) a second stroke that lights the sky again (`boltT`, `boltTwice`). The tracing script lived in the session scratchpad (`trace.py`); the photographs are not in the repo. Wikimedia's upload server rate-limits (429): only two of six photos came through. Nothing spent.

**Build 209: the far smoke takes the distance's haze (owner, with a phone picture of the far view: "Smoke is darker than mountain - can we make it same or lighter since the distance is lightening it. Also there can be more almost like layer of clouds about").** Far off (the plain, not among the rocks) the rising billows and the smoke layers are drawn in a pale smoke-grey (`PUFF[].h`, `SMOKEL[].h`), crossing to the dark smoke as the sky darkens (`hz`); and there are eight layers far off instead of five, the three lowest below the summit, passing behind the mountain and lying across the sky on its left. Nothing spent.

**Build 267: THE OPENING MADE BEAUTIFUL, A WHOLE OAK, THE VILLAGE UNDER THE HILL (owner: "opening is a little plain - what could we do to make this beautiful while maintaining simplicity . Tree needs a redo - cut out part doesn't look good. Town needs to look more polished and maybe closer to mt via story"; then "Sound great - can you make multiple passes with subagents providing critique ... like a painting that you want to stare at for an hour").** Two rounds of three critic agents (composition, simplicity and story, close-up craft) between passes. (1) **The oak painted again, whole** (one `gemini-3-pro-image` picture, 4:3 at 2K, about 15 cents, right first time; given the old oak as its style): a broad balanced parkland oak, nothing cut away; cut from its white, the rust-orange top leaves turned olive, a few small ragged sky holes opened in the crown (leaf-sized dabs, the boughs kept), the crown leaned a little toward where Wiseman waits, the edge eroded a pixel (no pale fringe): `assets/oak-leaf.webp` (936 x 640), `OAK_META` {ax 432, ground 606, k .76}. Fewer, smaller, dimmer twinkles (at most 70); the bright edge softer (.42). (2) **The sun through the crown** is a warm core with a soft glow; the turning star-flare spokes are gone (they read as a camera lens). (3) **The plain in layers** (`plainFill(y0)`, `SW`, `colAt`): three long low swells of field bunched toward the horizon, each a shade darker than the one behind, haze lying in the hollow behind each, the low sun on their crests in broken stretches strongest toward the sun; sampled on the land so they slide without wobbling; laid again below the far mountain's foot. (4) **The road** has a long flat warm sheen of the low sun under it (a worn track of two lines was tried and read as lane markings: taken out). (5) **Birds:** three, now and then crossing the opening's sky slowly (only on the plain before the meeting). (6) **THE VILLAGE OF MORALITY AT THE HILL'S NEAR FOOT, under the overhang** ("Go by that hill. The first house you come to is his"): baked at twice its shown size (`TOWN`, 900 x 170) on a gentle rise: Legality's hipped house first with two smoking chimneys, a garden wall and a great tree, cottages of every size, a barn, the church with tower and spire, round trees; a few soft lit windows; thin smoke threads rising straight; its foot fading into the plain's haze; drawn in front of the hill's foot mist. (7) **The story:** when Wiseman names the village the camera pans to it far off (`vil`; the road sinks, `cam.wl`, so it fills less of the picture); the hill is still hidden; at "Do you see that hill?" the hill comes slowly up out of the haze behind the village (`reveal` eases in about 2.5 s instead of at once; the camera is still, so no flicker). (8) **The hill's foot:** one level band of mist (the four drifting cloud pictures there read as blotches: removed). `HL.peek()` gives `vil` and `reveal`. Spent: about 15 cents. **Build 269, a third pass after the second critique:** fewer, feathered sky holes in the oak (three near its edge); the horizon's glare a soft glow, not a stripe; the crests' light one stroke whose brightness follows the stretches (no dots at the joints); the village darker than the field behind it (it had lost its roofs against it), standing lower in the haze with a deeper foot fade, its trees rooted, no garden-wall plinth, the cottages more varied, the smoke threads shorter. **Build 272: A REAL OAK (owner, of build 269's oak: "Too balanced - need to be natural organic"; "Yeah just tree , want it to look real - also please make sure character heads are clear of branches").** A first new picture given the balanced oak as its style came back still clumpy and balanced, with a clump floating free (about 15 cents, not used). A second, with NO picture given, only words (a realistic oil painting of a real, very old English oak "with the truth of John Constable's oil sketches", lopsided, shaped by wind, a short massive furrowed trunk leaning left and twisting, crooked elbowed limbs, one long crooked limb far out to the right with open space beneath it, small ragged leaf clusters with sky through them, a few dead branch tips, nothing floating; 4:3 at 2K, about 15 cents) came back right: `assets/oak-leaf.webp` (920 x 680, cut from its off-white paper and the mat it was painted inside, by distance from the paper's colour and lightness, specks under 150 px dropped, the warm highlights on its top leaves turned olive), `OAK_META` {ax 195 (the trunk), ground 664, k .88}, standing at `MEET - 250` so the trunk is left of the meeting and the long limb reaches over both men with clear room above their heads (checked at the talk and as Christian arrives). **Lesson: to get a REAL-looking tree from the image model, give it no stylised picture to copy; describe a real tree in words.** Spent: about 30 cents. **Build 275: the owner's five points ("Village could look more polished too, maybe use image model to make village. also hill should be there from the beginning ... the tree seems low resolution ... when christian goes in front of tree, he's shielded from sun, so the 'silver lining' should disappear. not sure i like the layers of hills - looks too busy - the calm simplicity of before was nice").** (1) **The hill is there from the first picture** (replaces build 185's hidden hill): far off on the right, the village of Morality at its foot, the fire in its clefts and its smoke into the lid of cloud; on the plain it keeps one size (`k` = .6 x `HSF`, not scaled by the camera's zoom) and slides only a little (`PH` .1, `HILL_F` 2040), as a far thing does, so it stays to the right of him from the opening through the talk; `reveal` is always 1. "Do you see that hill?" and the naming of the village now draw the camera back to one wide picture (`tx` = `C3.x + 420`, `z` .6): the two men small under the oak on the left, the hill and the village on the right. No words changed: "Do you see that hill?" still reads, now as Wiseman pointing out what has stood there all along. (2) **The oak at its full size:** the painting was being cut down to 920 px; now 1839 x 1360 (`OAK_META` ax 390, ground 1328, k .44: the same size in the game), and the dark leaf dabs that were drawn over it are gone (the painting's own leaves, toned dark, are finer); its edge eroded a little and the pale paper fringe darkened; its bright edge toward the sun is thinner and kept inside its outline (it had spread into a pale halo round the whole tree). (3) **No edge of light in front of the oak:** `figure()` samples the oak's outline (`OAKP.am`, its alpha at a quarter size; `OAKBOX`, where it was drawn this frame) behind the figure's head and shoulders; where the tree stands behind him it hides the low sun, and the edge (and its bloom) fades out, eased per figure (`RIMC`). (4) **The field's layers taken out** (`SW` = []): the plain is the calm gradient again, with the horizon's soft glow. Nothing spent. **Build 277 (owner: "i think hill and village can still be to the right, i like the open field and then shift right to see village , feel free to spend 15 cents"):** (1) the hill and village stand off to the RIGHT again, out of the opening's frame (`HILL_F` 4700, `PH` .3; still always drawn, `reveal` 1, one size on the plain), so the opening is open field and the oak; when Wiseman names the village the camera shifts right to it at the hill's foot (`tx` = `HILL_F + 160`, `z` .6), and "Do you see that hill?" holds there. (2) **The village painted** (one `gemini-3-pro-image` picture, 21:9 at 2K, about 15 cents, right first time; given the mountain painting as its style and a still of the scene): a small English village of the 1600s seen from the side, Legality's stone manor first with two tall chimneys, a garden wall and a great round tree, thatched and stone-slated cottages, a barn, the parish church with its tower and spire, round trees, its own lit windows and threads of smoke; cut from its white (its lower strip, a copy of our scene, cropped away), edges eroded a pixel and pale fringes darkened: `assets/village.webp` (1600 x 237, about 55 KB, `VILP`), toned into the evening haze (`hz` laid over at .44), its foot faded into the plain; the drawn silhouette `TOWN` stands in until it loads. (3) **A bug of build 275 fixed:** a `//` comment had swallowed the line that moves `vil`, so the camera never shifted to the village when Wiseman named it (the lesson in "Lessons": never put a comment before code on the same line).

**Build 281: feet in the road, a path over the brow (owner: "both christian and worldly can be shifted down slightly so their shadows don't extend past ground - also when christian walks down the hill can you make sure his feet and shadow are always firmly within ground region. finally, maybe add a slight path on the bump that he follows down").** On the fair plain every figure stands a little lower in the road (`FY` 12, was 5, set each frame in `draw()`; among the rocks and at the gate it stays 5) and the shadow (`foot()`) is flatter and lower, so it lies on the road, never on the field. Over the brow a faint worn track (`humpPath()`) runs 12 units below the hump's top line, exactly where his feet go, fading in from the road and on down the far side; it is drawn again with the near ground as he goes down behind the brow. Nothing spent.

**Build 282: mist behind them among the rocks, the path over the top, a glow for the low sun (owner: "can we put some mist behind christian when he's in mountain scene so he stand out a little with dark rocks. path should curve over crest of bump and disappear (same as christian's walk), still unsure about the look of the 'silver lining' ...would a light glow be better?").** (1) Among the rocks a soft pale haze in the low sky's rose-grey stands behind every figure (`HAZE`, drawn in `figure()` before the figure; off in the wide shot and the mist). (2) The worn track over the brow rises from 12 units below the top line to 2 at the crest (`pathOff`) and ends there, clipped to the hump, so it goes over the top and out of sight; his feet follow the same line (`C3.dy`), and his shadow thins away just before the top. (3) On the fair plain the crisp silver line is now only a faint trace (.3); the low sun is a soft warm glow behind the head and shoulders, spreading a little past the outline toward the sun (`RIM.halo`, a blurred copy of the silhouette, drawn behind the figure with 'lighter', faded down the body; it fades in front of the oak as before). The gold edge at the gate is unchanged. Nothing spent. **Build 283 (owner: "yes remove lining from tree too please"):** the oak's bright edge toward the sun (`OAKP.rimR`/`rimL`, build 174) is off too (`ra` 0 in the oak's draw; the code kept). Nothing spent.

**Build 286: a softer glow, the village behind the hill (owner: "make the glow around characters a little less. also, the little town looks too idylic - no trees maybe a slightly more from above angle perspective smaller house (maintain scale from when christian is near mountain.... maybe put town beyond mountain, peaking out from behind mountain on the left... finally is there an image model that's free like hugging face?").** (1) The sun's glow round the figures at .34 (was .55). (2) The painted village is drawn BEFORE the hill (`villageBehind()`), smaller (400 hill units wide, was 640), further off (its foot 62 units above the hill's foot) and a little hazier, its right part hidden behind the mountain's left foot, so it peeks out from behind it. Its trees and side-on view are still the old painting's: a new picture is needed. (3) **A free image model:** FLUX.1 Kontext dev on Hugging Face's free ZeroGPU (`black-forest-labs/FLUX.1-Kontext-Dev`, host `black-forest-labs-flux-1-kontext-dev.hf.space`, now allowed in the environment; no token needed; `gradio_client`, `api_name='/infer'` with (input_image, prompt, seed, randomize_seed, guidance_scale 2.5, steps 28)). It edits ONE given picture by words (no second style picture); about 20 s of the free daily GPU allowance a picture, shared with the daily clips. First try (the old village given back, "from a little higher up, no trees, small crowded cottages") came back as a 3D game-board diorama: say "distant hazy oil painting, not a 3D model, no ground slab". A second try was refused by the session's permission check (calls to that new host need the owner's say-so in the session). Other free ZeroGPU image Spaces checked running: `black-forest-labs/FLUX.1-schnell`, `FLUX.1-dev`, `Qwen/Qwen-Image-Edit` (each needs its host allowed). Nothing spent. **Build 290 (owner, of the free model's first try: "let's use that one you have (looks nice) just remove the hard edges and blend them in with field"):** that picture is now the village: cut from its white, the ground slab's hard front edges faded out (alpha eased away over 62 px above its lowest row, column by column), the tree and bushes at both ends cut off, colour .72 and 14% darker: `assets/village-far.webp` (1372 x 502, about 70 KB; a new name so phones fetch it; `village.webp` removed), drawn 270 hill units wide behind the mountain's left foot (`villageBehind()`, its foot 40 units above the hill's), so it peeks out small, seen a little from above, a crowd of stone and thatch cottages round the church spire. Nothing spent (the free model). **Build 292 (owner: "move village down a little bit and blend into mist some, also you can make the edges less harsh somehow - maybe some roads coming off or something"):** the picture's grass and paths are thinned (.55) and their alpha softened, every outer edge eased by a light blur; the picture widened by 300 px each side and 90 below, with three faint dirt lanes (tapering, fading with distance) running out from its left and right corners and its front point; drawn 388 hill units wide (the same village size) a little lower (`ty` = `hy` - 6k), more of its height fading into the plain's mist (from half its height, was .72) and a little more haze over it (.5). Nothing spent. **Build 295 (owner: "it looks funny to have decent resolution town next to low resolution edge of mountain"; my proposal, agreed: "yes sounds good!"):** the far mountain (`HILLV`) is baked at 1.3 times the hill's units (it was a third), so its outline is as crisp as the village's, its haze colours unchanged; and a soft breath of mist (an ellipse in the village's haze colour) lies where the village meets the mountain's left foot. Nothing spent. **Build 297 (owner: "looks fantastic ! can you put some rocky terrain around town to justify why christian was going through rocky terrain to get there"):** rocky ground round the village (`farRocks(front)` in `draw()`, drawn with the village before the hill): the mountain's own spur outline (`crag`, broad, seg 70 / 40) in a hazy dark mauve, a back row rising behind the village on its left like a small copy of the mountain, and a lower, darker front row across its foot and out to the left, both faded in from the left, their feet in the plain's haze, the right ends hidden by the mountain. Nothing spent. **Build 300 (owner: "a little more variety of rock and connecting to mountain"):** three rows now (`FR`: back, middle, front, each its own spur width `seg`, narrowness `wk`, tone and seed, with now and then a taller spur), rising toward the right, and the two back rows run on in under the mountain's foot with a floor that climbs to meet it (`.85 * smooth(-280, -50, u)`), so no sky shows between the rocks and the mountain. Nothing spent. **Build 301 (owner: "maybe a add the big rock on the left to what you have too. and some rocks to cover the left corner of town a little and blend into rock"):** two more entries in `FR` (`one: 1`: a single group faded at both ends, no floor): build 297's one broad rock on the left, drawn first behind everything, and a small group of rocks drawn after the village over its left corner (`u` -330 to -160), so the corner houses sink into the rock. Nothing spent. **Build 304 (owner: "can you decrease glow on christian and worldly wise a bit"):** the low sun's glow round the figures at .22 (was .34). Nothing spent. **Build 306 (owner: "can you make his shadow go our way since sun is behind him (relative to us)... also flashes of lightning that are super small should be removed. only the ones that go from clouds to mt should be preserved ... also can you tweak the billows a little and make a little elongated horizontally"):** (1) **Shadows toward us** on the fair plain (`SUNSH`, in `figure()`): each figure's own outline turned over at the feet and squashed flat (.24), made at a fifth of its size so it is soft, darkest at the feet and fading forward; the round contact shadow under the feet smaller and fainter there. (2) **Lightning:** the tiny forks are no longer kept, and a line of a strike is kept only if, following its forks back up, it comes from a line that starts in the cloud (`rootOK` in `fire()`), so no small floating flashes. (3) **Billows** drawn wider and flatter (3.2 by 1.4 of their radius, was 2.6 by 1.6) and strayed a little further to the sides (1.6). Nothing spent. **Build 308 (owner: "You went wrong direction with billows- make them less wide please- also maybe more wispy and diffuse"):** each billow drawn 2.2 by 1.6 of its radius (narrower than before 306's 2.6), strayed 1.2 to the sides; the billow shapes (`PUFF`) softer: lobe cores .7 falling off from the middle (were .88 / .74 to two thirds), 18 longer wisps (were 7), 7 soft holes (were 3). Nothing spent.

**TRIED AFTER BUILD 347 AND NOT KEPT: a crisp cauliflower column (owner asked to "look at 10 different photos of volcanos and billowing smoke to make the smoke look realistic", chose "A let's go, aim for Tavurvur", then on seeing the before and after: "I think I prefer the before").** Ten eruption photographs (USGS, NASA and others on Wikimedia Commons, looked at only) showed real columns as one dense dark mass of crisp cauliflower lobes, darker than the sky. Built on the branch `smoke-column` (never on `main`): one column from the summit vent, each billow baked as a lumpy surface lit by its slope (domes in a height map, creases darker), opaque, crisp on the windward side with a soft trail downwind, the side vents thin wisps. The owner prefers build 347's soft, pale, several-plume smoke. **Do not make the smoke crisp, opaque, lobed or photographic again (this, build 258's masses and build 260's painted plume were all turned down); keep the soft billows and change them only in small steps the owner asks for.** Nothing spent.

**Build 405: no second pull back; he runs on into the mist (owner: "when [he] leaves mountain [it] zooms out again... please re[move] this zoom ... just have him running and as he runs it fades out (don't let him stop running as it fades out)").** Build 202's farewell look at the whole mountain is no longer started (`farewell` stays 0; its code is kept). Near the start of the path (`PATH0 + 360`), while he runs back, the veil of mist comes (`ph` 'mist') and he runs on into it by himself (`C3.mistRun`, his plain run at its own pace) until it covers everything; the veil comes in about two seconds then (`.008` a tick, was `.018`), then the gate. If he had stopped short of the path's start, the mist comes as before. Nothing spent.

**Build 346: ONE CONTINUOUS PLUME, after a photograph (owner: "need more continuity and a little less detail - think big picture and maybe find some pictures of smoke from forest fire (or volcano) and how it billows").** Reference: the 1980 Mount St Helens eruption photograph (USGS, public domain, Commons "MSH80_eruption_mount_st_helens_05-18-80.jpg", looked at only, not in the repo): one continuous mass, big lobes made of smaller lumps, no sky between them, widening as it rises, then spreading flat under the overcast. In `cols()`: (1) a soft body under each column first (`cols('body')`: every billow's blurred copy `dB`, 1.8 times larger, at half strength), so no sky shows between billows; (2) fewer, larger billows (.78 of them, 1.15 the size), each at least .3 blurred (`gB`), so less fine detail; (3) the four columns lean together as they rise (.88 of the way to the summit column by .55 of the rise) and merge into one mass; (4) near the top they spread wide (`um`, up to 2.2 times), the umbrella under the cloud. Nothing spent.

**Build 343: the cloud itself detailed; the plumes' tops softer and lower (owner: "maybe make clouds themselves more detailed?", then of build 342: "looks better but slightly (very slighly) more blurry (diffuse?) and now billows go to high").** (1) Inside the storm deck (`mkDeck`): rolling masses from soft noise at two sizes, stretched sideways (`sh`), each mass's underside catching light from below (`dv`, the value's fall going down), soft broad hollows between (`cr`), a breath of warm colour where masses bulge near the underside; the firelight on the underside follows the masses; the pale far copy gets half the shading. Tried in this build and changed: thin crease lines (read as marble veins), a finer third noise size (grainy). Baked once, nothing per frame. (2) Build 342's top pass now uses the blurred billows (`dB`, `pB`) and ends lower (.42 to .82 of the rise, was to 1); the billows fade from .56 of their rise (build 339's .62 was too high). Nothing spent.

**Build 342: detail where the plumes meet the cloud (owner: "the top where plumes meet clouds could use a little more detail (seems too monochromatic)"):** after the front cloud tile, `cols('top')` lays the upper billows (from .42 to 1 of their rise) once more, faintly, over the cloud: their dark rolls (.3), a paler, warmer underside a little below each (`P.p`, .12) and a touch of the fire's and a strike's light (`P.l`), so the columns' shapes and a little colour carry on up into the cloud. A first try at twice the strength read as a pale wash over the cloud. Nothing spent.

**Build 339: plume detail higher, lit by the lightning (owner: "can detail in plumes extend a bit higher and can they light up (like the bottom of sky) when lightning flashes"):** (1) the billows keep their shape further up before melting into the cloud (they fade from .62 of their rise, was .5); (2) a strike lights them as it lights the cloud's underside: each billow gets a warm light copy (`PUFF[].s`, baked beside `p`) laid on in 'lighter', strongest near the strike (within 650 hill units, `bxS`) and higher up, timed with the strike's own flicker (`flS`, the same steps as `fl` in the deck). Tried in this build and taken out: thinning the front cloud tile where the columns stand (pale billows then showed above the cloud). `HL.peek()` now gives `boltT`. Nothing spent.

**Build 335: plume edges soft as a blur, not wisps (owner: "when i say diffuse i mean more guassian not wispy if that makes sense"):** every billow has a soft blurred copy (`dB`, `pB`, `hB`, baked once on a 240 canvas by shrinking to 30 and enlarging three times; no `ctx.filter`); the billows toward a column's sides cross over to that copy (`gB`, from `eg` and the plume's `E`), so the edges melt as a smooth haze; build 326's and 332's enlarging and fading of edge billows is eased (1 + .3 E eg size, at most half fainter). Applies to all four plumes, most to the far-left one (`E` 1.9). Nothing spent.

**Build 332: the far-left plume's edges more diffuse (owner: "the far left plume edges should be more diffuse"):** each entry of `PLUMES` may carry an edge strength `E` (6th entry; 1 by default); the far-left plume has 1.9: more of its billows count as edge (`eg` = distance^(1.5/E)), drawn up to 1.76 times larger and up to 82% fainter. Nothing spent.

**Build 328: a fourth plume (owner: "maybe one more plume to the left exiting near the light on the left of the top"):** a small column (`PLUMES`, hill x 12, 48 billows, .62 the width) from the left shoulder just below the light at the left of the summit, with its own small fire glow (`gl` in `PLUMES`). Nothing spent.

**Build 326: softer plume edges, a third plume, no oak in the wide shot (owner: "the interiors of the plumes of smoke look good but the edges could be a little more diffuse (change and compare) - also maybe we add one more plume (what do you think) (change and compare). also the oak tree should not be visible at all when you zoom out from mountain as it is back up the hill far away").** (1) In `cols()` the billows toward a column's sides (`eg`, from how far each strays, `EDGE`) are drawn up to 1.4 times larger and up to 55% fainter, so the columns' edges melt while their insides are unchanged. (2) A third column (`PLUMES`, hill x 315 on the right shoulder; 44 billows, .6 the width, out of step), with a small fire glow of its own at its foot, so the three step down from the summit. (3) The oak is never drawn among the rocks (`!rocky`): it stands at the crossing far back, and the wide shot's camera showed it on the horizon. Nothing spent.

**Build 325: stones on the way, no sliding in the pull back, mist off the mountain, steadier near rocks (owner: "(1) can we have a couple small rocks then later a couple some medium boulder fall as he walks from left to right at base of mountain (to make walk interesting) maybe even a shake or two. (2) ok when it zooms out there's a parallax like effect with the rocks around him as they scale differently than mountain on zoom. (3) the fog should go past the local rock layers at the left base part of the mountain so its framed by the rock and doesn't extend over mountain at all. (4) the sharp dark rocks in foreground flicker some as christian walks").** (1) At fixed places on the path, the first time he passes them (`FALLS`, `pathFalls()`, `PATHR`, world units): two small stones, then one, then two medium boulders come down off the heights ahead of him and land on the rocks just behind the path (never on him), bounce twice and lie there, with dust (`dustW`, `DUSTW`); each boulder jolts the ground (`jolt`, added to the trembling in `draw()`) with a low thud. (2) While the camera pulls back, every crag layer goes to parallax 1 (`p` eased to 1 over the first 12% of `Zw` in `crags()`), so they no longer slide against the mountain. (3) The mist bank behind him (build 314) is cut away wherever the mountain stands (the painting as it is clipped, and the face band) and off the crags behind the path where they rise into its foot (their outline `MIDPTS`, faded in from `FOOT - 650` to `FOOT - 250`): it lies in the open gully, framed by rock. (4) The crag outlines are sampled at fixed places on the land (a step of a whole power of two, `du`), not at fixed screen places, so they do not shimmer as the camera moves (measured: about a fifth less frame-to-frame change in the near rocks' outline; what is left is the sub-pixel movement). Nothing spent.

**Build 318: no ghost roots below the fade (owner: "you can see roots of tree popping out below transition though"):** each strip of the fade now ends fully clear (alpha 1), so the faint ragged root ends that showed below it are gone; the varied line stays. The rule this gave is in "Picture rules" (attention to detail at every seam). Nothing spent.

**Build 316: the roots' fade varies (owner: "a little better, but now looks like a clean cut - a variable transition would be better"):** the fade's line wanders along the oak's foot (in 4-pixel strips, by slow waves and a little jitter, `loadOak()`), so some roots reach deeper into the earth and some fade higher; no straight line. Nothing spent.

**Build 313: build 311 made much subtler (owner: "that seemed to have broken the entrance to level 3 - the fade is too much - could use a much more subtle integration"):** the swell of earth round the oak read as a dark smudge in the opening: now flat and faint (radius 300, height .035, at most .5); the roots fade only in their last few units (from 12 above the ground line to 16 below, at most .85), so they show again. The brow's change stays. Nothing spent.

**Build 311: the oak's roots and the brow sit in the ground (owner: "can you integrate roots of tree into ground a bit more" ... "and bump too").** (1) The ragged strip of roots at the oak's foot fades out downward in the picture (`loadOak()`, from 34 units above its ground line to 14 below), and a low soft swell of the road's own earth gathers round its foot (`MEET - 250`, drawn before the oak), covering the road's pale edge there, so the roots go down into the earth instead of lying on it as a flat dark band. (2) The brow (`crest`) is no longer one flat grey shape: `humpG()` fills it from a touch of the field's olive at its top down into the road's earth at its foot (also for the near ground as he goes down behind it), its top edge laid four times a hair higher and fainter (soft), and its foot runs on a few units into the road, faded at both ends, so there is no seam. Nothing spent. **Build 314 (owner: "the mist around christian should maybe be replaced by mist localized to the layer behind him (so he doesn't glow so much when he's near mountain) ... it is difficult to see him"):** build 282's glow behind each figure among the rocks (`HAZE`) is off; instead one low bank of mist lies in front of the crags behind the path, along his stretch of it (`alongPath(inPath, ...)` after `crags(.75, ...)`), from about 330 units up, thickest at chest height, in the old haze's rose-grey, gone in the wide shot (`Zw`). The men stand dark against a paler band.
