Nothing spent. **Build 321 (owner: "when he first mentions house can he just reference it (down there beyond) then only zoom over when he mentions mountain"):** naming the village ("In that village, Morality ...") only warms its windows far off (`vil`); the camera stays on the two men under the oak and shifts right to the village and the hill only at "Do you see that hill?" (`pan`, `panTo`, set by that line). No words changed. Nothing spent. **Build 323 (owner: "the bump has hard line between it and the ground below. also the path looks different as it goes from one to the other"):** the brow is now the road itself bent up: inside `road()`, column by column, the road's own gradient (its pale worn edge, its earth, darkening down) is hung from the brow's top line instead of the road's, so there is no seam at its foot and the road's worn edge runs up over the top as the path; `humpG` and `humpPath` are gone, `humpDraw()` keeps only the soft top edge. The near ground drawn again over him past the crest starts on a whole pixel (a faint upright line showed there). Nothing spent. **Build 327 (owner: "i like the path arcing over hump (it just need to be opaque so it doesn't look different over the transition) also, looks like there are two kinds of shadows at christians feet - a circular one and then one coming towards us. can we keep just the one coming towards us?"):** (1) the brow's soft top edge (`humpDraw()`) is laid only ABOVE its outline (it was laid over the whole brow and lightened it, so the brow read paler than the road); the road's low-sun sheen is lifted with the brow column by column (`HC`, `sheen(dy)` in `road()`), so the brow is the very same road surface, its warm edge running up over the top; (2) the round contact shadow under the feet (`foot()`) fades out as the shadow toward us (`SUNSH`) comes in, so on the fair plain only the shadow toward us shows (the round one returns as the sky darkens and among the rocks). Nothing spent. **Build 329 (owner: "should we elongate shadow?" ... "yes go ahead"):** the shadow toward us (`SUNSH` in `figure()`) is a third of the figure's height (was a quarter, `.36`), fading more gradually toward its far end (four stops), so it reads as a low sun's shadow and melts into the road well above the words. Nothing spent. **Build 334 (owner: "can you put a gravelly path arcing from where he started over the hill (showing its a detour from the flat path forward"):** a worn gravel track (drawn after `humpDraw()` in `draw()`) leaves the road just past the meeting under the oak (`MEET + 20`, faded in over 220 units), runs along the road a little way, then climbs over the brow exactly on his feet line (`pathOff(x) - crest(x)`) and ends at its top (`CREST`), where he goes out of sight; the road goes on flat beyond the brow. A soft pale bed of trodden earth (six layered bands, so no hard edges) with fine small stones set by place on the land (a dark underside and a lit top; by a fixed hash, so they slide with the ground and never shimmer), all darkening with the sky; plain only. Tried in this build: larger sparse stones read as scattered dots. Nothing spent. **Build 336 (owner: "the tip needs to transition over hill better (look like path going over) - also please add a path going straight so it looks like fork in the road"):** the gravel is ONE way along the road on the fair plain (`gravel()`, its bands and stones in `way()`), and where the brow begins (`BROW0 - 330`) it FORKS: the straight way goes on flat along the road at the feet line (drawn last, nearer than the brow), the turning climbs the brow on his feet line and goes over the top, narrowing as it tips away (to 5% by `CREST + 110`) and clipped to the brow's outline, so it is lost behind it; `gravel()` is drawn again with the near ground past the crest. Nothing spent. **Build 338 (owner: "the straight path should look a little less hard lines but have some slight bumpiness (wave) to it"):** the gravel way wanders gently up and down (`wav`, up to about 3 units, three slow waves set by place on the land) and swells and narrows along its length (`wid`, .66 to 1.08); the turning starts on the same wandering line at the fork and straightens as it climbs. Nothing spent. **Build 340 (owner: "can you make straight path a little wider"):** the straight way is 1.4 times as wide as the turning (`way(..., T)`), its stones spread over the width and a little thinned, so the true way reads as the broader, more trodden road and the turning as a narrower track. Nothing spent. **Build 354 (owner: "the rocks need to land and not float"):** the stones that fall on the path (`PATHR`, build 325) used to come to rest at a fixed height 16 to 34 units above the road, so where the path's stony far edge is low they hung in the air; now each rests on the ground under it (`groundAt(x, r)`: the highest point of the stony edge under its width, from `edgeAt`, the same outline `road()` draws, sunk in a fifth of its size), checked at every bounce. Nothing spent. **Build 355 (owner: "when evangelist shows up, there some cropping needed on evangelists sprite"):** (1) his walk and his standing pictures (`evangelist-walk.pack`, `evangelist-stand.pack`; the standing one is also Level 1's field) were trimmed like Christian's in build 226: soft pixels more than 2 px from his solid body cleared, the soft edge sharpened (alpha `(a - 90) * 255 / 140`), edge pixels coloured from the body next to them (no pale paper fringe), and pale paper caught inside his outline between staff and robe darkened to the robe round it; `PACK_V` bumped. (2) He no longer cross-fades from walking to standing (both half see-through at once showed his arm and staff twice, a grey flap under the sleeve): at the planted step the standing picture takes his place whole, and the walking picture is drawn 1.048 times its size and 37.5 picture pixels right (`FK`, measured: 292 against 306 pixels feet to head, the standing body 37.5 right of its `ax`), so he neither grows nor jumps. The script (`evtrim.py`) was in the session scratchpad. Nothing spent. **Build 356 (owner: "Can you make stones disappear behind blue layer"):** the stones that fall on the path strike the top of its stony far edge (dust, a thud, the jolt) and then drop on behind it out of sight: they are drawn before the blue-black path (`road()`), so it covers them, and they are removed once wholly below the edge (`r.gone`); no more bouncing to rest on top. `HL.peek().stones` gives each stone's place and whether it has struck, for tests. Nothing spent.

**Build 262: back to build 256's billows, spread out (owner, shown 256, 258 and 260 side by side: "I like 1 best still (sadly) maybe we can just spread out billows a bit and make them ever so slightly more billowy").** The columns are build 256's soft billows again (`PUFF`, `cols()` restored from that build); the painted plume (260) and the billow masses (258) are taken out (`PLUME`, `assets/smoke-plume.webp`, `MASS`, `bakeMasses()` removed; all in git history). Now each billow strays 1.35 times as far to the sides and .65 (was .5) up and down, sways a little more, and its shape has ten lobes with fuller cores (.88 / .74 to 66% of the radius, then .3 at 88%). Nothing spent.

**Build 260: THE PAINTED PLUME (owner, of build 258's masses: "Yeah not my favorite - let's try B"; one `gemini-3-pro-image` picture, 9:16 at 2K, about 15 cents, right first time).** Given the mountain painting (`hill-rock.webp`) as the style and a still of the scene, asked for ONE column of volcanic smoke alone on pure white: John Martin's rolling billows in the painting's own brushwork, light from below only (amber undersides, dark brown-black tops), a narrow neck at the bottom swelling upward. Cut from its white (alpha from the deficit from white, colour un-whitened), mirrored to lean left with the wind, toned (colour .72, 22% darker, the bright glow held down so the base is no torch: the scale rule), its top third faded and its top corners feathered so it melts into the cloud: `assets/smoke-plume.webp` (768 x 1376, about 85 KB; its neck at 65% of its width). In `hill.js`: `PLUME`, loaded with the level; `warped()` redraws it once a frame into its own canvas two whole-pixel rows at a time (`tintC.plume`), each row swaying and swelling a little on slow waves that grow with height, so the billows seem to roll (thin strips drawn straight onto the picture left seams); `plume()` lays it on both vents (the second .78 the size and .85 the width, out of step), a pale copy far off in the haze, a little brighter at a flash; it feeds the mask `SMK` softly (.7: at full strength the cut-out of the cloud's light showed as dark blocks). Build 258's masses (`MASS`, `bakeMasses()`) stand in until the picture loads. Comparison sheet (256, 258, 260) was shown to the owner, not kept. Spent: about 15 cents.

**Build 258: BILLOW MASSES, the smoke rebuilt (owner: "gosh I can't get smoke right ! need some deep thinking here"; my diagnosis and plan A, agreed: "A let's go").** Why it kept missing: (1) the light was backwards (build 256 made billows lighter on top; the light here is the fire BELOW, the look the owner chose in build 154 was "a full dark billow lit from beneath"); (2) no structure: about a hundred small identical blobs read as fuzz; (3) each blob slid on its own path, so the column shimmered instead of rolling; (4) code fuzz beside a painted mountain. Now each column is a stream of big masses (22 and 18 alive, a new one every 1800 / N ticks from the vent): each mass one of six cauliflowers baked once (`MASS`, `bakeMasses()`, 256 x 208: a core and 22 rounded lobes with soft edges, drawn from the bottom lobe up), dark body darker on top (`d`), and a separate light layer (`l`) of warm glow on each lobe's underside where every lobe first hides the light of the lobes behind it, so the lumps read one by one; pale copies for near the rock (`p`) and far off (`h`). A mass rises, swells (28 to 140 units), turns over slowly (up to about a quarter turn, flipped at random) and melts into the cloud; the youngest are drawn in front; the firelight on them is strongest near the vent. Tried in this build and changed: crisp spheres with bright underglow read as bunches of grapes; now soft-edged, overlapping, gentle glow. The old `PUFF` shapes are no longer drawn by the columns. The mask `SMK` (builds 244 and 248) is fed by the masses. Plan B, held: a painted plume from the mountain painting itself (about 15 to 25 cents), moved by code. Nothing spent.

**Build 256: more billowy (owner: "smoke still needs to be a bit more billowy").** Each billow shape is nine rounder lobes with full cores and a firmer rounded edge (alpha .85 / .7 to 62% of the radius, then falling off), only 7 faint wisps and 3 small holes; its dark and its pale are each a gradient, lighter on top and darker beneath ([68,56,60] to [42,34,39]; pale [124,106,104] to [90,76,78]), so the column reads as rolling volumes. Nothing spent.

**Build 253: the billows halfway back from wispy (owner: "Too [wispy] bit looking much better!!").** A fuller core, 14 fainter wisps (was 22) and 5 lighter holes (was 9); colours as build 251. Nothing spent.

**Build 251: the billows in the cloud's colour, paler near the rock, wispier (owner: "Can you match billows color with clouds better - also lighten billows closer to mountains d make em make slightly wispier").** The columns' dark is the cloud underside's own colour ([54,44,49], was a redder [62,46,50]); billows just leaving the rock are laid with a paler warm smoke-grey (`PUFF[].p`, [108,92,92]) fading to the cloud colour by about two fifths of the way up (`lo`); each billow shape has a smaller core, a fringe of 22 drawn-out wisps and a few soft holes worn through it, so its edge frays. **Two pushes were numbered 250** (this chat's touched strikes, 03140bf, and the small-tweaks chat's lantern arm, 629bbb6; they crossed). Nothing spent.

**Build 250: a touched strike comes out of the cloud above the touch; no glow where the smoke meets the cloud (owner: "Glow still Shines thru where billows hit upper clouds - can we make it that lighting exits from cloud where viewer clicks (is that possible?)").** (1) The front cloud tile's firelight is now cut out where the smoke stands too (it was laid over the columns' tops on purpose in build 233; the owner prefers them dark), so no light of the cloud shows on or through the columns anywhere. (2) In the wide shot a touch in the sky brings a strike whose top leaves the cloud straight above the touch (`fire(k, atX)`: the strike's top is moved there and the rest leans to meet its landing on the rock, which is under the touch where there is rock below, else at the rock's nearest edge, hill x 40 to 640); the cloud lights from within at that place. `HL.peek().bolt` gives the strike's lines (first and last points) for tests. Nothing spent.

**Build 248: no light through the billows (owner: "The light still shines thru billows - don't like that").** The two columns of smoke are worked out first as a mask (`cols(true)` fills `SMK` before the cloud is drawn; `cols(false)` draws them in their old place), so the back tile's lit underside (fire and strike) is cut out where the smoke stands, as the front tile's strike light already was (build 244); and each billow's dark is laid 1.9 times as strong (`DENSE`), so the glow of the sky and summit no longer shows through the columns. Their firelight from beneath is unchanged. Nothing spent.

**Build 246: the mountain's foot one with the ground (owner: "the blue shale layer and other layers in the zoomed in view shouldn't continue across page when we zoom out but taper off quickly . Then merge the base of the mountain with the the dark layer so they look continuous ... as is the fog and blue make it feel distinct which I don't like").** The slate path, its low stone edge and the warm haze over it (`[90,66,64]` band) now lie only along his stretch of the path (`inPath`: from `PATH0 - 900` to `FOOT + 800`, faded over 600 and 550 units), laid through a soft mask across the picture (`alongPath`, canvas `tintC.wb`). Beyond it the ground is the mountain's own foot: a soft rise in the rock's base tone laid a little over the painting's lower edge (from 34 units above the road line), darkening down into the nearest crags' floor colour [15,12,11] at their base line, so the rock, the ground and the dark band at the bottom read as one. Close up nothing changes along the path; at the foot the slate fades out under the overhang. `tools/stills3.js` takes `DSF=2` for stills at twice the size. Nothing spent.

**Build 244: the strike's light behind the smoke (owner: "I want the lighting up level of the clouds from lightning to be behind columns of smoke going up").** The front cloud tile is drawn after the columns, so a strike lit it over them and the smoke was washed out. Now the columns' shapes are also laid in a mask (`SMK`), and on the front tile the strike's light (`strikeAt`) is cut out wherever the smoke stands (`SMK3`); the fire's glow on it (`fireAt`) and the back tile's lit underside are as before. The columns stand dark against the flash. Nothing spent.

**Build 238: the near rocks rounder and lower (owner: "Maybe not quite so pointy and slightly more varied in shape and maybe not quite so much covering Christian - overall great though").** Each near spur's width varies (.65 to 1.55 of `wk`, now .62), its brow is a little longer (by .04 to .16) and rounded (`1 - .32 u²` for the near layer, was nearly flat), and the layer is lower (210 to 430 units, was 300 to 560): he mostly shows, now and then hidden to the waist or chest, now and then nearly all. Tried in this build: longer brows (.1 to .28) read as flat tabletops. Nothing spent.

**Build 237: the sky in four layers, the gully clear, livelier near rocks, a quicker walk, a wide picture that answers touches (owner: "layers from bottom to top: (1) dark cloud backdrop across sky (2) 'light up' layer for lightning flashes (3) billowing up smoke in front of light up layer (4) another cloud layer across sky providing foreground cover and covering the upper part of billowing smoke so it's integrated into cloud layer above ... When we're down with Christian we remove mist that's between mountain and next layer up. Also top layer of rock (dark) can be more varied up and down so we see his body go behind and then appear quickly. Can we speed up his pace a little ... When we're zoomed out can we pause for a bit and viewer can click cloud or cracks of fire to make effects").** (1) **Four layers:** the back tile of the cloud (`layer(DECK[0], 0)`, a little more solid, .9) is the backdrop, with its firelit and strike-lit underside at full strength; then the two columns of smoke; then the front tile drawn WHOLE across the sky (`deckWisp` now calls `layer(DECK[1], 1)`), covering the columns' upper part. Build 233's wisps-only bake is gone. (2) **The haze in the gully** between the mountain and the crags behind the path is taken out. (3) **The nearest crags** are narrower spurs (`wk` .5 of the width, a new parameter of `crag()`/`crags()`) on a low floor (`fl` .22), 300 to 560 units tall all along the path: he passes behind one, then shows in the gap, every second or two; still low where he and Evangelist talk (`clear`). (4) **His pace on the path** is a third quicker (1.3 times `MAXV`; it slows only to .7 near the foot, was .5); the walk pictures step faster with him, so the feet still match. (5) **The wide picture waits and answers touches** (`wideTouch`): once the storyteller's lines are done a side message says "Touch the cloud, or the fire in the rock." and the picture holds about eight seconds more, four more after each touch, up to 25 seconds; a touch in the sky brings a strike down from the cloud there (`fire(k, atX)`), a touch on the rock makes its nearest crack flare (a hot bloom, `FLARES`; a burst of steam from that vent, sparks, a low rumble). The story runs by itself here (`G.state` 'auto'), so `index.html`'s touch handler now passes such touches to `HL.touch` in Level 3. Nothing spent.

**Build 233: a second column of smoke, and the cloud's wisps in front of both (owner: "still need another layer of wispy smoke to go over billowing up smoke so it's going into cloud in front of lit up section. Can we make another column going up from other lit up spot at peak of mt").** (1) A second, slimmer column (64 billows, .78 the width, a little out of step) rises from the brightest fire in the painting, just right of the summit (hill x 205, on the rock's top by `rockTopAt`), to the same height as the first. (2) The front tile of the cloud also bakes its hanging wisps alone (`wd`/`wh`/`wl`, a little denser), and after the columns are drawn those wisps and their firelight are laid once more, only over the stretch of sky where the columns rise (`deckWisp`, faded at the sides), so the smoke goes up INTO the lit wisps. Nothing spent.

**Build 232: smaller boulders far off, the smoke over the cloud's lit underside (owner: "Boulders too big when it's zoomed out. If zoomed in, they can be bigger. Smoke going up from the top of mount can go above light layer of billowing smoke from light").** (1) The falling boulders and their dust are drawn smaller the further the camera stands back (`bS` = k / 1.8, between .3 and 1: about a third of their size in the wide shot, full size once the mountain is seen close). (2) The summit's column of smoke is now drawn after the cloud ceiling, so it rises in front of the cloud's firelit underside and on up into its dark body (to 90 hill units above the underside), melting away there. Nothing spent.

**Build 229: darker figures among the rocks, a column of smoke into the cloud (owner: "Can character be darker when they are in rock layer going to mountain - more smoke going from top of mountain to cloud layer above").** (1) Among the rocks the figures' tint is near-black at once ([38,35,39] to [30,27,32] as the sky darkens; it used to ease in with the darkness from a pale grey), so Christian and Evangelist read as dark shapes against the rock and sky with only a trace of their watercolour; on the fair plain unchanged ([74,68,60], easing to [52,47,52]). (2) The summit's smoke now rises as a column straight up into the cloud ceiling (`Dh`, the deck's height: 195 hill units among the rocks, 90 to 195 on the plain as the sky darkens): 96 billows, narrower and rising more upright, widening as they go, fading only where the deck covers them, so the smoke and the cloud are one thing. Nothing spent.

**Build 224: the rocky path in blue-black slate (owner, of build 221's brown path: "I thought the blue brought in some nice color (I love nice color palette s!) ... If you don't bring blue back in what could you do instead?"; shown four stills (all brown, strong blue, soft slate, plum) and then, from a screenshot of the old path, "I like this subtler blueish").** Among the rocks the path is a blue-black slate (`rc` [28,29,37] to [21,22,29] as the sky darkens), keeping build 221's stony edge and grain; the nearest crags stay warm near-black. The picture's colour rule there: warm light (fire, firelit rock, rose sky) against one cool shade (the path). The strong blue read as water; plum was too quiet. Nothing spent.

**Build 221: a wispier ceiling, a stony path, Christian lighter on the grass (owner: "The blue path layer can have some contour (or ruggedness) to it to make it match surroundings - what you think would look best. The cloud layer looks fantastic but maybe slightly too wavy - what about wispier and layered but keeping color and the way the bottom lights up. Also Christian is a bit too dark in first part of level 3 when he's in green grass").** (1) **The deck** (`mkDeck`): its underside is nearly level (only a slow gentle swell, no hanging tongues), its edge torn into long horizontal streaks, and three thin layers of wisp hang under it (`WISP`: each lower and fainter, broken into long strands, lit from beneath like the deck); same colours and firelit underside. (2) **The path among the rocks** is the crags' own warm brown-black (it was the night's blue), its far edge a broken line of low stones up to about 20 units high, behind the feet (the walking line stays level), darker than the crags behind as a nearer layer should be, with the rock's grain (`ROCKPAT`) over it; no lit line along it (tried: it read as a thread of lava). (3) **On the fair plain** the figures' tint is lighter and warmer ([74,68,60], was [50,46,44]; Wiseman [80,72,62]): the dark of build 194 was against see-through spots, which `solidOf` and the solid packs (build 212) have since fixed. Nothing spent.

**Build 218: ONE CLOUD CEILING (owner: "Should the smoke and cloud be more continuous and less varied (maybe more dark overall? Open to suggestions here"; my proposal, agreed: "Yes please code !").** The canopy of round billows (151), the long layers of smoke (203, 209) and the storm bank of billows (213) are all gone; in their place ONE storm deck (`DECK`: two tiles baked in `build()`, 1200 x 220, repeating seamlessly, each dark, pale for the haze and as the firelight on its underside): one solid dark body, nearly black at the top, its underside a long slow swell, a little ragged, with a few soft tongues; the nearer tile darker and a little lower, both drifting slowly left. The sky below it stays open and glowing down to the horizon so the black mountain reads (John Martin). The summit's short rise of smoke goes up into it; the strikes come down out of it (`STORM_Y` 120; its underside about 175 hill units above the summit in the wide shot); the fire lights its underside just above the summit, a strike lights it from within near the strike. Far off on the plain it is a pale lid in the haze over the far mountain only (`lid`); as he turns aside it darkens and spreads over the whole sky (`spread`); among the rocks, where the summit is far above the picture, it hangs as a ceiling across the top of the picture (`ceil`, 13% down) and the near mountain is cut out of it, so the cliff stands in front of the cloud. Drawn through two picture-sized canvases (`tintC.deck`) with soft masks across (`across`): drawing it in strips left seams. Nothing spent.

**Build 213: strikes out of a storm cloud, ending on the rock (owner: "The vertical lightnings look great - the horizontal ones do not look too good - maybe look for other appropriate photos or just remove those horiz ones - do we need dark clouds that they are emerging from and should we make sure strands always end on top of mountain?").** (1) The stroke through cloud (`side`) is no longer used. (2) A low dark storm bank hangs over the mountain (replaced by the deck in build 217; `STORM_Y` 210 hill units above the summit; sixteen dark billows drawn after the strikes, so each strike's top is hidden in it; lit from within near each strike while it flashes; hazed far off); the wide shot stands a little further back (`Z_WIDE` .068, was .085) so the bank is in the picture. (3) Every strike begins inside the bank and lands on the rock's top (`rockTopAt`, from the rock picture's alpha): single strokes have their lowest point set on the rock; the great branching strike is made taller than the drop so the rock cuts through its lower part, where its branches fan out; every line is cut at the rock's surface; each branch is joined to the line it forks from, a line is kept only if it reaches the rock or leads to a branch that does, and kept lines are trimmed back to their last fork that does, so no strand ends in the air (tiny forks excepted). Nothing spent.

**Build 215: THE GATE FROM PHOTOGRAPHS (owner: "The ending of level three is weak - need a nice door - maybe look online for pics of old wooden doors . Also rocks could be picture based with more texture and more continuous (grout lines too strong) . When door is opened can light stream out - is there a reason we don't see doorkeeper ?").** (1) **The door** is a photograph of the weathered oak door of St Mary's church, Wroxham, Norfolk (Wikimedia Commons, "Wroxham Church doorway.JPG", **public domain**, released by its photographer), cut to its round arch, made nearly grey and warmed: `assets/gate-door.webp` (`GDOOR`); the arch is a little larger (`GR` 40, `GH` 126; he stops at `GATE_STOP` = `GATE_X - 74`). (2) **The wall** is a photograph of rough fieldstone (Commons, "Grey taupe moderately deteriorating aged eroded worn fieldstone seamless building wall texture.jpg", **CC0**, already seamless), its large-scale shading taken out, made grey about .8: `assets/gate-stone.webp` (`GSTONE`), laid over the light by 'multiply' at 380 units a tile, so the stones show only where the lantern or the door lights them and the joints are the photograph's own soft shadows; the drawn `STONES` stand in until it loads. (3) **Light streams out:** when Good-will comes the door swings in on its hinge at the right (`lw`, `gapR`), the gap is bright, its free edge catches the light; a soft wedge of light in the air spreads left over Christian with four slowly breathing rays and dust turning in it; a long flat pool on the road; his shadow lies flat behind him. Glows are kept out of the doorway (`notDoor()`). (4) **Good-will in the doorway, a dark figure against the light** (`GWF`: Evangelist's standing frames with the staff cut away, mirrored, nearly black): a stand-in; Level 4 still needs his own figure. Why he was not seen before: he had no figure (Level 4's open question). The end card waits a little longer (`stillT > 380`) so he is seen. **Departure:** in the book Good-will asks who is there before he opens (`{60}`, `{61}`); here the door opens partway as he comes. Fetching from Commons: the API and the originals answered 429 again; **thumbnails at `upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/<a>/<ab>/<name>/1280px-<name>` came through at once** (the upload host, not `thumb.wikimedia.org`). Nothing spent.

**Build 216: the gate, second pass (owner: "Looks great . Door frame still look fake . Doorkeeper maybe should be on right side and door opens on other side (so there's some natural space between two figures ) please make doorkeeper little smaller and less dark . Top of wall can be little more rugged matching contours of rocks").** (1) **A stone surround:** dressed jamb stones and nine voussoirs between `ri` (the door set back in a 5-unit reveal) and `ro` (19 units out), paler than the rubble because more of the same light falls on them (`frameP()`, laid before the stone photograph's multiply, so they carry its texture), soft thin joints, a soft shadow along the outer edge away from the lantern; the reveal's far side catches the light from within when the door opens; the inscription over the gate is one of these stones, its words cut in (no flat painted bar). (2) **The hinge is on the left:** the leaf swings in on Christian's side (`gapL`), the opening is on the right, Good-will stands in it with the leaf between the two men. (3) **Good-will smaller and less dark:** `FK` .86 (as if a step further in), tint [52,44,40] so his robe and beard show faintly in the light (they are Evangelist's: still a stand-in). (4) **The wall's top** is a row of rough rounded cap stones of every width (14 to 38 units, 1 to 5.5 high, now and then one missing; `CAP`, `cap()`) over the slow swell. (5) The stream of light is painted at an eighth of the size and stretched up (`BEAMC`), so its edges are soft (a straight hard edge showed on the wall). Nothing spent.

**Build 219: a real lantern, a carved stone, and the stone seen close up (owner: "The lattern and sign need redoing to make super realistic - how about we make sign clickable so one click on it and zoom in (kinda like book)").** (1) **The lantern** is a photograph of an 18th-century iron lantern in the Rijksmuseum ("VOC lantaarn, NG-1985-1.jpg" on Commons, **CC0**), cut from its grey ground (the handle's hole left open, the floor shadow taken out), its iron darkened to the evening, its glass panes (and the company's VOC letters behind them) made into glowing horn, mottled, with the soft core of the flame showing through, as lanterns had in Bunyan's day: `assets/lantern.webp` (`GLANT`, 180 x 358, the flame at 71.5, 229), 40 units tall, hanging by its handle from a wrought-iron hook on a plate in the wall; it breathes with the flicker. (2) **The stone over the gate** is a dressed stone in the surround (`SW` 104, `SH` 26), chamfered, Bunyan's words cut into it in real letters (too small to read in the scene). (3) **A touch on it opens it close up** (`signPt()`, `showSign()`, `signPic()` in `hill.js`; `KS.look(src)` in `keepsakes.js`, a new overlay `#kslook` that grows out of the scene, the world paused, a touch anywhere closes it): a weathered dressed stone (the wall's own photograph at several sizes for its grain, speckle, chips, rain-darkening toward the foot), lit from the left by the lantern, "KNOCK, AND IT SHALL BE / OPENED UNTO YOU." cut as a V-groove in IM Fell capitals (the upper-left wall of each cut in shadow, the lower-right catching the light), and Bunyan's own margin reference "MATT. VII. 8." (`{59}`, book file line 942: "[Matt 7:8]"). Touchable at the gate, while knocking and after. Not yet: a glint to show it can be touched. Commons thumbnails: only the standard widths work (960, 1280; 640 and 320 answered 400). Nothing spent.

**Build 222: the wall lit stone by stone, a drawn lantern, light and shade on the figures (owner: "Yes do A ... Also lamp looks like wrong perspective can we redo with computer design using this as design reference ... Christian looks kinda flat is there a way to introduce shadows to spice up his sprite?").** (1) **The wall** (my plan A; B, one large non-repeating wall photograph with damp streaks, is held for later): the stone photograph is read as a height map and lit pixel by pixel ONCE by the lantern (a point light 26 units out from the wall, falling off quickly, `R` 110) and by the open door (two points in the doorway): each point takes light by the slope of its stone toward the light, and is shadowed where a nearer stone stands between (a short march toward the light over the heights); relief `Hs` 4.5 units; a little ambient so it is not foil-like; faded out 220 units from the baked region's ends. Baked at 2 pixels a unit over 1120 x 400 units round the gate, 36 rows a frame (`stepBake()`, `RELIT`, `WALL_LIGHTS`), started under the veil of mist so the arrival does not stutter (about half a second of work in all); laid on in 'lighter' (`RELIT.L` with the flicker, `RELIT.D` as the door opens). The old wash and 'multiply' stand in until it is ready. The surround and the stone over the gate are a smooth wash of the same lights with only the photograph's fine grain (dressed stone), so they read against the rough wall. (2) **The lantern is drawn in code straight on** (`bakeLantern()`, `LANT`, 22 x 40 units, baked at 5 pixels a unit), after the Rijksmuseum lantern: ring, turned finial, flared stepped roof with a pierced band that glows, one mottled horn pane with the flame a soft brightness behind it, corner posts lit on their inner edge, corner brackets, a pierced base, two feet; the photograph (`assets/lantern.webp`) was removed. (3) **Light and shade on the figures** (TAKEN OUT in build 225, the owner preferred him near-black at the gate; `shade()` in `figure()`, all Level 3 figures): each picture's outline becomes a rough shape (its mask blurred at two sizes), a slope is read at each point, and for each light in the scene (`KEYS`: the lantern and the door at the gate; the fire among the rocks; the sky above on the plain) a grey map of that shape lit from that side (16 directions, made once per picture at half size, `shapeOf`, `shadeMap`) is laid on with 'soft-light': the side toward the light comes forward, the far side and the burden fall into shade, and the watercolour's detail stays. At the gate the warm lift of his tint is a little less (`lit * (.24 + .26 * door)`). Level 2's figures are not shaded yet. Nothing spent.

**Build 225 (owner: "Instead of shadow can you just make Christian super dark . Please lighten door as it opens as light hits it too . Dark Christian's shadow please . Make sure you sync across multiple chats !!").** (1) Build 222's light and shade on the figures (`shade()`, `KEYS`, `shapeOf`, `shadeMap`) is taken out everywhere; instead, at the gate Christian is a near-black figure ([16,13,13], by `gateish`) against the lit wall and the light from within, like Good-will. (2) The door leaf lightens as it swings in: its dark wash thins and the light from within falls on it, strongest at its free edge. (3) His flat shadow on the road, away from the open door, is near-black and a little longer. The other chats (Level 3, the one with Level 4's notes) were told before and after. Nothing spent.

**Build 227: the gate wall, three points (the owner, in the other small-tweaks chat, asked "What do you think of wall now?" and said "Yes please do" to that chat sending them here; checked in that chat's own record).** (1) **Too much lit with the door open** (the right half glowed to the top corners): the door's light falls off quickly from the low narrow opening (`WALL_LIGHTS` 'D': two points at 32 and 80 units, 22 out, `R` 78). (2) **The stones read as crumpled paper or bark:** the tile is laid larger (640 units, was 380), the photograph's heights are softened (a small weighted blur) and pushed apart with a smooth curve (stones higher, joints deeper, tops not flattened), the relief deeper (`Hs` 6.5) and the shadows longer (18 steps toward the light, each starting at a different fraction of a step so they do not streak). Tried and taken out in this build: a hard cut at the photograph's darkest quarter (flat-topped stones with blotchy joints) and stones built as domes from the distance to the nearest joint (the joints are not clean enough in the photograph: ink blots). (3) **The left third went flat:** the bake covers 1000 units left of the gate to 500 right (`RELIT`, faded over its last 160 units), and a dim cool light from the dusk sky above (`dir`, `k` .2) lets the stone go on into the dark. The bake is about 2.4 million points at 24 rows a frame, under the veil of mist. Plan B (one large non-repeating wall photograph) is still held. Nothing spent.

**Build 231 (owner: "Wall looks worse than before - I think christian looks too dark").** The wall's bake is back to build 225's exactly (build 227's larger, softened stones, the quick fall-off of the door's light and the dim sky light are gone; the three points of build 227 stay open). Christian at the gate is mixed only .7 of the way to a dark warm brown ([30,24,22]), so he reads dark against the light with his robe and burden still showing. Nothing spent.

**Build 234: Good-will's own figure in the doorway** (the owner approved the figure in the other small-tweaks chat, "Looks good to me", checked in that chat's record; that chat asked this one to put it in): `assets/goodwill-stand.webp` replaces the stand-in (Evangelist's frames without the staff), mirrored to face Christian, at the stand-in's size (`FK` .86), dark against the light as before, breathing in code (`breath(3)`). Nothing spent.

**Build 235: the wall from one big photograph, one set of lights (owner: "A make the lighting here realistic and consistent among figures and items . Let's go for A for on wall for now !"; A of three photographs offered).** (1) **The wall** is a photograph of the stone-and-flint wall of St Peter's church, Ash, Surrey (Wikimedia Commons, "St Peter's Church, Ash Church Road, Ash (May 2014) (Stone and Flint Wall).JPG", **CC0**; fetched as the 3840 px thumbnail, the original answered 429): its daylight shading evened out (divided by a wide blur, softly), contrast deepened (gamma 1.35), colour halved, a little darker at the foot; its right 360 px blended into its left so it repeats without a seam (mirroring was tried and showed an inkblot symmetry); `assets/gate-wall.webp` (1040 x 1011, about 300 KB), laid 560 units wide from the road up (`GWALL`, `wallPat`). The stones keep their own real relief. Build 222's height-map bake (`stepBake`, `RELIT`, `WALL_LIGHTS`) is removed. (2) **One set of lights for everything at the gate:** a faint cool dusk over the whole wall (so the stone is just there in the dark), the lantern's warm pool (brightest just round it, falling off quickly), and the door's light from the opening as it opens; the wall picture is laid over them by 'multiply'. The surround keeps its smooth dressed stone under the same lights. **The figures follow the same lights:** Christian takes the door's warm light on the side that faces it and the lantern's warmth on his head and shoulders (`FIRE` in `figure()`, now with optional `warm`/`cool` colours; at the gate warm [255,206,140], cool [30,26,34]); Good-will, lit from behind, stays dark with the light wrapping his edges. Nothing spent. `gate-stone.webp` stays for the surround's grain and the carved stone's close-up.

**Build 236 (owner: "Too big of rocks need to find a much bigger swatch of rocks . Also need to go back to Rembrandt style lighting that we had before !").** (1) **A wider swatch:** no free photograph found shows more than about a metre and a half of wall straight on (searched Commons' "Flint walls" and others; the wide ones are in perspective or CC BY-SA), so two photographs by the same photographer of the same kind of Surrey stone-and-flint wall are joined: Ash (build 235's) and St Mary the Virgin's, Worplesdon ("St Mary the Virgin's Church, Worplesdon Road, Worplesdon (May 2014) (Stonework).JPG", **CC0**; fetched as the 3840 px thumbnail), each graded the same way, blended where they meet, the ends blended so it repeats sideways and the top blended into the foot so it repeats upward: `assets/gate-wall.webp` (2052 x 750, about 310 KB), laid 640 units wide (`k = 640 / GWALL.width`, pattern 'repeat'), so the stones are about two thirds of build 235's size. (2) **The Rembrandt dark again:** the dusk fill nearly gone (.12), and the lantern's and the door's pools fall off as steeply as build 225's lit stones did (several stops: full at the lamp, .4 at 30% of the radius, .12 at 55%, nothing beyond), so most of the wall is dark and the stone is seen only in the two pools. Other CC0 flint photographs of the same series, if wanted later: Ripley ("St Mary Magdalen's Church, High Street, Ripley (May 2014) (Flintwork).JPG") and Nepcote ("Detail old flint wall at Nepcote.JPG"). Nothing spent.

**Build 239 (owner: "Looks fantastic - maybe one more go over to make wall really look like one . Also another go over on lighting thinking big picture about what is realistic and natural and beautiful (stunning!) and also artistic (Rembrandt ish)!!").** (1) **One wall:** the Worplesdon photograph's stones were about half the size of Ash's, and its mortar lighter: it is now scaled up 1.7 times (cropped to the same height) and its tones matched to Ash's (luminance by histogram, colour by channel means) before the two are graded, joined and blended as before; `assets/gate-wall.webp` is 2845 x 750 (about 430 KB), laid 885 units wide (the stones the size of build 236's). (2) **The light, thought through (Rembrandt: one light rules at a time, light behaves as real light):** the lantern rules until the door opens (it warms his head and shoulders from straight above: `FIRE.x` the lantern, cool side a warm dark brown [30,22,18]); then the light from within takes over and the lantern is the lesser light (its haze dims). Real light's own touches: the lantern's roof stops its light going straight up, so a soft dark cone rises up the wall above it (three nested soft wedges); when the door opens its low light throws Christian's shadow onto the wall behind him, large (2.1 times, about a point 48 units up in the doorway), soft (his own last drawn picture, darkened, made at a quarter size and stretched up: `CHRSH`, captured in `figure()` for his slots `CHR_SLOTS` while `gateHere`; `SHC`) and leaning away from the light, kept left of the surround; a faint wider halo round the lantern in the evening air. Nothing spent.

**Build 241 (owner: "Ok looking good (I kinda liked last version slightly more ) the issue is the rocks seem too big for wall is there a way to create more repetition or bring in other pictures so all rocks can be much smaller ??").** Four CC0 photographs of flint walls, each scaled so its stones are about the same size (about 64 px before halving) and its tones matched to Ash's: Ash and Worplesdon (as before), Ripley ("St Mary Magdalen's Church, High Street, Ripley (May 2014) (Flintwork).JPG", flints and dark pebble patches) and Nepcote ("Detail old flint wall at Nepcote.JPG", the brick pier and coping cropped off); laid as two rows (Ash + Worplesdon's upper part; Ripley + Nepcote + Worplesdon's lower part), blended at every seam (110 px across, 80 px between the rows), the ends and the top and foot blended so it repeats both ways, graded as before, then halved: `assets/gate-wall.webp` 1002 x 360 (about 117 KB), laid 470 units a tile (`k = 470 / GWALL.width`), so the stones are about 15 units, near real size beside him (they were about 35). The light is unchanged from build 239. Nothing spent.

**Build 249: the gate after Rembrandt, a second pass (owner asked whether a piece of wood should hold the lantern, whether it matches Rembrandt and whether the lighting is spectacular; my answer, "about halfway", and the plan, agreed: "Yes!").** (1) **The lantern hangs from a wrought-iron arm** (a plate on the wall, the arm out over the lantern ending in a curl, a scrolled brace, a short hook; iron, not wood, by a flame), the lantern's iron darker, its horn pane a deeper mottled amber with a small hot flame, a soft glow over the pane; the lantern's halo is laid before the iron, so the iron stays black. (2) **Christian is a dark figure** (`tintM` .86 toward [24,19,17]) **with a gold edge on the side toward the open door** (`RIM.gold`: a deeper, warmer rim and a stronger bloom), as Rembrandt lights one side and lets the rest fall into dark. (3) **The stream of light is laid BEHIND him** (it was laid over him and washed him pale beige): moved into the gate block. (4) **Tighter pools, the eye adjusting:** the lantern's pool 230 units (was 300), the door's 210 (was 260); as the door opens the lantern's pool sinks by nearly half and the dusk fill goes, so the doorway is the one bright thing; the road's pools narrower (300, was 470), the road at the gate darker with no pale edge. (5) His shadow thrown on the wall is smaller and softer (1.5 times, at a seventh of the size, .42): at 2.1 times it read as a straight dark band left of the lantern. Nothing spent.

**Build 250: the lantern's arm comes out at us (owner: "Looks amazing . Can we make wrought patterned holder come out at us instead of going right").** The arm stands straight out from the wall toward us, seen a little from below and from the side: a quatrefoil iron plate with four rivets on the wall above the lantern, the arm short and thicker toward its near end, lit along its underside, a scrolled brace from lower on the wall, a narrow curl at the near end (seen nearly edge-on), the hook. When the door opens its light throws the lantern's and the arm's shadow up and left onto the wall (soft: made small and stretched up), which shows it hangs off the wall. Nothing spent. (Two commits carry the number 250: the Level 3 edits chat's touched strikes and this one; pushed a minute apart.)

**Build 252: Rembrandt's warm darks at the gate (owner: "Wasn't Rembrandt more reddish or is this the right color scheme?"; shown the colours side by side with a warmer still: "Let's do it").** Rembrandt's darks are warm browns leaning to red, and his light falls from gold through orange to red-brown before the black; ours had cool blue-black darks. Now, by `gateish`: the sky's top and low tones mixed .75 toward [22,11,8] and [74,34,22]; the wall's base fill [22,13,10]; the road at the gate [17,9,7] (.75); the dark round the picture's edges [16,7,5]; the lantern's and the door's pools fall off through red-brown ([214,116,60], [150,62,32], [104,38,20]; door [200,104,56], [124,48,26]); Christian a shade warmer ([30,15,11]). It leans toward Level 4's planned Prodigal Son red ochre and gold, so the two levels meet at the gate. Nothing spent.

**Build 254: the wall and the door a little softer (owner: "Ok everything looks amazing - can you soften texture of rocks and door ever so slightly so it looks more watercolor and less realistic").** As each picture loads, `soften(im, f, a, wrap)` lays over it a copy made three times smaller and stretched back up, at half strength: the stones and planks keep their shapes but lose the photograph's crisp grit. The wall repeats, so it is softened from a 3 x 3 tiling and its edges still meet. Nothing spent.

**Build 255: softer still, less contrast (owner: "Yeah turn it up slightly more - still a bit too high contrast for me").** `soften()` now uses a copy four times smaller laid at .7 (was three times, .5), and a new `flat` (.22 for the wall and the door) lays the picture's own mean colour over it, so its darks and lights come closer together. Nothing spent.

**Build 416: THE WHEAT IN FRONT, A TRIAL (owner: "Worldly wise comes to Christian in a field no?" ... "we could use grass from level one and he could cross from in front of us to Christian" ... "Wheat looks pretty good actually - you could probably use as it is, no? Maybe try both and see which looks better").** Only with `?wheat=1` (the storyboard's wheat: loose stalks and nodding ears, haze between the rows) or `?wheat=2` (Level 1's crop: an even-topped body, fine awns, ears, stems); **without it Level 3 is unchanged.** Three rows lie in front of the road on the plain (`WROWS`: [parallax, ground below the road line, height]; the first row's ears just at Christian's feet, the nearer ones taller and darker; baked once as seamless tiles, `wheatTiles()`, fair and dark copies; faded with `mono`, so gone among the rocks). Mr. Worldly Wiseman ("come crossing over the field to meet him", `{37}`) wades through them deep (`wDepth`: between the second and the nearest row, wheat to the knee, slower while wading) and steps up onto the road over his last half-stride before the oak; rows nearer than him are drawn over him (`wiseDraw`, drawn among the rows while `d > 0`). Seen and open: variant 1's stalks still show thin pale gaps (it was painted soft in the storyboard); his deep stretch is short (he is seen only once near). Nothing spent.

**Build 419: THE WHEAT KEPT, LEVEL 1'S KIND (owner: "Yes like 2 best, maybe ever so slight up and down like 1 (even more slight than 1)").** The wheat of build 416 is now always there (`?wheat=0` hides it, for comparing); the storyboard variant is removed; the crop's top rises and falls gently along its length (`wave(x) * .4`, about 4 units each way, under half of variant 1's). Nothing spent.

**How it goes (`{37}` to `{60}`):** alone on the plain; he sees one far off (`SEEN`) → Wiseman walks in and they meet at the lone oak where the ways cross (`MEET`), the talk (`WISE`) → "Do you see that hill?" (act `'hill'`): the camera draws far back, the child touches the hill → he turns out of his way (`aside`): the sky darkens, the far light slips behind the hill, a bent, low tune (`WRONG`), holding moves him less and less → he stands still under the overhang (`still` at `STILL`); fire and thunder (`fire()`); he sweats and shakes → Evangelist comes (`come`; he has no walk, so he appears out of smoke) → the talk (`EVANW`): Christian falls at his feet (the weeping video) and is raised → back with haste: holding runs him back to the crossing (`back`, his plain run) → a veil of mist ("in process of time", `mist`, automatic) → the wall and the little wicket-gate under the far light (`gate`) → act `'knock'`: the child knocks three times on the gate → Good-will comes; the door opens a hand's breadth of light → end card "At the gate" (Level 4 begins with Good-will).

**Code map:** places `START`, `MEET`, `BROW0`, `CREST`, `BROW1`, `PATH0`, `FOOT` (= `STILL`), `HX0`, `EV_X`, `GATE_X`, `GATE_FROM`, `GATE_STOP`, `HILL_F`, `VIL_W`, `PH`, `HSF`, `HSN`, `Z_NEAR`, `Z_WIDE`; words `SEEN`, `WISE`, `TURNED`, `HIGH`, `STOOD`, `COMING`, `EVANW`, `HASTE`, `ATGATE`, `KNOCK`; phases `ph` (`plain`, `meet`, `aside`, `down`, `path`, `still`, `come`, `evan`, `back`, `mist`, `gate`, `knock`, `open`); `C3`, `wise`, `evan`; moods `dark`, `hope`, `flash`, `veil`, `door`; `walk(dir, v)`, `sawHill`, `knock`, `turnC`, `hillPt`/`gatePt` (`HL.pt('hill'|'gate')` for tests), `HL.title()`, `HL.peek()`.

**Wiseman has his own figure (build 149; the owner liked the look: "Ok sounds good"):** `assets/wiseman.pack` (a 37-frame walk from one video, about 56 cents, right first time: made from his standing painting placed small in a white 1280 x 720 picture, "walking steadily to the LEFT ... the camera tracks with him ... does not zoom"; the video zoomed in during its first second anyway, and the loop is cut from the steady part; the video was MIRRORED before `tools/video-frames.py`, because the tool measures foot slide for figures walking right, and scaled to 85% with white padding, because the tool's crop window (488 video px) cut off his hat) and `assets/wiseman-stand.webp` (the painting mirrored, cut with `tools/cutout.py`, ground at the higher, front foot). In `hill.js`: `WIS`, `WIS_META` (`rest` 2 and 19: both feet down), `WIS_ST`, `WIS_V` (his video's own pace), `wFig()` (Obstinate's pictures stand in until his load). He keeps the stand-in's warmer, paler tone. **Bug fixed with it:** he used to appear already past his meeting place, so he never walked and stood behind Christian's stopping place (the two ended back to back); he now starts at `MEET + 508`, is seen coming across the field, and waits at `MEET + 135`. Lines: 37 of 53 recorded (the rest stopped at Google's daily limit: run `tools/make-voice.py` again). **Evangelist walks up to him (build 156, the owner said yes; one video, about 56 cents, right first time; plus two failed picture tries at levelling his feet, about 19 cents):** `assets/evangelist-walk.pack` (39 frames, facing right, `EVW`, `EVW_META` with `vpx` 5.0 MEASURED BY HAND: the tool said 9.9; `rest` 0 and 20), from his standing frame 0 with its feet levelled by `tools/flat-feet.py` and the flat-side-view prompt: planted feet now within 2 to 3 px of each other (his old frames: 10). He comes out of the haze from `EV_X - EV_FROM` at his video's pace and his standing frames dissolve in at `EV_X` (`evan.walk`, `evan.st`, `evan.x`); without the pack the old smoke appearance remains. **His standing frames (`assets/evangelist-stand.pack`, also Level 1's field) were levelled too.** `assets/evangelist.pack` (Level 1's turn and point, 80 frames) was levelled next (owner: "Yes please"): the higher foot comes down from about 297 to about 306, beside the near foot at about 311, through the turn too; the shoes are a little taller where stretched, fine at game size. The hand-taking is not shown. Wiseman's standing picture was levelled the same way (its front foot was 8 px high).

**Build 366: LEVEL 3'S WORDS CUT IN HALF, THE PICTURE TELLS THE REST (owner: "Can you shorten the dialogue in this level - it seems like a lot of waiting and more time could be spent on visualizations"; chosen line by line in `level3-cuts.html`: "everything else as proposed", keeping #11 "So Christian turned out of his way", ideas A to F).** 31 lines became 16; measured from the recordings, about 3:04 of speech became about 1:25; Evangelist's talk 71 s to about 40 s. Cut: the storyteller naming Wiseman (`SEEN` empty; Evangelist names him later), "Who sent you this way?" and its answer, "I see the mud of the Slough ...", "Sir, which way is his house?", "Flashes of fire ...", "Now he was sorry he had listened ...", "Then Christian fell down at his feet, crying:" (the kneel shows it, now begun on "Woe is me"), "At the sight of this, Evangelist caught him by the right hand" (shown), "Then Christian revived a little ..." (the rise shows it), "He hurried like one treading on forbidden ground ..." (`HASTE` empty), the arrival at the gate and the reading of the sign (`ATGATE` empty), "He knocked, more than once or twice, saying:" (now a quiet TIP "Knock on the gate."). Reworded (8 new recordings, about 8 cents, all first takes): "To that little gate ahead, to be rid of my burden."; "There is no more dangerous way in all the world!"; "Do you see that hill? Go by it. The first house you come to is his." (the touch on the hill follows); "The hill hung so far over the way that Christian was afraid it would fall on his head."; "What are you doing here, Christian? You are out of the way."; "That man was Worldly Wiseman. Mr. Legality cannot free you."; "Sir, is there hope? May I go back to the gate?"; Evangelist says "God-speed." himself. Shown instead: (A) at the foot the overhang creaks, the ground jolts and five small stones come down beside him one after another before the line, and the pull back waits a little (`stillT` 150); (B) **EVANGELIST'S HAND**: after "Woe is me" he reaches out his hand (`evan.g`, frames 40 to 58 of his Level 1 pointing, `EVA`, now loaded by Level 3; `evFig()`), a faint warm light gathers round it, and the child touches it (TIP "Touch Evangelist's hand.", act `'ehand'`, `handPt()`, `takeHand()`: a warm chord and the mercy light), as Help's hand in Level 2; he draws it back as Christian rises; (C) the fire sinking at "the man at the gate will receive you" (kept); (E) on "God-speed." he lifts his hand a little (`evan.bye`), and the mercy light lingers where he stands while Christian hurries off (at .55, warm only on the ground there); (D) stones fall behind him as he runs back (`backFallT`), and in the farewell wide shot the far light shows again low on the left (`fw`); (F) the stone over the gate glints now and then until it is touched (`signSeen`). His head lifting toward the overhang (part of A) is NOT done: Level 3's figures have no head tilt yet. Departures added: the child takes Evangelist's hand (the book: he "caught him by the right hand"); Evangelist says "God-speed" himself (the book reports it); the far light returning is ours. `tools/stills3.js` now passes any act after a while and takes `PORT`; `tools/phone-test-3.js` touches the hand. Checked: the playtest and the real-touch test reach the end card, no page errors; before and after stills of the foot, the hand, God-speed, the run back, the farewell and the gate.

**Build 367: FOUR EASTER EGGS (owner: "need new easter eggs and things to show (like dodging rocks or something ;)"; chose 1, 3, 4 and 7 of eight offered). Nothing can be failed or missed for good.** (1) **A stone that misses him** (`ROLLX`, `ROLL`, `rollers()`): twice on the rocky path (3420, 4020) a stone tumbles down off the heights onto the way AHEAD of him, aimed where he will be a moment later plus a step (`C3.lastV`, set in `walk()`): holding on, it crashes down just in front of him (dust, a thud, the ground jolts), bounces high and drops off the path's near edge behind the nearest crags; letting go, it lands further ahead. Drawn nearer than the figures, before the nearest crags. Kept within the rule "no obstacles": there is no way to be hit. (3) **Lizards** (`LIZ`, `lizards()`, `scurry()`): two on the top of the path's stony far edge (near 3420 and 4080, at the highest stone there), dark silhouettes with a thin warm line of firelight along the back, head bobbing now and then; as he comes near or at a touch one darts away along the edge (a tiny scratch of claws) and is gone. (4) **The oak** (`oakTouch()`, `OBIRD`, `OLEAF`): a touch on its crown sends a bird out (flutter and two chirps) flying left over the field, and shakes down a few leaves; on the plain only. (7) **Three hidden notes** (`HIDDEN`, `hiddenTouch()`): faint lights in crevices along the path's far edge (3260, 3760, 4240); a touch sings one note of the hymn's first words ("A-maz-ing", D G B) bent flat, as the tune goes wrong off the way, and the light drifts up and fades; (build 375: no longer sung again in tune once all three are found). Touches reach them only while no act is waiting (`touch()`). Tests: `HL.pt('oak')`, `HL.pt('hid')`, `HL.peek().eggs`. Checked: stills of the stone mid-fall and bouncing in front of him, the lizard close up, a hidden light before and after its touch, the bird; the playtest and the real-touch test reach the end card. `level3-cuts.html` deleted (owner: "yes delete cuts page"). Nothing spent.

**Build 369: QUICKER, WITH MUSIC AND LIGHTS (owner: "ways to keep viewer engaged ... a little more fast paced ... music lights etc"; then "Let's do your pick": 1, 3, 4, 6, 7 of ten offered). Nothing spent.** (1) **Shorter empty stretches:** the gate is 9000 (was further), the brow nearer the meeting (`BROW0` = `MEET + 420`), the rocky path 3330 to 4400 (`PATH0`, `FOOT`), the falling stones, rolling stones, lizards and hidden lights moved in with it, Evangelist walks in from 400 off (`EV_FROM`). (3) **Shorter waits:** the pull back starts at `stillT` 120 and takes 175 ticks, its hold ends about 4 s after the words (or 17 s at most, a touch adds about 3 s), the way back in 140; the farewell shot 340 ticks; the veil of mist quicker (.018 a tick); the end card at `stillT` 280. (4) **A music bed made in the browser** (`PAD`, `BED`, `padTick()`): four soft voices (sine and a detuned triangle through a 700 Hz lowpass) holding one chord per phase and gliding between them: G major on the plain, a flattened seventh off the way, a low D minor on the path that grows as he nears the foot, darker and louder under the hill, a warm chord when Evangelist's mercy light gathers, G major back, and a bright one as the door opens; halved while anyone speaks; a watchdog fades it out if the level stops (`HL.on`) or ticks stop coming. No hymn in it. (6) **The hymn as lights in Level 3** (`LITES`, `layLights()`, `lightsLife()`): the first phrase of Amazing Grace laid along the road from the start (sung one note a light as he passes, each drifting up into the sky), none off the way (the tune is lost there), and the first phrase again along the wall leading to the gate. (7) **A glint the first time each touchable thing is seen** (`GLINTS`, `SEEN_G`, `glint()`, `glints()`): the oak's crown (once more later if not touched), each lizard, each hidden light (once more), the tray on the plain; a four-pointed sparkle with a high soft chime. Checked: the playtest and the real-touch test reach the end card; oscillators start (a headless count) and the bed is running. **Also fixed, a fault of build 366:** the wide shot at the foot waited for the words to change from "The hill hung ..." (build 366 cut the line that followed), so on a phone it held its full 40 s with no "Touch the cloud" hint; now it waits only for the line to be said. The real-touch test reaches the end card in 355 s (was 595). **I cannot hear it: the owner's ears decide its level and character.** **Build 374 (owner: "i do not want to overplay amazing grace"):** the hymn's lights of (6) are taken out again (`LITES` gone): no lights along the road on the plain or at the gate. **Rule: do not add more of Amazing Grace; it stays a thing discovered now and then.** What is left of it in Level 3: the tune bending flat off the way, and the three hidden notes on the rocky path (easter egg 7). Nothing spent. **Build 375 (owner, agreeing to my advice: "yes"):** finding all three hidden notes no longer plays them again in tune; each still sings its one bent note. **The hymn is saved to come right, whole and in tune, at the Cross (Level 5), when the burden falls.**

**Departures from the book in Level 3 (checked line by line in build 140):** the talk with Wiseman cut to its bones (his question about wife and children, his first advice to be rid of the burden with speed, "how came you by the burden", the remedy "at hand", Civility, the empty houses and cheap provision left out); his list of dangers shortened and "death" left out of it; Evangelist's questioning of what Wiseman said (`{49}`, `{50}`) and most of his sermon (the verse about refusing him that speaks, the three things to abhor, Legality as the bond-woman's son, Civility) left out; Evangelist's "severe and dreadful countenance" not shown; the words spoken out of the mountain left out (fire and thunder only); Evangelist calling to the heavens left out; "fell down at his feet as dead" made "fell down at his feet", and "looked for nothing but death" before his crying out left out; his question to Evangelist shortened ("Shall I not be abandoned for this?" dropped); Evangelist's kiss left out (the smile and God-speed kept); the lone oak at the crossing, the veil of mist between the crossing and the gate, and the wall beside the wicket-gate are ours; three knocks stand for "more than once or twice"; evening at the gate (build 139; the book only says "in process of time"); Christian's knocking verse cut to its first question; **build 164 (less waiting):** cut: "he saw one far off", Wiseman's "Will you listen to me ..." and Christian's answer, Wiseman's list of dangers and Christian's reply, Christian's moment of doubt (the storyteller), "Yes, very well", "There he stood still", "his burden seemed heavier", "Here he sweated", "he saw Evangelist coming" (he is seen walking up), "he stood speechless", "Are you not the man ..." and its answer, "Did I not show you the way ..." and its answer, "Stand still a little ...", "The just shall live by faith ...", the fire out of the mountain after the hand-taking, "a thousand fools", "Only take heed ...", "So he went on with haste"; the rest of Wiseman's and Evangelist's lines reworded shorter and plainer ("Who sent you this way?", "Sir, is there hope? May I go back to the gate?", "May I come in? Will he open to me, sorry as I am?"); the verses Bunyan sets between sections (`{46}`, `{60}`) are left out. **Open questions for the owner:** the hill's look (a painted hill?); how frightening the fire; whether the geography reads (out to the hill and back, then mist to the gate).

**THE OWNER'S RULINGS FROM THE VISION CHAT (after build 393; clicked answers in the app; see `vision/README.md`).** (1) **Put back the why** (done in build 397, below). (2) **Good-will's promise** ({68}) is said by Good-will in Level 3's doorway until Level 4's gate scene is built. (3) **Christian after the Cross:** new pictures made into free clips (walk, stand, kneel without the burden, in a new coat), not painted out, not the mannequin. (4) **Level 4's look:** code-drawn rooms to the standard of Level 3's gate, the game's own Christian steered from room to room, each master kept as its light and one quoted object. (5) **The dark parts:** ruled level by level as each is built (no single switch). (6) **Ignorance's end:** kept, quietly (he finds no roll, the gate stays shut, he is led toward a dark door in the hill; no words of hell). (7) **Sixteen levels:** the Flatterer and the Enchanted Ground joined, the lions open Level 7, and Bunyan's "So I awoke ... And I slept, and dreamed again" as a card after the Delectable Mountains. (8) **Music after the Cross:** a second, walking tune for the road (an old free tune); Amazing Grace silent but for one remembered phrase at three moments of grace, then whole on the trumpets at the Celestial Gate.

**Build 397: THE WHY PUT BACK (owner: "Let's put back the why"; about 15 cents of voices, the owner said yes).** The vision chat's book audit found the game faithful in order and words but missing the sentences that say why. Twelve lines, recorded in the same voices (two retakes of the song), one new designed voice for Good-will (`GW`, "a grave, gentle old English man in his seventies, the keeper of a gate"); `VOICE_V` 12. Level 1: Christian to Evangelist "Sir, this book tells me our city will be destroyed, and this burden on my back will sink me." ({11}); at his door "He had told his wife and children of the danger, but they would not believe him. Now they saw him run." ({11}, {18}); "At the edge of the city stood the city gate." (so it is not taken for the wicket-gate). Level 2: "Its name was the Slough of Despond."; Pliable "Is this the happiness you told me of? You can have your fine country to yourself!" ({31}). Level 3: the greeting "To that little gate ahead. I cannot get this burden off myself." ({39}); Evangelist "That man was Worldly Wiseman. No man was ever rid of his burden by Mr. Legality." ({57}); at the gate Christian reads the stone, "Knock, and it shall be opened unto you." ({59}); as the door opens Good-will says "Bear your burden a little longer. At the place of deliverance, it will fall from your back of itself." ({68}). Level 5: LOOKED is two lines, "He stood still to wonder, that the sight of the cross should ease him of his burden." / "He looked, and looked again ..." ({94}); after the three leaps Christian says his song, "Blest cross! Blest sepulchre! Blest rather be the Man that there was put to shame for me!" ({94}). **Departures added:** Good-will's promise is said at the gate as he opens (in the book, inside, after the narrow way is shown, {68}); Christian reads the stone aloud. Checked: playtests 1, 2, 3 and 5 reach their end cards.
