The tunnel, the door and the footprint use the same point. Nothing spent.

**Build 465: the door in the middle of the passage, with thickness (owner: "can you put door in middle of wall cavity instead of one side. can you also give door some thickness and have it rotate correctly (towards doorman) when it opens").** The oak door hangs at the middle of the passage (`xm` = `X(GATE_X)`), hinged on the passage's far side, a slab `DT` 7 units thick; it swings in toward Good-will about its hinge. Each point of it is `pt(r, side)` (r from the hinge, side -1 the outer face, +1 the inner); drawn are its free edge (the planks' cut end, a lighter strip) and its outer face in strips of `GDOOR` with three iron straps. The inner half of the passage keeps the light from within once the door has been opened (the inside stays lit after it shuts); the outer half is lit only while it stands open; the inner opening a warm [222,150,98], not white. Nothing spent.

**Build 467: a quieter edge of light on Christian at the gate (owner: "the light outline on christian doesn't seem realistic").** `RIM.gold` (the light from within on his side toward the door): the edge 1.8 picture pixels deep (was 4.6), softer and warmer, at .6 (was .95), its bloom .16 (was .9), so he is a dark shape with a faint warm edge, as Good-will is. **A `//` comment written before code on the same line swallowed the rim's cut-out while making this (he showed as a pale see-through wash): caught in the stills and fixed. The rule in "Lessons" stands.** Nothing spent.

**Build 468: further from the door, solid knocks, the light behind the hills (owner: "make it so christian can't get too close to door. also make the knocks in the game more solid sounding"; "the sun shoudn't be on ground on that side either").** (1) He stops a step short of the wall (`GATE_STOP` = `GATE_X - 118`, was -74); the pull walks him the longer way through, and the door begins to shut only once he is past it (`C3.x > GATE_X + 50`). (2) **`knockWood(when, k)`** in `index.html` (beside `sweep`/`hiss`): a heavy oak door's low falling thump, the wood's tock, the dull mass of the hand, a tiny knuckle click; each knock is two raps (`knockWood(0, 1); knockWood(.17, .85)`), at the wicket-gate (`knock()` in `hill.js`) and at the Interpreter's door (`knockS` in `rooms.js`, which falls back to its old sound if the helper is missing). (3) Inside the gate the far light is drawn before the land, so its foot sinks behind the far rises; the warm glow over the land lies on those rises (`Y(-150)`), not on the road. Nothing spent.

**Build 469: THE GARDEN INSIDE THE GATE, IN TRUE PERSPECTIVE (owner: "if the walls vanishing point is where it is the trees are too short - do perspective analysis and something has to change - what do you think before you code?", then "yes, go ahead").** The analysis: the vanishing point is the eye's height, about 100 units (Christian's chest, a child's eye); a thing `d` behind the road stands on the line `gvy() * (1 - q)` and is drawn `q` = 1 / (1 + d / `GPL`) its size; the old trees (bases 30 up, so under 2 m behind the road, drawn 45 to 70 tall) were bushes, not trees. Now, inside the gate (`THE GARDEN INSIDE` in the inside land block, `at(wx, d)`, `tree(wx, d, H, sd, col, flip)`): two oaks near the path at their real size (540 and 470 units tall, 150 and 210 behind the road, at `GATE_X + 470` and `+ 1150`, right of where the two men stand so they read against the light); a low garden wall across the picture 1100 behind the road (125 tall, a capped top touched by the light); two trees beyond it (1500, 1700); nine small hedgerow trees on the far rises (3600 to 4500). Each tree is the level's own painted oak (`OAKP.c`) in one flat tone (`GTREE`, a tinted half-size copy per tone), mirrored by turns, hazier with distance (`hz`); a soft stand-in shape until the oak loads. The drawn horizon haze (-140) stays a little above the eye's height (the far rises read as hills). Nothing spent.

## Level 4 opens at the Wicket-Gate (Good-will), book `{60}` to `{69}`, then the Interpreter's House: PLAN ONLY, not yet built

**THE GATE IS SHARED WITH LEVEL 3 (owner, build 219: "other chats are working on gate and it may need updating").** Level 3 ends at the wicket-gate and Level 4 opens at the same gate, so there is ONE gate: Level 3's (`hill.js`: `GDOOR`, `GSTONE`, the drawn lantern `LANT`, the stone surround, the sign `signPt()`/`showSign()`, Good-will's stand-in `GWF`; builds 215, 216, 219; build 222 relit the wall from a height map (removed in build 235) and drew the lantern in code, `bakeLantern`/`LANT`; build 225 made Christian near-black at the gate, the door leaf lightening as it opens, his road shadow darker, and took build 222's light and shade off the figures; build 227's wall changes were taken back in build 231 (the owner: "Wall looks worse than before"), and Christian at the gate is dark but no longer near-black; build 235 made the wall a big photograph of a stone-and-flint churchyard wall, `GWALL`, under one set of lights shared by the wall, the surround, the door and the figures; build 236 joined a second photograph to it, made the stones smaller and brought back the deep Rembrandt dark; build 239 matched the two photographs into one wall and added the lantern's roof shadow and Christian's shadow thrown on the wall by the open door; build 241 made every stone small, a patchwork of four photographs; build 249 hung the lantern from an iron arm (build 250: the arm comes out at us; build 252: warm red-brown darks, after Rembrandt), made Christian dark with a gold edge, put the stream of light behind him and tightened the pools), which the Level 3 chat may still be changing. **Level 4 must not copy it:** when Level 4 is built, take the gate's drawing out of `hill.js` into a part both levels call (or export it from `HL`), so any later change to the gate shows in both levels at once. Until then, every Level 4 sketch of the gate is made from a fresh still of Level 3's gate (as `concept/level4-gate-prodigal.jpg` was, from build 219) and may need making again. **Anyone changing the gate: say so in this paragraph, and check Level 4's plans still fit.** Level 4's own changes to the gate are only its colour and light (the Prodigal Son's red ochre and gold, `concept/level4-gate-prodigal.jpg`), the door opened wide, and Good-will's own figure (the stand-in `GWF` is Evangelist's frames; Good-will's hands reaching for Christian quote the father's hands in the Prodigal Son).

**GOOD-WILL'S OWN FIGURE (after build 228; owner: "Looks good to me").** One picture from the image model (about 15 cents, right first time; `concept/goodwill-two-poses.png`), given Evangelist's and Help's standing frames as the style: an old man, bare-headed (so he is not taken for hooded Evangelist), thin white hair, a short white beard, a long gown, a cord belt, a heavy cloak, hands empty, in two poses. Cut free: `assets/goodwill-stand.webp` (hands folded; 115 x 312, META `{ax: 61, ground: 306}`) and `assets/goodwill-reach.webp` (leaning forward, both hands reaching out low, the father's hands in Rembrandt's Prodigal Son, for the pull in Level 4; 173 x 305, META `{ax: 62, ground: 299}`). **Both face RIGHT like every figure picture, so draw them mirrored (face -1) to face Christian.** Drawn eyes and mouth were painted out with the skin round them (OpenCV inpainting and a skin-tone fill kept inside the figure; the script is not in the repo); both cut at the same scale (`tools/cutout.py` 300 and 293); the far foot brought down with `tools/flat-feet.py` (soles within 4 px). In the game since build 234: the standing one stands in Level 3's doorway (mirrored, `FK` .86, tint [52,44,40], breathing in code; `GWST`, `GWST_META`; the stand-in `GWF` only until it loads); the reaching one waits for Level 4, where a short video (about 56 cents) could join the two poses.

**THE ROOMS, EACH AFTER A PAINTING (owner, build 219: "do all the rooms and a different Rembrandt style color scheme for each (but then have intense rooms skipped unless toggle on main page is flipped ... think elegant and clean)"; "bring in details from those painting as homages").** The mood boards: `concept/level4-moodboard.jpg` (sketches of each light) and `concept/level4-moodboard-paintings.jpg` (each beside its painting, its colours and its one detail). Gate: Rembrandt, Prodigal Son (the father's hands). Narrow way: Rembrandt, The Mill (silver evening, the mill on its rise). The house at night: Rembrandt, Rest on the Flight into Egypt (the fire under dark trees mirrored in water, the lit windows on the hill). Rooms: 1 the grave person's picture, Rembrandt's Apostle Paul (the head lifted to heaven, as the book says); 2 the dusty parlour, Vermeer's Milkmaid (the stream from the jug as the sprinkled water); 3 Passion and Patience, Chardin's Soap Bubbles (the treasure gone like a bubble); 4 the fire fed with oil, La Tour's St Joseph the Carpenter (the hand round the light); 5 the palace, Rembrandt's Night Watch (the lances, the figure lit gold in the crowd); 6 the iron cage and 7 the dream of judgment, Rembrandt's Philosopher in Meditation (the spiral stair) and Belshazzar's Feast (the hand writing in light): **these two are the darker rooms, played only when a quiet line on the contents page, "Shew the darker rooms", is marked** (off by default, kept on the device, also in the pause menu; without it the Interpreter goes from room 5 to the wall of Salvation, with no sign of a skip). Quote ONE detail per room, redrawn in the game's own layered tones; never paste a painting in (the one exception planned: the grave person's picture hangs as a painting). The paintings are public domain; the Chardin photo on the board is a visitor's, CC BY-SA, reference only.

**THE ROOMS' WORDS, AGREED (owner, Nov 17: "Ok sounds great!").** Bunyan's lines, shortened; N storyteller, I Interpreter, C Christian; ▸ = the child's act (one in every room, so no room runs more than about five lines without one). The level runs about 4 minutes (gate about 1.5, house about 2.5): accepted as longer than the usual 2. **Door** (`{70}`-`{72}`): N "Then he went on till he came to the house of the Interpreter, and knocked over and over." ▸ knock; C "Sir, the man at the gate said that if I called here, you would show me excellent things, to help me on my journey."; I "Come in. I will show you what will help you." ▸ touch the candle, his man lights it. **1 Picture** (`{72}`-`{73}`): C "What does this mean?"; I "His eyes are lifted up to heaven, the best of books is in his hand, and the world is behind his back. A crown of gold hangs over his head."; I "He is the only man the Lord has sent to be your guide in hard places. Take good heed, lest you meet some who pretend to lead you right, but their way goes down to death." **2 Parlour** (`{74}`-`{76}`): N "He led him into a very large parlour, full of dust, because it was never swept." ▸ touch the broom, the dust flies, he coughs; I "Bring the water, and sprinkle the room." ▸ touch the jug, the room is clean; C "What does this mean?"; I "This parlour is the heart of a man. The dust is his sin. He who swept first is the Law: it stirs the dust up, but cannot make the heart clean."; I "She who brought the water is the Gospel. By it the heart is made clean, fit for the King of glory to live in." **3 Passion and Patience** (`{77}`-`{79}`): N "In a little room sat two little children. The name of the eldest was Passion, and of the other, Patience."; C "Why is Passion so discontented?"; I "He must wait for his best things till next year, but he will have all now. Patience is willing to wait." ▸ touch the bag, treasure poured at Passion's feet shimmers and is gone like a bubble, rags; I "So are the men of this world: they must have all their good things now."; C "Then it is best to wait for things to come."; I "You say the truth. For the things which are seen are temporal; but the things which are not seen are eternal." **4 Fire** (`{80}`-`{82}`): N "A fire burned against a wall, and one stood by it, always casting water upon it. Yet the fire burned higher and hotter."; C "What does this mean?"; I "This fire is the work of grace in the heart. He who casts water on it is the Devil. Come, and see why it burns on." ▸ hold to walk round the back of the wall; I "This is Christ, who with the oil of his grace keeps the work going in the heart. He stands behind the wall, for it is hard for the tempted to see it." **5 Palace** (`{82}`-`{83}`): C "May we go in there?"; N "At the door stood many men who wished to go in, but dared not, for men in armour kept it."; the brave man "Set down my name, Sir." ▸ touch the writer's book; N "He put a helmet on his head, drew his sword, cut his way through them all, and pressed on into the palace."; voices from the top "Come in, come in; Eternal glory thou shalt win."; C "I think I know the meaning of this." **6 Cage ◇** (`{84}`-`{88}`): I "Stay, till I have shown you a little more."; C "What are you?"; Man "I am what I was not once."; Man "I am now a man of despair, shut up in it as in this iron cage. I cannot get out. Oh, now I cannot!"; C "How did you come to this?"; Man "I left off to watch and be sober. I hardened my heart."; I "Why, the Son of the Blessed is very pitiful."; Man "O eternity, eternity!"; C "This is fearful! God help me to watch and be sober, and to pray." **7 Dream ◇** (`{89}`-`{90}`): C "Why does this man tremble so?"; Man "This night I dreamed. The heavens grew black, it thundered, and I heard the great sound of a trumpet."; Man "I saw a man sit upon a cloud, and heard a voice: 'Arise, ye dead, and come to judgement.'"; Man "I saw many caught up into the clouds, but I was left behind. I was not ready." **Leaving** (`{91}`-`{92}`): I "Have you thought on all these things?"; C "Yes, and they put me in hope and fear."; I "The Comforter be always with you, good Christian, to guide you in the way that leads to the City."; N "The highway was fenced on either side with a wall, and that wall was called Salvation." ▸ hold to run on with the burden, then the end card. **Departures:** every child's act is ours; the picture's meaning cut to its bones ("beget children" dropped); the talk of first and last cut; most of the caged man's speech cut; the pit of hell and the burning lake left out of the dream; Belshazzar's writing hand is the painting's, not the book's (quote its light, not a hand); Christian's verse on leaving left out. **Figures (proposed, not agreed):** the Interpreter one picture (about 15 cents, only on a yes); the rest dark silhouettes or existing pictures as stand-ins. **Room 2 sketched as a still** (in the chat, nothing spent): Vermeer's cool window on the bare plaster wall, Christian and the Interpreter in the shade by the door, the damsel in the window light sprinkling from her jug, the sweeper with his broom in the settling dust, the dark boards with a flat pool of window light; stand-ins: Good-will's picture for the Interpreter, his wife's for the damsel, Help's for the sweeper. **Then the owner: "Let's really aim for painting style (even with people) - can we copy over elements from painting - also pops of nice colors from palette are welcome"**: the rooms are to look PAINTED in their master's style, people included, borrowing the painting's own objects (for the parlour: the basket and brass pail on the wall, the Delft tiles along the wall's foot, the foot warmer) and its colours (the Milkmaid's linen white, yellow, ultramarine, red). This loosens the rule "never paste a painting in" for Level 4's rooms: the painting is the style and the source of objects, redrawn by the image model over OUR layout (a flat side view), not pasted. Cutting real pieces out of the painting and laying them on a code-drawn wall was tried and read as a collage (taken out). The way that works: the image model paints over our layout with the painting as the style reference (`concept/level4-room2-draft.jpg`, a draft on the cheaper model, about 4 cents); a final plate without people, and the people painted separately to be cut out and moved. In the draft the people's faces got eyes and mouths: say "NO eyes, NO mouth" more strongly, and keep the faces plain in the cut-outs. The empty room on the better model (`concept/level4-room2-plate.jpg`, 21:9, 2K, about 13 cents) has Vermeer's light (the window on the left the one bright thing, the wall glowing near it and dimming to the right) but reads more photograph than painting, and its side walls and floorboards go back in depth. **Christian's grey watercolour pictures look pasted on it, even coloured in its light**: the rooms and the one figure the child steers must be brought into one language (asked the owner).

**BOOKMARK: THE STUNNING ROOM (owner, Nov 17: "I live the room so much I don't really want to do A ... try A but keep a big bookmark for coming back to this if it doesn't look amazing!").** `concept/level4-room2-plate.jpg` is the owner's favourite: Vermeer's light on the bare parlour, made by `gemini-3-pro-image` (21:9, 2K) from our layout and the Milkmaid. KEEP IT and come back to it before settling the rooms' look. The prompt is in this chat's record ("Paint the room again as a finished oil painting truly by Vermeer ... WIDER (21:9) and with NO PEOPLE ... THE LIGHT IS EVERYTHING ..."). Plan A (free: soften the room toward brushwork, flatten its floor, colour Christian in its light) was tried: a strong pass (oil-paint filter) made it blotchy and cheap-looking; a gentle pass hardly changes the room, and Christian still reads as a paper cut-out. **The mismatch is Christian, not the room**: the next step is a painted Christian (or the room repainted a little quieter), not a duller room. **A painted Christian (owner: "Yes let's go"; about 13 cents, right first time):** `gemini-3-pro-image` given his book-free standing picture, a crop of the painted room and the Milkmaid, asked to paint the SAME figure (pose, outline, hood, burden, flat side view, feet level, plain face, light from the left, on white): `concept/christian-painted-stand.webp` (cut in COLOUR by distance from white, edge colours taken from inside so no pale rim: `tools/cutout.py` makes figures grey, which is wrong here), and him standing in the room (now in `concept/level4-room2-all.jpg`). He belongs in the room now. **Scale (owner: "basket and window look big compared to him"):** he is now drawn 780 of the plate's 1344 rows tall (58%), the basket about a third of his height. His face is a pale blank inside the hood: the rule for Christian is a soft profile, "neither black nor blank" (fix by hand or in the next picture). If kept, his walk in this manner needs one video (about 56 cents) and serves every painted room. **The owner: "I like him - let's do cheapest next steps".** The room's people on one sheet (`gemini-3-pro-image`, 16:9, 2K, about 13 cents, right first time), given the painted Christian as the style, the room, the Milkmaid, and the old grey pictures of his wife, Help and Good-will as pose guides: the Interpreter (grey beard, dark skull-cap, Rembrandt red-brown gown, hands folded, facing right), the damsel (dressed as the Milkmaid, pouring from a red-brown jug, facing right), the man who swept (olive jerkin, broom, facing left). Cut in colour (`concept/room2-interpreter-painted.webp`, `-damsel-`, `-sweeper-`); the model drew eyes and mouths on the two men. **FACES IN THE PAINTED ROOMS (owner, Nov 17: "We can probably unplain faces"):** in Level 4's painted rooms people may have painted faces as the masters painted them; the two men's faces are kept as painted (a plain wash was tried first and taken out). The damsel's face and the painted Christian's came out blank from the model; giving them faces needs a new picture. The plain-face rule still holds for the watercolour levels (1 to 3) unless the owner says otherwise. All four standing in the room: `concept/level4-room2-all.jpg` (the Interpreter at the window, Christian by the basket, the damsel sprinkling in the light, the sweeper in the dust). Still to come: his painted walk (one video, about 56 cents), and the damsel's sprinkling and the sweeper's sweeping as small moves in code. **THEN, WATERCOLOUR (owner, Nov 17: "I think I need to tone down and move stylistically toward true rest of the game - That mean some Water color so we can bring rooms and rest of game together").** The rooms move toward the game's watercolour, keeping each master's light; with watercolour rooms the game's own Christian fits, so no painted walk is needed. Tried: a code watercolour filter over the stunning room (mean-shift washes, edge pooling, granulation) looked like a photo filter; a draft on the cheaper image model given the photographic room as layout only copied it with paper grain (about 4 cents). **Lesson: given a photographic picture as its layout, the image model copies its realism; to change the medium, give it a simple layout (a code sketch) and the game's figures as the style, and describe the light in words.**

**TWO CHATS ON LEVEL 4, ROOM BY ROOM (owner, Nov 17: "let's just work on diff rooms").** Since build 261 the whole house is built in `rooms.js` by the room-3 chat at the owner's request (the other Level 4 chat was told); before that, the Level 4 chat had room 2 (the parlour); the chat once called "Level 2 mud rework", now "Level 4 build", has room 3 (Passion and Patience) and goes on from there. Say here which room you take before starting it. **Room 3 sketched as a still** (`concept/level4-room3-passion-patience.png`, drawn in code, nothing spent): after Chardin's *Soap Bubbles*: calm umber plaster under a dark beamed ceiling, ONE small deep-set leaded window high on the left giving a soft glow and a soft breath of light across the room (no hard shapes); Passion in his ladder-back chair in the light, leaning to the gold poured at his feet, the gold rising away as bubbles, one large luminous bubble the focus (the homage); Patience upright and quiet in his chair in the half-shade, facing him; Christian and the Interpreter dark in an arched doorway on the right; the line "So are the men of this world ..." on the floor. Stand-ins: the boys are code silhouettes (they would need their own picture, about 15 cents, only on a yes), the Interpreter is Good-will's picture. **Room 3 painted (owner: "paint using style from inspiration painting ... make boys ... at same time to save $$" and "Let's go with your ideas"; one `gemini-3-pro-image` picture, 21:9 at 2K, about 13 cents, right first time):** given the Chardin (cropped from the mood board), the painted parlour (`level4-room2-plate.jpg`, so it is the same house) and the code sketch as layout; the boys painted INTO the room (they sit still), plus a second Passion in rags on a white strip at the right in the same call. Cut into `concept/level4-room3-plate.jpg` (the room: warm umber plaster, a dark beamed ceiling, one small leaded window high on the left, Passion leaning forward in his chair in the light, hands reaching to the empty floor where the gold will pour, Patience upright and quiet in the half-shade, an empty arched doorway on the right for Christian and the Interpreter's painted figures from room 2) and `concept/room3-passion-rags-painted.webp` (cut in colour: paper by lightness and low colour, every paper patch over 250 px cleared, so the gaps between the chair's slats are see-through). The plan in code: the bag, the gold pouring, the bubbles rising, the gold gone; then the ragged Passion dissolves in over the fine one. **As in room 2, the room goes back in depth** (side walls and a deep floor); the owner liked that in room 2, so it is kept. Patience's long plain coat reads a little like a gown. **REELING THE PAINTED ROOMS IN (owner: "I fear these painting rooms are diverging from style of game ... face in particular look too different"; "let's try it (please save old since I still like a lot)").** Tried free on room 3, kept beside the original (`level4-room3-plate.jpg` untouched): `concept/level4-room3-game.jpg`: (1) the boys' faces a plain wash with the profile kept (eye, brow and mouth painted out by OpenCV inpainting and a soft blur only there), Patience's pale face held down; (2) the room quieter: fine texture and cracks smoothed away (two edge-preserving passes), colour .78, highlights held down, the side walls, the deep floor and the ceiling sunk in shadow, a strong dark round the edges; (3) Christian's and Good-will's own watercolour figures (stand-ins for the Interpreter) in the doorway, multiplied to the room's dark umber. **Second pass (owner: "The bottom one is better ... the blurriness is a bit distracting ... less detailed [is fine] but out of focus look is somewhat distracting ... color Christian and interpreter slightly like room occupants"):** the smoothing is now small-scale only (one edge-preserving pass, `sigma_s` 14, then the edges sharpened back), so the room is crisp and simply less busy; the two men are coloured like the room's people: each watercolour figure's tones mapped (with more contrast) onto a ramp of the room's colours, Christian in the boys' umber-ochre, the Interpreter in a deeper Rembrandt red-brown, lit a little more on the window's side, edges softened by one pixel (no pale rim). **Third pass (owner: "somewhere in between more watercolor but still slight variation of color ... move Christian and doorman toward slightly more color (meeting in middle)"):** the two men's colour ramps are richer (Christian a warmer ochre, the Interpreter a fuller red-brown). The boys toward watercolour could NOT be done well in code: OpenCV's watercolour filter (`stylization`) made them a comic book (dark outlines), and cutting them out of the painting (GrabCut) took pieces of the wall; both taken out. The boys meeting in the middle needs them painted again as figures (about 13 cents, asked). **The boys repainted in between (owner: "Ye. Please do"; one `gemini-3-pro-image` picture, about 13 cents, right first time):** the oil plate itself was given back with Christian's and Good-will's watercolour pictures as the style and asked to repaint ONLY the three boys (the room, light, chairs and composition kept exactly) as soft watercolour figures with a slight warm umber-ochre colour and plain faces (no eyes, no mouths; Patience now in a short coat and breeches, not the gown). Saved beside the oil one: `concept/level4-room3-plate-watercolour.jpg` and `concept/room3-passion-rags-watercolour.webp`; `concept/level4-room3-game.jpg` is now made from it (the men coloured as in the third pass): all four people now sit together. **Editing the existing picture this way (give it back, change only the people) is the method for every painted room.** The parlour (room 2): painting out its men's eyes and mouths in code was tried and looks like a blur-out on such realistic faces (not kept). **Repainted (owner: "Oh 1 sounds good"; one `gemini-3-pro-image` picture, about 13 cents, right first time; done by the room 3 chat at the owner's request):** `concept/level4-room2-all.jpg` given back with Christian's and Good-will's watercolour pictures and room 3's repainted boys as the style, asked to repaint only the four people: `concept/level4-room2-all-watercolour.jpg` (the original kept). The Interpreter, Christian, the damsel and the sweeper are now soft watercolour with muted colour and plain faces; the room, window, basket, pail, tiles and foot warmer are kept, though the whole room came back a little lighter and more watercolour too. Proposed rule if the owner likes it: people in Level 4 are the game's watercolour figures with plain-wash faces (taking back the painted-faces exception above); each room keeps its painting's light, colours and one detail, quietened toward the game's layers.

**ROOM 3 IN THE GAME (build 257; owner: "Ok k let's do it!!" to building room 3 before painting more rooms; nothing spent).** `rooms.js`, object `RM`, run by the same engine (`talk`, voices, the tray); reached only by `?room=3` (open the book and it goes straight in, `goRoom()` in `index.html`); no contents line yet, since Level 4 itself (gate, narrow way, other rooms) is not built and the other Level 4 chat may shape it. The room is ONE still picture: `assets/room3.webp` (the watercolour plate, quietened in code as `level4-room3-game.jpg` was, without the men), laid at `S` .38 units a painting pixel and moved up (`OY` 35) so the boys' feet are above the words, its last rows stretched down as more floor. Christian and the Interpreter (Good-will's picture as stand-in) are separate sprites coloured in the room's palette (`room3-christian.webp`, `room3-interpreter.webp`), breathing in code, moving with their voices (`talkMove`). The words: N names the boys; C "Why is Passion so discontented?"; I answers; N "Then one came to Passion, and brought him a bag of treasure." (a leather purse appears by his chair, drawn in code); TIP "Touch the bag." (act `'bag'`) → it tips, the gold pours at his feet with small chinks and glints (`coins`), lies glinting a while (`glad`), then each coin rises as a soap bubble (`bubbles`, after Chardin), one great bubble last (`great`), and Passion in rags dissolves in over the fine boy (`room3-rags.webp`, a soft-edged patch of the same picture: the boy and his chair painted out with wall and floor from beside him, Poisson-blended, and the ragged Passion laid in, toned to the room) → I "So are the men of this world ..."; C "Then it is best to wait ..."; I "You say the truth ..." → end card "Passion and Patience". Faint motes turn in the window light. Departures: the child's touch on the bag; Passion's rejoicing and laughing Patience to scorn not shown (painted boys); the bubbles are the painting's. The new lines are not recorded (silent, passed by reading time). The editor counts it as level 4 (`ED`). Scripts to remake the pictures are in that session's scratchpad (`rm/base.py`, `rags.py`, `sprites.py`), not the repo. Still to do: the ragged Passion's face is pale; voices (about 7 cents, ask).

**THE INTERPRETER'S HOUSE IN THE GAME (build 261; owner: "Let's build these into game - work all night ... continue through all seven rooms"; budget agreed: up to $3 for pictures and voices).** All of `rooms.js` (object `RM`) is the house, in the book's order: **the door** at night (after Rembrandt's *Rest on the Flight into Egypt*: dark trees, a tiny far fire by still water, the stone house, one lit window; N "Then he went on ... knocked over and over." ▸ knock three times; the door opens on warm light, the Interpreter in it; C "Sir, the man at the gate ..."; I "Come in ...") → **1 the picture** (after Rembrandt's *Apostle Paul*: the room dark but for the servant's taper ▸ touch the candle; the light spreads, the picture of the grave person, the crown glints; C "What does this mean?"; I's two lines) → **2 the parlour** (the watercolour parlour `concept/level4-room2-all-watercolour.jpg`, its four people in the picture; dusty haze; ▸ touch the broom: dust flies, two coughs; I "Bring the water ..." ▸ touch the jug: drops fall, the dust settles; C, I, I) → **3 Passion and Patience** (as build 257) → **4 the fire** (after La Tour: a partition wall; the fire, the man casting water, steam; C, I "Come, and see ..." ▸ HOLD to go round: the camera pans and the dark behind the wall lifts on the man pouring oil; I "This is Christ ...") → **5 the palace** (after *The Night Watch*: the palace, armed men at the door, people in gold on its top, the crowd, the writer at his table; C "May we go in there?"; N; the brave man appears: "Set down my name, Sir." ▸ touch the book: a scratch of the pen, he arms himself (`room5-brave.webp` → `room5-brave-armed.webp`, one picture with both poses), strides through the guards with clashes and sparks into the lit door; "Come in, come in ..." the gold people glow; C "I think I know the meaning of this.") → **6 the cage** and **7 the dream** only when "Shew the darker rooms" is marked on the contents page (`pp-dark` in `localStorage`, the line `#darkRooms` under IV; ◇ off, ◆ on): 6 after *Philosopher in Meditation* (spiral stair, golden window, the man in an iron cage; ▸ touch the cage, an iron creak; the agreed eight lines), 7 after *Belshazzar's Feast* (the man risen from his curtained bed, trembling: the part of the picture round him is laid again a hair off, back and forth; ▸ touch the man; his dream: the room goes black, thunder, a trumpet; the light on the wall swells for the man on the cloud; fades as he was left behind) → **leaving** at the door (I "Have you thought ..."; C; I "The Comforter ..."; N "The highway ... Salvation." ▸ HOLD to go on: his own walk pictures, dark against the night, past the lit door and away) → end card "The Interpreter's House", next: the Cross (not yet built). Rooms fade through dark with two footsteps between them.
**How it is made:** each room is ONE picture by `gemini-3-pro-image` (21:9, 2K, about 13 cents each, all right first time; door and rooms 1, 4, 5, 6, 7 and the brave man's sheet: about 91 cents) given room 3's and room 2's watercolour pictures as the style, the master's painting (cropped from `concept/level4-moodboard-paintings.jpg`) for light and colour, and a flat-block layout drawn in code; the people of the room painted in; Christian and the Interpreter left out ("leave the right fifth empty") and added as the two sprites (`room3-christian.webp`, `room3-interpreter.webp`, their pale rims cleaned in build 261), coloured per room (`tone`, baked once by `toneOf`), at `k` .58 where the picture's people are smaller (the door, the palace). Each picture is quietened in code (one edge-preserving pass, colour .85, highlights held, a soft dark round the edges) and saved at .8 size: `assets/room-door.webp`, `room1.webp` ... `room7.webp`. Room 7's painted "shaking" lines were painted out. Positions in the code are in the full-size picture's pixels (3168 x 1344; room 3's is 2566 wide). The prompts and scripts are in that session's scratchpad (`h/`), not the repo.
**Code map:** `ROOMS` (`door`, `r1` ... `r7`, `leave`; each `pic`, `FY` floor line, `cam0`, `tone`, `enter`, `run`, `act`, `upd`, `under`, `people`, `over`), `ORDER`, `enterRoom`, `goTo` (skips 6 and 7 unless `darkOn()`), `start(id)`, `visitor`, `fig`, `toneOf`, `walker` (his walk pictures for leaving), `glow`, `darkWith` (darkness with soft holes of light), `doorLight`, sounds made in the browser (`knockS`, `clash`, `cough`, `creak`, `thunder`, `trumpet`, `scratch`); hold acts `RM.HOLDS` ('round', 'walkout': `index.html` keeps the finger in `downs`, the editor holds `keys.push`); `RM.peek()`. `goRoom(id)` in `index.html`; the contents line IV; the pause menu's fourth level; **Level 3's end card now offers "Go on" into the house** (`hill.js` `finish()`; the wicket-gate talk with Good-will is still to come between them, the other Level 4 chat's part).
**Departures (Level 4 house, tell the owner):** every child's act is ours (knocking at the Interpreter's door is the book's: "knocked over and over"); the night at the door, the far fire and the house's look are ours; the candle lit by the child (in the book the Interpreter bids his man light it); the brave man's fight is shown short and without wounds ("cut his way through them all", the book's "giving and receiving many wounds" left out); the people in gold walk on the palace's battlements (the book: "upon the top of the palace"); the dreamer's light on the wall is the painting's (no hand, no writing); the bubbles (room 3) are the painting's. Lines are the agreed ones (see "THE ROOMS' WORDS, AGREED").

**Build 263: three critics, then fixes; the house's voices.** Three agents went over build 261 (the book, the pictures at twice the size, and a child's real touches on an emulated iPhone). Changed: (1) **book:** room 6 gets the book's question and answer before the Interpreter's line, which was read as "pathetic" ("Is there no hope, but you must be kept in this iron cage?" / "No, none at all." / "Why, the Son of God is full of pity."; Bunyan: "the Son of the Blessed is very pitiful"), Christian's "Now let me go on my way." before "Stay ...", "I hardened my heart, so that I cannot repent.", and a second touch on the cage ("Touch the cage again.", the cage rattles) so no talk runs more than about five lines; room 5 is in the book's order ("He drew his sword, put a helmet on his head ..."), and the child calls out the welcome by touching the people in gold; room 4 drops Christian's "What does this mean?"; room 7 asks "Why does this man tremble so?" before the touch; "O eternity" is said "Oh, eternity, eternity!" (the check could not hear "O"). (2) **pictures:** the visitors are tinted pixel by pixel (`toneOf`: colour .6, contrast 1.4 about their own middle, then the room's colour; alpha untouched, so no pale rim), toned per room (night [100,94,112], room 1 [205,185,165] at 1.06 the size, room 4 [185,130,105], room 5 [225,205,180] with less colour); Christian's room picture is the standing frame 30 (frame 78 had a dark smudge; the straight robe front is his own outline in every standing frame); in room 3 the two stand in the arch, smaller and further back; the floor below a picture is its own last rows mirrored, darker; room 2's dust stays low and thins, the water is fine and pale; room 4's far side is fully dark until they go round, and they appear there; the brave man is darker, the sparks are a few warm streaks; room 7 is framed on the bed, the dream darkens the room but for the man and the light, the "man on the cloud" is a tall soft light, the thunder lights only the upper wall, two more painted marks painted out; the walking Christian at the end darker. (3) **play:** a touch that misses shows the ring at once (`touch()`); every ring comes back if it is gone (`>=` and `!G.ring`); a hold act's ring comes back when the finger lifts; leaving is a shorter, quicker walk; room 3's watching is shorter (about 12 s). **Voices:** five new designed voices (the Interpreter, the man in the cage, the trembling man, the brave man, the voices from the palace; descriptions in HANDOFF.md; IDs in `tools/make-voice.py`, which now reads `rooms.js` too), and all 50 of the house's lines recorded (about 55 cents with retakes). Times measured by the playtest before these fixes: about 4 min 45 s through the house without the darker rooms, 6 min 30 s with them.

**Build 264: the Interpreter's own figure; room 2 without painted visitors (about 26 cents, the owner's budget for tonight).** One `gemini-3-pro-image` sheet (16:9, right first time): the Interpreter, an old grave kind man with a short grey beard, a dark skull-cap and a long red-brown gown with a cord, in two poses, standing with folded hands (`assets/room-interpreter.webp`) and an open hand raised as he explains (`room-interpreter-say.webp`); cut from the white paper, mirrored to face left like the other room figures, mapped onto the old red-brown range so he sits with Christian; he lifts his hand while he speaks (`sayK`, by his voice or his name on the words) and folds them again after. Good-will's picture no longer stands in (`room3-interpreter.webp` removed). Room 2's watercolour picture was given back to the image model and asked to remove only its painted Christian and Interpreter (`assets/room2.webp` remade; the damsel, the sweeper and the room unchanged), so the same two figures stand in every room (room 2: the Interpreter by the window at 560, Christian at 1290, `tone` above white to lift them in Vermeer's light). The sprite contrast is 1.2 (was 1.4).

**Build 265: a second critique, its fixes (nothing spent).** Each visitor has his own contrast and depth in `toneOf` (Christian `ct` 1.5, `mul` .9, a little warmer: `VIS.C.warm`; room 2 lifts him with `toneC`); the Interpreter's speaking pose is laid OVER his folded one (no see-through double) and swaps in about a sixth of a second; one small soft shadow per figure (none in room 2's bright room); room 3's pair stand apart (Christian 2235, the Interpreter in the arch at 2420); room 7's man on the cloud is a tall soft light on the empty wall (1650, 330), with no grey ball while the room is dark; leaving, he walks on (a little quicker, as the book has him run) out of the picture, as dark as his standing figure; room 5's camera comes back for the welcome and its ring is lower; room 3's purse is bigger, its glints sharper; room 4's far side keeps both men in the frame; room 2's haze no longer washes the picture flat.

**Build 266: the visitors in scale with each room's people (owner: "In the child one the kids are too big relative to interpreter and pilgrim - can you run through all rooms with this in mind").** The image model painted room 3's seated boys as big as men, so the two visitors are drawn 1.22 times their size there, and placed apart: Christian by the window on the left, facing in, the Interpreter in the arch on the right, the boys and the gold between them (`cam0` 15). Room 7's man in bed was also large: the visitors are 1.22 there too, moved right (2470, 2720) and the camera with them (`cam0` 185), so the Interpreter's head no longer sits in the painted light like a halo. The others checked against their painted people and left as they are: room 1 (the servant), 2 (the damsel and the sweeper), 4 (the man with the bucket), 5 (the crowd, `k` .58), 6 (the man in the cage), the door (`k` .58 against the door). **Rule: in each room, measure a painted standing adult (or a seated one, about .7 of standing) and size the visitors to match.**

**Build 268: room 3 repainted, Christian in colour, a passage between rooms (owner: "Looks too crowded with them on each side - can we re draw to make room for them? . Also can we colorize or even shade Christian just a bit so he fits in environments better - I'd pay up to $.50 for this but use it sparingly please . Ok to do 1 also"; two pictures, about 26 cents, both right first time).** (1) **Room 3 painted again** from its own picture: the boys real children (seated, their heads about a third of the picture above the floor), Passion at a fifth of the way across, Patience at half, empty floor, the arch at three quarters; Passion in rags on a white strip at the side in the same call. `assets/room3.webp` (2561 wide, `PW`), `room3-rags.webp` (a soft-edged patch at P 700,600, 570 x 720: the fine boy covered by wall and floor from beside him, Poisson-blended and toned to the surroundings, the ragged boy, mirrored to face Patience, laid in). The two visitors at normal size on the right, Christian on the floor, the Interpreter in the arch; the bag and the gold moved to the new boy's feet. (2) **Christian painted once in colour** (`assets/room-christian.webp`, from his standing frame 30, the same figure in muted brown-ochre and olive with gentle shading, light from the left; face softened to a plain profile; mirrored to face left like the room figures; `room3-christian.webp` removed): used in every room, with his own gentle contrast (`ct` 1.15, `mul` .88, colour .85). In the dark passage and when leaving he still walks in his grey walk pictures, toned warm-dark. (3) **Follow the Interpreter** (`ROOMS.pass`, act `'follow'`, a hold): after every room but the door, a dark passage: "Hold to follow the Interpreter."; the Interpreter goes a step ahead carrying a candle in his folded hands, its light on the wall and flat on the floor, Christian walking behind in his own walk; footsteps; then the next room fades in. It breaks the talk at each change of room, so no stretch now runs more than five lines without the child doing something.

**Build 270: mood sound in every room, and light on the visitors where a strong light is near (owner: "No hymn please, but you have mood music?" ... "Yes please"; nothing spent).** (1) **Mood sound** (`MOODS`, `ambStart`/`ambStop`/`ambTick`, made in the browser, into `CLEAR`): each room a very quiet bed that fades in with the room and out with it, and sinks to half while anyone speaks (`SPK`): the door and leaving, night wind with a far owl now and then; room 1, a low warm held chord; room 2, airy high hums and a breath of air; room 3, two soft notes swaying; room 4, a deep low note, the fire's roar and its crackle; room 5, a distant bright chord over a low one, which swells at "Come in, come in" (`AMB.swell`); room 6, hollow low notes and a drip now and then; room 7, a slow deep throb; the passage, the candle's flutter and a low note. No hymn, no tune. `RM.stop()` (called wherever `index.html` leaves the house) and the end card stop it. (2) **Light on the visitors** (`R.light(x)`, `litOf`: the figure's side toward the light warmed by a gradient laid only on the figure, baked once per side and colour, drawn over it at the light's strength): room 4, the fire on the side facing it, flickering with it (and the small fire behind the wall once they have gone round); the door, a warm edge from the open doorway (and when leaving); room 1, a little candle warmth once the candle is lit. Room 4 and the night door are toned a shade darker so the light reads. The evenly lit rooms are left alone. **I cannot hear the sound: the owner's ears decide its level and character.**

**Build 273: the room seen whole, framed in the dark (owner: "rooms feel too claustrophobic and the characters seem a little cartoonish ... maybe it's because we are zoomed in too much ... i'd like to be zoomed out a little - if you want to you can frame the rooms in black"; nothing spent).** Each room's WHOLE painting is shown at once, 0.8 the size (`VZ`; a narrower painting such as room 3's is not enlarged), hung on the dark like a picture on a wall, its edges sinking softly into the dark, the floor line still above the words; nothing pans any more (`cam.x` held at 0, `setView`, `VIEW`), so room 4's far side and room 5's door are in the picture from the start (room 4's far side still waits in the dark until they go round). Touches are turned back through the view (`unview` in `touch()`); `darkWith` follows the view. The passage between rooms is unchanged (full screen). Before and after stills of every room were made and shown.

**Build 274: the house repainted in oil, after its masters, and the visitors in shadow (owner: "let's do the $1.40 ... more rembrandty - what if we put christian and interpreter in the shadows so they don't have as much color, but then dial up the realism of the other characters ... super nice paintings - like the masters ones"; eleven `gemini-3-pro-image` pictures, about $1.43).** (1) **Every room repainted as a finished oil** by its master (each given its own picture back and the master's painting; rooms 2 and 3 also the old oil plates `concept/level4-room2-plate.jpg`, `level4-room3-plate.jpg`), everything kept in place, the people made real (slender, natural proportions, real faces painted softly): `assets/room-door.webp`, `room1` to `room7.webp` (quietened as before, `proc.py` in the session scratchpad). Room 3's first try made the boys grown-ups and Patience a woman: retaken once (two little boys, about 8 and 9). Room 1's painted picture lost its gold crown ("A crown of gold hangs over his head"): the old crown cut out by its gold and laid back in, with a faint glow. **Still a departure:** the grave person in the picture now looks out at us rather than up to heaven. (2) **Christian and the Interpreter painted once more, in Rembrandt's manner**, slender, Christian's face deep in his hood (`room-christian.webp`; `room-interpreter.webp` and `-say.webp`, the far shoe brought down with the flat-feet tool), and **set in the shadows** (`VIS`: Christian `mul` .62, `sat` .42; the Interpreter `mul` .66, `sat` .45): dark, nearly colourless, only the rooms' light (fire, candle, door) warming the side that faces it. (3) **The brave man** painted again, plain and armed, the same way (`room5-brave`, `-armed`, `BRAVE` ax 94 / 123). (4) Room 3's rags patch made again from the new picture (`room3-rags.webp`, the ragged boy cut from the picture's white strip, un-whitened, toned into the room); Christian at 2170, the Interpreter in the arch at 2450. Room 7's painted light moved right: the code's light follows (2200, 395) and the visitors stand at 2640 / 2900. (5) Fixed from build 273's zoomed-out view: room 2's dust haze, room 4's dark behind the wall and room 7's thunder light were sized to the screen and showed as boxes; now sized to the picture. Scripts (`cut2.py`, `proc2.py`) in the session scratchpad, not the repo.

**Build 278: FREE VIDEO FROM HUGGING FACE, AND THE INTERPRETER'S HAND (owner: "is there a way to animate with some free apps or other ai models to save money - google seems pricey"; "Hugging face if we could hook it up - we could have it run each day on a timer"; "yes use clips branch, set it up").** (1) **Free clips:** the environment now allows `huggingface.co`, and the owner's Hugging Face read token is saved as a network secret for the Space `r3gm/wan2-2-fp8da-aoti-preview` (Wan 2.2 image-to-video on free ZeroGPU; host `r3gm-wan2-2-fp8da-aoti-preview.hf.space`; the proxy adds the token, never in code or the repo). It takes a FIRST and a LAST picture (`gradio_client`, `api_name='/generate_video'`, frame_multiplier an int 16), gives 832 x 480 at 16 frames a second; a 3.5 s clip used about 24 s of GPU, but the allowance is charged by the GPU time the Space ASKS for, from the clip's length: a 5 s clip reserves about 90 s, and the free account has about 4.5 minutes a day (the first day: the test and three 5 s clips, then "exceeded your free ZeroGPU quota"; it comes back 24 hours after first use). So about 3 clips of 5 s or 5 of 3 s a day; Hugging Face PRO (about $9 a month) gives 40 minutes a day. Room clips (a crop of a room painting animated) may CUT to another view partway: say "one single continuous shot" and keep them short. The daily scheduled task "Daily free clips" fires a fresh session at 19:41 UTC. If that Space disappears, `Lightricks/ltx-video-distilled` and other Wan 2.2 Spaces run on ZeroGPU too (each needs its own host saved). **The clip queue, its runner and the clips themselves live on the `clips` branch** (never pushed to `main`, which republishes the game); a daily scheduled task runs the next jobs. (2) **The Interpreter's gesture is a real video now** (the first free clip, right first time): from his folded hands (first picture) to his open hand lifted (last), both feet planted, camera still. Cut from white in colour (paper and the pale rim taken out), mirrored to face left, 57 frames on one sheet: `assets/room-interpreter-say.webp` (8 across, 142 x 336 a frame; `VIS.I.say` {cols, fr, W, H, ax 82, g 334, `s` = 635/331, its size factor in `fig()`}); `sayFrames()` cuts it into one picture a frame once; `sayK` now moves at the video's own pace (1/214 a tick: about 3.5 s up while he speaks, the same down after), and the frame shown is `sayK` of the way through, so his hand lifts and folds back smoothly. The old two-pose swap remains only as code for a sheet that has not loaded.

**A SECOND FREE SOURCE: MODAL'S MONTHLY CREDITS (after build 362; owner: "can you make sure i don't get charged!?").** Modal gives $30 of credits a month; its client speaks gRPC, which this session's proxy does not carry, so it runs from GitHub Actions: `.github/workflows/clips-modal.yml` (on `main`; the owner's Modal token in the repo secrets `MODAL_TOKEN_ID` (`ak-...`) and `MODAL_TOKEN_SECRET` (`as-...`); dispatch with `task` setup|run and `max`; also daily at 07:17 UTC, at most 4 clips) checks out the `clips` branch and runs `clips/modal_run.py` (the same queue, pictures and checks as `clips/run.py`; jobs marked `via: 'modal'`) against `clips/modal_video.py` (app `pp-video`, the same Wan 2.2 Lightning model as the Space, in diffusers, on one A100-80GB; weights in the Modal volume `pp-weights`, fetched once by `task: setup`). Money guards: one GPU at most, 10 minutes a clip, shut down a minute after; `clips/modal-spend.json` keeps the month's estimate and nothing starts past $18; the owner is to set Modal's Workspace budget to $25. The first clip (`christian-burden-falls`, 81 frames) took 214 s with the cold start: about 17 cents of credit. A queue with nothing to do costs nothing.

**Build 284: the two free walks in the house (owner: "yes please do").** From the first day's free clips (`clips` branch): the Interpreter walking and Christian walking, both in the painted manner, each cut to one clean loop (`walkcut.py` in the session scratchpad: the loop found by the closest silhouette after frame 20, the planted foot's slide measured by matching the bottom rows frame to frame: 6 video px a frame for both) on a sheet: `assets/room-interpreter-walk.webp` (27 frames) and `room-christian-walk.webp` (15 frames), mirrored to face left like the other room figures. `VIS.C.walk`, `VIS.I.walk` ({cols, fr, W, H, ax at the torso, g, `s` to match the standing figure's height, `vpx`}); `walkFig()` draws frame f; `walkSpeed(key, k)` is the floor speed at which the planted foot stays put at the video's own pace (`WALK_FPT` 16/60: the video's 16 frames a second, never sped up). **The passage:** both walk at their own pace (the Interpreter's frames turn a little slower to match Christian's floor speed), the candle's light at the Interpreter's hands, a step sound at each of Christian's steps; the passage is shorter (900, was 1500) so it still takes about 5 s. **Leaving:** Christian walks out past the lit door in his painted walk (the grey walk pictures, `walker()`, stand in only until the sheet loads) and fades from 2800. The Interpreter's gesture (build 278) stays.

**Build 289: the Interpreter's face whole again; the children child-sized (owner: "interpretors mouth looks funny (something about clipping didn't turn out)"; "kids in level (first especially but also patience and passion) are much too big relative to adults").** (1) Cutting the video frames from their white paper also cleared every near-white pixel INSIDE the figure, so his white beard and the pale skin of his lips were cut away and his profile ran flat from nose to beard. Now paper is cleared only in the lowest tenth of the figure (between the feet); the gesture and both walks were cut again (`walkcut.py`, its `all` mode for a gesture; the gesture is now 56 frames, `ax` 84). **Rule: when cutting a figure from white, never clear white inside it except between the feet.** (2) Room 1's servant boy stood as tall as the men: the visitors there are 1.48 (was 1.06); room 3's seated boys were nearly half a man's height: the visitors there are 1.3, Christian at 2060, the Interpreter in the arch at 2460 (his size had been fixed at 1 in code). A free talking clip of the Interpreter (lips and beard moving, drawn large for a sharper face) is queued on the `clips` branch (`interp-talk`; the runner now takes one frame of a sheet, `cell`).

**Build 302: no code breathing in the house (owner: "i don't like the moving pic around to simulate breathing (or livliness) - looks bad").** `fig()` in `rooms.js` no longer sways the figures up and down (`sy` 1) nor leans them with their voices (`talkMove` no longer used there); figures in the house move only by real video frames (the Interpreter's gesture, the walks). The brave man's stride bob and room 7's trembling (story acts) are kept. Nothing spent.

**Build 307: Christian and the Interpreter as sharp as the rooms' own people (owner: "the interpreter and christian in the first room are too low resolution (need to match high resolution of child)").** In room 1 (visitors 1.48 times) Christian's picture was stretched 1.5 times and the Interpreter's gesture frames (from an 832 x 480 clip) nearly 3 times. Upscaled free with Real-ESRGAN (`RealESRGAN_x4plus.pth` from the model's GitHub release, run on the CPU here with `spandrel`; huggingface.co's file store is not reachable): the transparent area filled with edge colours first, RGB through the model then down to size, alpha by cubic resize with a slightly firmer edge. `assets/room-christian-hd.webp` (386 x 948) and `room-interpreter-hd.webp` (285 x 952), 1.5 times (`VIS` `ax`/`g` scaled, `s` 1/1.5); the gesture 2.8 times, 398 x 941 a frame, in four sheets of 14 (`room-interpreter-say-hd-0..3.webp`, about 1.3 MB; `say.parts`/`per`, `sheetFrames` now only describes frames and `toneOf` cuts them; the toned cache is cleared at each room so big frames do not pile up). The old files removed. The walks are unchanged. Script `up.py` was in the session scratchpad. Nothing spent.

**Build 312: the passage by one touch, shorter, with a verse (owner: "can you make walks in dark room happen without holding down walk (maybe just click and christian goes)"; "and maybe slightly shorter and a verse for each transition?").** `ROOMS.pass`: "Touch to follow the Interpreter."; one touch (or a hold, or a key) starts the walk (`start()`) and they walk on to the end by themselves (`this.go`). The passage is 700 units (was 900, about 4 s). As they walk a King James verse shows quietly on the word strip (italic, not spoken), keyed by the room they go to (`VERSES`): r2 Psalm 119:105 (the lamp; ours, after the picture's "best of books"), r3 Romans 5:20 (Bunyan's margin for the parlour), r4 Matthew 6:20 (ours, after Passion and Patience; Bunyan's margin is Luke 16:25), r5 2 Corinthians 12:9 (Bunyan's margin for the fire), r6 Acts 14:22 (Bunyan's margin for the palace), r7 Mark 13:37 ("Watch", ours, after the cage), leave Isaiah 26:1 (Bunyan's margin for the wall called Salvation). Each checked word for word against es-kjv and bible-kjv (the brackets of italic words dropped). They arrive once the passage is walked and the verse has had time to be read. Nothing spent.

**Build 315: the storyteller reads the passage verses (owner: "yes have the storyteller read the verses").** `VERSES` entries are now `[[N, text], ref]` so `tools/make-voice.py` finds them; `start()` calls `say(N, text)` and waits for its recording's length before they arrive. Seven lines recorded, each first take (about 7 cents). The reference is shown, not read. The Interpreter's walk in the passage was checked: it is the 27-frame video walk and does animate (a filmstrip was shown), but his long gown hides most of the stepping and his frames turn a little slower to keep his feet from sliding at Christian's pace.

**Build 320: the Interpreter's walk at full pace, a clean loop (owner: "full speed is fine (just make sure it looks good)").** The old 27-frame loop began with his head bowed and ended with it raised, so it snapped down at every turn of the loop. In the passage both now play at their videos' own pace, each at the floor speed that keeps his planted foot still (`ix` from his own `walkSpeed`), so the Interpreter closes a little on Christian over the passage. Script `iwcut.py` in the session scratchpad. Nothing spent.

(2) The Interpreter's walk had a white wedge of paper under the beard, shut in by the solid-body fill: paper reached from outside through near-white pixels (`d` < 20, flooded from the frame's edges) is now never counted as body; the beard (grey, not paper-white) stays whole: `assets/room-interpreter-walk3.webp` (the same 28 frames). **Rule: a figure's solid-body fill must not swallow paper that reaches it from outside; flood the near-white paper from the frame's edges first.** Scripts `cwcut.py`, `iwcut.py` in the session scratchpad. Nothing spent.

Drawn live in `r4.under()`: the fire as soft stacked teardrops that sway and breathe (`flame()`), a smaller fire behind the wall; the man casting water is Help's standing picture (`assets/room4-caster.webp`, frame 0 of `help.pack`, cut to its outline) as a dark figure, his bucket swinging up every few seconds (`cast`) and a stream of pale drops arcing into the fire (`water`); the man pouring oil behind the wall is Good-will's reaching picture (`goodwill-reach.webp`, mirrored) with a jug at his hands, the oil drops falling from it (`R4D`). Both are stand-ins, toned dark (`mul` .42). The steam is fainter there. Not yet: the room's one quoted detail (La Tour's hand round the light), the Interpreter's and Christian's dark painted figures beside the code room (they are kept as they are). Nothing spent.

**Build 379: THE PARLOUR DRAWN IN CODE, A SECOND TRIAL (owner: "can you draw the parlour").** Also only with `?drawn` (`?room=2&drawn`); the painting is unchanged without it. `bakeR2()` into `pics['room2-drawn']`: Vermeer's one light, a tall leaded window on the left (a dark reveal, bright glass with a faint diamond lattice, one mullion and transom, a sill), the pale warm plaster brightest beside it and falling away to the right and toward the top, the window's light lying across the wall as one soft slanting pool (painted small and stretched up, laid with 'soft-light'), a few faint broad stains, a low band of pale tiles at the wall's foot with a faint blue figure on each (the Milkmaid's Delft tiles), the floorboards a nearer, darker layer with the window's light lying flat on them. Drawn live in `r2.under()` instead of the painting's clips: the girl is his wife's picture from Level 1 (`wife.webp`) holding a red-brown jug at her hand, which tips as she pours (`df`, as before), the water falling from its spout (`spout`); the sweeper is Help's standing picture (`room4-caster.webp`, mirrored) with a broom drawn in code that swings at its foot while he sweeps (`sf`, the old clip's phase) and leans when he stops (`R2D`). Both stand-ins, toned warm-dark (`mul` .62 / .56) so they read solid against the bright wall (lighter, they looked see-through). The dust, the haze, the talk and every place are as before. **Found while drawing:** a figure's picture pixels become plate units by `s / K` (`fig()` draws at `S / K * k * s`), so a point on a figure (a hand) is `x + (px - ax) * s / K`. Nothing spent.

**Build 381: THE DRAWN PARLOUR WORKED TOWARD THE PAINTING'S POP (owner, of build 379: "would be nice to have that amazing pop of the painting - can we work harder to match it with computer graphs").** Still only with `?drawn`. `bakeR2()` redone: (1) **the plaster** after the Milkmaid's: warm cream in the light, cool grey-blue patches in the half-shade (`cool`), a deep warm brown in the far corner and toward the top, texture at three sizes laid with 'soft-light' (broad washes, mottling, fine grain), nine faint hairline cracks and a few small chips; (2) **the light** one great soft slanting pool from the upper left down toward the floor (painted small and stretched up, laid twice with 'soft-light' and once faintly with 'screen'); (3) **the side wall coming toward us on the left** in shade, with the window in it seen a little from the side (a depth the owner liked in the painted parlour): deep reveal, bright green-grey glass, leaded diamonds, mullion, transom and sill, a soft dark seam at the corner; (4) **the basket and the brass pail** on their nails, each casting a soft shadow down and to the right: a round-bellied wicker basket (rows of light slanting strands over dark gaps, ribs, a light rim, a hoop handle) and a pail with one bright gleam on the window's side; (5) **the Delft tiles** with small blue figures (a ring, an arch, a little house), shadowed toward the corner; (6) **the foot warmer** by the right wall, a wooden box with holes, lit on the window's side; (7) **the floorboards** each its own tone, with grain and seams, the window's light lying flat on them. **The two stand-ins in colour** (`colourFig(name, out, parts, lightRight)`: the grey watercolour's contrast raised, a colour map of rectangles softened by drawing it small and stretching it back (hard edges showed as straight lines across the shoulders), laid once with 'color' and once with 'multiply' at .7, then lit from the window's side with 'soft-light'): the girl (`wife-col`) in the Milkmaid's linen head-cloth, yellow bodice and sleeve, ultramarine apron, red skirt; the sweeper (`sweep-col`) in an olive jerkin and brown breeches. The visitors are unchanged. **Bug found and fixed while doing it:** clipping to a shape whose drawing function also strokes an open handle clips to the handle's path (the weave vanished): clip to a path built for the clip alone. Nothing spent.

**Build 389: ONE HOUSE, THE PASSAGES ARE ITS REAL WAYS (owner: "i'd like the rooms to be part of a house and clearly connected - like which ones are upstairs (if so we need stairs between them) and how is each a clear room of a house"; my plan, shown as a rough cutaway, agreed: "ok sounds good").** The rooms placed by the book's own words: ground floor, front to back, 1 "a private room", 2 "a very large parlour", 3 "a little room", 4 the fire "against a wall" (the kitchen hearth, its chimney the wall they go round); 5 "a pleasant place" where the palace stands, the GARDEN behind the house (out of the back door); 6 "a very dark room", the CELLAR; 7 "a chamber" with one rising out of bed, the BEDCHAMBER upstairs. The way: front door, 1, 2, 3, 4, out the back door to 5, back in and down to 6, up the great stair to 7, down and out the front door (darker rooms off: 5, then round the house to the front gate). **The passage between rooms (`ROOMS.pass`) is now that way** (`passKind(from, to)`: 'hall' a ground-floor corridor, a beam overhead, the doorway behind them and the next room's doorway ahead with its door ajar and a little of its light; 'out' the corridor ending in the back door, the night garden beyond it (sky, stars, the garden wall, dark shrubs); 'down' a landing and the cellar stair going down to a low arch; 'up' the great stair going up to the bedchamber's door; 'round' out of doors along the house's back wall with its lit windows to the front gate; all in one dark tone each, only what the candle reaches seen, `darkWith`). On the stairs both men walk on the steps (`floorAt(x)`: the flight from `S0` 760 to `S1` 1760, `RISE` 250; `walkFig` now takes a standing line `y`), the candle's flat pool on the floor under the Interpreter. **A small cutaway of the house** in the corner (`housePlan()`, `PLAN`: each room's place on a 100 x 62 plan; the roof, two floors, the cellar, the garden): the room they leave lit, the room they go to coming up, rooms already seen faintly lit (`SEEN_R`), the darker rooms shown only when switched on, the candle's dot moving between them. `RM.peek()` gives `pass` (kind > to) and `pp` (how far along). **Still to do (agreed):** the cutaway seen whole at the door; the rooms drawn in code one by one to match (doorways where the passages lead, the same beams and boards on a floor, windows that look out on the right thing). Nothing spent.

**Build 393: THE HOUSE SEEN WHOLE (owner: "Ok sounds good" to the agreed next step).** A cutaway of the house by night (`houseCut()`, drawn in code over the scene, same plan as the corner map `PLAN`): roof with tile courses and a moonlit edge, the chimney over the kitchen hearth with soft smoke, the upper floor (the bedchamber between two unused rooms), the ground floor (the front hall with its open lit door, rooms 1 to 4, a few plain things in each: the picture, the parlour's window, two little chairs, the hearth), the great stair from the parlour up, the cellar stair down to the cage, the garden behind with its wall and the palace small on its rise; the darker rooms (cage, bed) shown only when switched on. **At the door** it fades in while the storyteller says "Then he went on till he came to the house of the Interpreter ..." with every room dark but the hearth (the fire burns all along), and fades out before "Knock on the door." **Leaving**, it shows again while the Interpreter asks "Have you thought on all these things?", the rooms he has seen lighting one after another (`SEEN_R`, cleared at the door), then fades before the walk out. A room may now set `cut` (0..1) and `cutLit(id)`; `draw()` lays the cutaway over everything in screen space. `RM.seen` exposes `SEEN_R` for tests. Nothing spent.

**Build 423: no flicker at the door (owner: "the opening flickered between new (floor plan) and old (painting)").** The cutaway of the house (`houseCut`) used to fade in over the door's painting after the room had come up, then fade out again: painting, plan, painting. Now the door and leaving start with the cutaway whole (`cut` 1 in `enter()`), so the room comes up out of the dark onto the house and the cutaway fades once to the door. New `tools/stills4.js` for Level 4 stills. Nothing spent.

**Build 424: ROOM 1 DRAWN IN CODE, AND ONE SHELL FOR EVERY ROOM (owner: "start to redo each room - use sub agents to consider all the factors: design, cohesiveness, faithfulness to story, beauty, playability, engagement").** Three critic subagents first wrote a house design system, a story-and-beauty brief per room and a play review (kept only in that session's scratchpad; their main rulings: the house a flat SECTION, back wall frontal, end walls cut edge-on, ONE shell for every room so beams, skirting, floor and doorways match; night in every room; enter by the left door, leave by the right; visitors stand left facing right, the room's subject centre and right; one light per room in the gate's fall-off; the room's people as dark quiet figures). In `rooms.js`: `HOUSE` (FY 1250, ceiling 150, one beam band 44, skirting 28, lintel 320, end walls 110), `bakeShell(o)` (plaster grain at one strength, the room's lights as soft pools on wall and floor, the beam's warm arris only in the light, doorway holes with a jamb in the end walls; `o.wall` and `o.near` add a room's own things), `lightPool()`. **Room 1** (`bakeR1`, `R1`): dark but for his man's taper; ▸ touch the candle (his man lights it on a tall turned stand, the dark lifts from it); ▸ touch the door in the wall (the book: "bid his man open a door"): two oak leaves swing open, his man steps up beside them, the view eases in on the picture (`vz`/`vx` per phase); C "What does this mean?", I's first line; ▸ touch the crown (it shines, two notes); I's second line. The visitors stand at 520 and 800 at their own size (k 1, was 1.48). Departures: the door is a cupboard door in the wall, opened by the child's touch. **Stand-ins until the free image model's allowance comes back (it ran out today):** the portrait in the frame is cut from the old painting (`room1-portrait.webp`: it looks up with open hands, book and globe, but sits; the book has him STAND, the book in his hand); his man is Help's old picture as a near-black silhouette (`room4-caster`). `?painted` shows the old painted room. A fourth critic reviewed the stills and its eight fixes are in. No words changed. Nothing spent.

**Build 426: ROOM 4 ON THE HOUSE'S SHELL.** The `?drawn` trial of build 378 is now the room (`?painted` shows the old painting): `bakeR4()` is built by `bakeShell` (the same ceiling, beam, skirting, floor and doorways as room 1), the fire's pool on the wall and floor, a second low light behind the wall, the chimney breast floor to ceiling in the end walls' near-black with its face toward the fire warmed, a low hearth step. The fire now does what the book says ("yet did the fire burn higher and hotter"): at each cast it shrinks where the water strikes (a hiss), then rises higher than before (`grow`, `dip`), its glow with it. The man casting water now faces the fire; the visitors stand at 400 and 680 on the left (then 2700 and 2930 behind the wall); the dark behind the wall is .82, so the one pouring oil is just to be made out. Stand-ins still: the caster (Help's old picture) and the one pouring oil (Good-will's reaching picture), both near-black. Nothing spent.

**Build 430: THE CRITICS' SUGGESTIONS, FIRST PASS (owner: "Ok let's take critics suggestions and go").** (1) **The drawn rooms fill the screen** (`shell` 1 on a room; `setView`: the plate fills the picture's width, its floor line at .86 of the picture's height, `zin`/`vx` to ease in on a thing; room 1 eases in 1.22 on the picture): the play critic's answer to "claustrophobic" is more room drawn, not a smaller picture on black. The painted rooms keep their frame. (2) **Room 6, the cellar** (`bakeR6`, `R6`; the book: "a very dark room"): `bakeShell` takes `ceil`, `skirt`, `wallC`, `beamC`, `floorC` now; a low flat ceiling (290), dark rough stone in broad soft patches (stone courses were tried and read as a grid of tiles: taken out), the spiral stair they came down at the far left as wedge treads round a newel (Rembrandt's Philosopher in Meditation, the room's quoted detail), ONE thin grey shaft from a grating high on the left with dust turning in it, landing short of the cage; the cage drawn in code over the man (it shakes when touched); the man is a dark, softly vignetted crop of the old painting (`room6-man.webp`, the bars painted out by OpenCV and the crop darkened: a stand-in); the visitors at 860 and 1160 facing the cage; a drip now and then. (3) **Room 7, the bedchamber** (`bakeR7`, `R7`): wine-brown plaster, the door to the stair on the right, the bed and the man rising from it on the left (a dark vignetted crop of the old painting, `room7-bed.webp`, trembling: a stand-in), the bare wall in the middle the dream's stage, told only in light: on the first line a soft oval of black sky with clouds sliding against each other and lightning lighting them (an oval flash, no box), thunder and the trumpet now on the game's clock (they used `setTimeout` and ran on through a pause), the one gold light high on the wall at the man upon a cloud, pale lights rising into it on the last line, and one left low by the bed, fading. Visitors at 2620 and 2880 facing left. `?painted` shows the old painted rooms 1, 4, 6 and 7. Nothing spent.

**Build 431: THE CRITICS' SUGGESTIONS, SECOND PASS.** (1) **The passage's doorways** were 560 to 600 high beside men about 656 high (lower than their heads): now the house's lintel height, 763 (the doorway behind them, the next room's doorway with its door ajar, the bedchamber's door at the stair's head, the back door), the cellar's arch 715. (2) **Fewer words, no new recordings:** room 4 opens on the Interpreter's line (the storyteller's "A fire burned against a wall ... higher and hotter" is cut: the picture shows it, and the fire now visibly rises after each cast); the passage verses are all still shown, but the storyteller reads only three (`READ`: going into room 2, room 5, and leaving), so the other passages take about four seconds instead of up to ten. Nothing spent.

**Build 435: THE WHOLE HOUSE DRAWN, THE PAINTINGS GONE (owner: "I want painting looks gone (I mean you can save them if I want to go back) and replace with drawn look across the board").** Every stage of Level 4 is now drawn in code and fills the screen (`shell` 1); `?painted` brings back every old painted room (the pictures stay in `assets/`). New: (1) **the door and leaving** (`bakeDoorScene(leaving)`, `HSE`; after Rembrandt's Rest on the Flight): night, two layered masses of dark trees over still water on the left with one tiny far fire mirrored in it, the stone house in the right half (one plain mass, steep roof, chimney, one lit upper window that dims as the door opens), the arched door at the old `DOOR`; leaving, the low coursed wall called Salvation begins on the right and a soft first grey lies along the horizon there, toward Level 5. (2) **Room 2, the parlour at night** (`bakeR2s`, `R2S`; `?drawn` still shows build 381's daylight trial): Vermeer's window frontal in the back wall as moonlight (the glass the brightest thing, the plaster cool beside it, warm umber shade), the basket and pail on one nail as quiet filled shapes, a low pale band of Delft tiles; the girl and the sweeper (`room2-girl`, `room2-sweeper`) a shade quieter in the moon; the dust haze a third as strong; visitors at 820 and 1100. (3) **Room 3, a little room** (`bakeR3`, `R3`): calm umber plaster, a small deep-set window high on the left showing only the night, a horn lantern on a bracket the one light; **the boys cut from the old painting with OpenCV GrabCut** (`room3-passion.webp`, `room3-patience.webp`, `room3-passion-rags.webp`; a fragment of wall by the ragged boy cleared by a bounded flood fill), standing in their chairs on the drawn floor, moved right by `R3.X` 640 with the bag, the gold and the bubbles; the visitors on the left at 480 and 760. (4) **Room 5, the garden and the palace** (`bakeR5`, `R5`, `sil()`): the garden at night in deep blue, the house's back wall and lit back door on the left, the palace in moonlit stone in the right half with a tower and battlements, its door the brightest low thing; live: the people in gold pacing on its top (soft figures of light), the crowd as a huddle of dark silhouettes, the writer at his table with a small light, the armed men either side of the door with their lances swaying; the brave man and his arming clip as before; visitors at 380 and 560. A fault found and fixed: `PAINTED` was declared after its first use (the page would not start). Checked: the house plays from the door to the end card, drawn and with `?painted`. Nothing spent.

**Build 437: THE HOUSE OUTSIDE AT THE SIZE OF ITS ROOMS (owner: "House on outside should have same size as house on inside").** Inside, a floor is 1100 high for a man 800 tall; outside the figures are .58 of that, so a floor is about 640. The door's and leaving's house (`bakeDoorScene`) is no longer a small cottage under a roof: its front starts at `HSE.X0` 1680 and runs off the right and the top of the picture; a string course at FY - 640 marks the upper floor; the bedchamber's window lit upstairs (`HSE.WIN` 2620, 300; it dims as the door opens), a dark one beside it, the parlour's tall window below on the right, the cellar's barred grating at the wall's foot (the grating whose shaft lights room 6); the arched door is unchanged, about a man and a tenth high, like the lintels inside.
