It still shows when leaving, with the rooms he has seen lighting one by one. Nothing spent.

**Build 441: HIGHER ROOMS, THE FIGURES SMALLER ON SCREEN (owner: "the characters are too big on screen - either enlarge room height (think arched or vaulted ceilings in all) or zoom out and more black top and bottom - what do you think??"; my answer, mostly higher rooms and the view drawn back about a fifth so they still fill the width; "Let's do it").** Every drawn room (and the door, the garden, leaving) is baked on a larger canvas (`EX`: plate x -360 to 3528, y -700 to 1344; `mkBake()`, `cv.ex`; the old plate's coordinates unchanged, so every place, touch and line stays), and `setView` fits that whole width to the screen: the figures go from about 45% to about 37% of the picture's height, with a great dark above them. `bakeShell` takes `roof`: 'beams' (the default: a high ceiling at `HOUSE.CEIL` -300, its joists a dark band, a great beam with beam ends every bay; rooms 1, 2, 3, 4), 'vault' (room 6, the cellar: a stone barrel vault springing at 240 and rising to -260, faint voussoir joints) and 'rafters' (room 7, upstairs under the roof: wall-plate at 120, a tie-beam, rafters and king posts rising into the dark); the wall darkens upward into the ceiling so the light stays a low pool round the people. Room 4's chimney breast runs up into the dark; the cellar's newel post too. Outside: more night sky with stars above the house and the palace; the house's front runs on up out of the picture. Fixed with it: the parlour's dust haze, room 4's dark behind the wall and the parlour's Delft band were sized to the old picture and showed as boxes. Room 1 eases in a little more on the picture (`zin` 1.3). Checked: the house plays from the door to the end card; stills before and after of every stage. Nothing spent.

**Build 446: THE HOUSE'S PEOPLE AS THEIR OWN FIGURES, TONED INTO THE GAME'S LOOK (owner, of two new image-model sheets, about 30 cents: "is the look too painted"; shown a comparison of the boys as painted and toned: "Yes" to toned).** From two `gemini-3-pro-image` sheets (16:9, 2K): each figure cut from its white (paper flooded from the edges, shut-in paper over 250 px cleared, the largest piece kept), its face softened to a plain wash inside its outline (the profile kept: the plain-face rule holds again in the house), then toned onto one dark warm ramp ([16,12,11] to [118,96,80], 15% of its colour at half strength) and saved at plate size: `room3-passion2`, `room3-rags2`, `room3-patience2` (the boys, one scale, .6556 of the sheet; Passion and the ragged boy share the chair's place), `room1-servant` (holding up his taper; the taper's glow follows his hand, `this.tip`), `room1-portrait2` (the grave person STANDING as the book has him, eyes lifted, the Bible in his hand, the globe behind, a crown above; kept a painting, colour .8, darkened; `R1.PIC` and `NICHE` narrowed to it, `CROWN` on its crown), `room4-caster2` (his own bucket; the code bucket gone, the water from its lip), `room4-pourer` (hooded, with a jug; the oil falls from its spout), `room6-caged` (on a stool, facing the visitors, the bars drawn over him), `room7-rising` (in his nightshirt pulling on his coat, trembling beside a four-poster drawn in code, `drawBed7`). Drawn with `fig` at `s` .8, a little darkened per room. The old stand-ins and crops (`room1-portrait`, `room4-caster`, `room6-man`, `room7-bed`, `room3-passion`, `-patience`, `-passion-rags`) stay in assets, unused by the drawn rooms. Spent: about 30 cents (the two sheets).

**Nothing is built** (but the house, above) The other chat designs the picture first; then a file `gate.js` (object like `SL`/`HL`), a title button 4, `go4()`, Level 3's end card "Go on".

**Proposed picture (agreed master for the wicket-gate: Rembrandt, one warm light in a great dark):** night or deep dusk; a long dark wall; the little gate opened a hand's breadth at the end of Level 3, now opened wide: warm light pours out low and flat (the Level 1 rule: a flat pool, never a shape down the picture); Good-will a grave old figure in that light. Far to the left, a dark castle on a rise (Beelzebub's, `{62}`), seen only as a silhouette. Inside the gate: the narrow way, ruler-straight, running away under the far light, with crooked wide ways butting onto it (`{67}`), drawn as pale lines in the flat side view (they must not run down the picture toward us: lay them along the ground as bands that meet the way).

**Proposed moments and phases:** `open` (the level opens at the gate as Level 3 ended: Good-will asks who is there) → `ask` (talk `GW_ASK`) → `pull` (act `'pull'`: as Christian steps in, arrows hiss from the castle; Good-will pulls him in: the child holds Good-will's hand, an echo of Help's hand in Level 2; the arrows as sound and a few dark streaks that fall short, never striking) → `inside` (three lines: who sent you; the open door) → `way` (Good-will: "Look before you. Do you see this narrow way?": act `'way'`, the child touches the way, as they touched the light and the hill; the far light brightens along it) → `burden` (he asks to be helped off with his burden; "be content to bear it": a quiet beat, the burden's creak) → `leave` (the Interpreter's house named; God-speed) → hold to walk on along the narrow way; the gate's light behind him → end card "Through the gate", next: the Interpreter's House. About a minute and a half.

**The words (Bunyan's, in his order, shortened and made plain; `GW` = Good-will):**
- `{60}` N: "At last there came a grave person to the gate, named Good-will. He asked who was there, and where he came from, and what he wanted." (Level 3's last line ends at "named Good-will"; this level repeats it in full as its opening.)
- `{61}` C: "Here is a poor burdened sinner. I come from the City of Destruction, and am going to Mount Zion, that I may be delivered from the wrath to come. Are you willing to let me in?" GW: "I am willing with all my heart." N: "And with that he opened the gate."
- `{62}` N: "As Christian was stepping in, Good-will gave him a pull." C: "What does that mean?" GW: "A little way from this gate there stands a strong castle. Its captain is Beelzebub. From there they shoot arrows at those who come up to this gate, to stop them before they can enter in." C: "I rejoice, and tremble."
- `{63}` GW: "Who sent you here?" C: "Evangelist bid me come here and knock, as I did. He said that you, sir, would tell me what I must do." GW: "An open door is set before you, and no man can shut it." (kept: it answers how he came, without retelling Levels 1 to 3)
- `{66}` GW: "Come a little way with me, and I will teach you about the way you must go." GW: "Look before you. Do you see this narrow way? That is the way you must go. It is as straight as a rule can make it." (act `'way'`)
- `{67}` C: "But are there no turnings or windings, by which a stranger may lose his way?" GW: "Yes, there are many ways that butt down upon this one, and they are crooked and wide. But you may tell the right from the wrong: the right only is straight and narrow."
- `{68}` N: "Then Christian asked him if he could not help him off with his burden. For he could not get it off without help." GW: "As to your burden, be content to bear it until you come to the place of deliverance. There it will fall from your back of itself."
- `{69}` N: "Then Christian made ready for his journey." GW: "When you have gone some distance from the gate, you will come to the house of the Interpreter. Knock at his door, and he will show you excellent things." N: "Then Christian took his leave of his friend, and he again bid him God-speed."

**NO RECAP (owner, build 143: "remove recap - keep it going").** Good-will's questions that retell Levels 1 to 3 (`{63}` from "how is it that you came alone" to `{65}`: the neighbours, Obstinate and Pliable, the Slough, Wiseman and the mountain) and "We make no objections against any, whatever they have done before" are left out. **Departures (proposed; tell the owner):** the recap above; "the celestial glory" not needed now; who made the narrow way ("the patriarchs, prophets, Christ, and his apostles") left out; "gird up his loins" made "made ready"; the child holding Good-will's hand for the pull, the arrows falling short, and the far light along the narrow way are ours; the verse over the gate (`{60}`) left out as in Level 3.

**ONE LEVEL WITH THE INTERPRETER'S HOUSE (owner, build 149, agreeing to my advice: "Ok sounds good").** Asked "is this level now as necessary?": without the recap the gate is about 20 lines and a minute and a half, too thin to stand alone. The book runs straight on (`{69}` Good-will names the Interpreter's house; `{70}` "he went on till he came to the house of the Interpreter"), so the wicket-gate OPENS the Interpreter's House level: the gate opening in its one warm light is its first picture, the pull and the narrow way its first walk, the knock at the Interpreter's door its first act (an echo of the gate). Not instead: adding the gate to the end of Level 3, already the longest talk in the game. **Still open for the owner:** the castle and arrows shown, or sound only; Good-will's figure (a picture and a gesture video to pull Christian in: about 71 cents; or an arm reaching out of the light, drawn in code, free); the Interpreter's House itself is not yet planned (book `{70}` to `{92}`).

## The whole game: about 17 levels (a plan, not agreed level by level)

Counted by episode from the book's section numbers (later long conversations shrink to a few lines each). Find each start in the book file by searching for the name.

| # | Level | Book |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | City of Destruction | `{10}` |
| 2 | Slough of Despond | `{25}`/`{29}` |
| 3 | Mr. Worldly Wiseman and the hill | `{37}` |
| 4 | The Wicket-Gate and the Interpreter's House (owner agreed, build 149: the gate opens the level) | `{60}`, `{70}` |
| 5 | The Cross: the burden falls off | `{93}` |
| 6 | Hill Difficulty, the lost roll, the lions | |
| 7 | Palace Beautiful | |
| 8 | Apollyon, in the Valley of Humiliation | |
| 9 | Valley of the Shadow of Death | |
| 10 | Faithful (and Talkative) | |
| 11 | Vanity Fair | |
| 12 | Hopeful, By-ends, the silver mine | |
| 13 | By-path Meadow and Doubting Castle | |
| 14 | The Delectable Mountains | `{295}` |
| 15 | Ignorance, the Flatterer's net | |
| 16 | The Enchanted Ground | |

**LEVEL 5, THE CROSS: THE PLAN (owner: "ok let's do it", then "go with your recommendations, make the sketch"; checked against `{92}` to `{94}`, `{100}`, `{101}`, `{120}`).** After Caspar David Friedrich (*Cross in the Mountains*, *Monk by the Sea*): the opposite of Level 3's terror, power by emptiness. A low horizon, about three quarters sky; he comes up the walled highway (`{92}`, "that wall was called Salvation", a low coursed wall that ends where the land opens) onto "a place somewhat ascending", one long gentle rise; on its crest in the right third ONE small plain rough cross, nothing on it; the sepulchre "a little below, in the bottom" on the far slope, a low rock with a dark mouth. **Dawn, the game's first** (the book names no time; it falls between the night at the Interpreter's and "almost night" at the arbour, `{102}`): cold blue-grey before; the far light he has followed since Level 1 rises exactly behind where the beams cross. The burden falls BY ITSELF as he comes up (the book: "just as Christian came up with the cross, his burden loosed"), the child does not cut it loose; it tumbles down into the tomb ("I saw it no more"); silence first, then colour returns from the light outward and Amazing Grace plays whole and in tune for the first time. He weeps and looks up ("while I stood looking up", `{120}`). **The three Shining Ones are figures of light** (the book gives them no look here; at the River "raiment that shone like gold; also their faces shone as the light"). **He wears a new coat from here on** (the book: rags at `{10}`, "change of raiment" at the Cross, "this broidered coat which you see", the coat noticed by Formalist and Hypocrisy, `{100}`, `{101}`; "the mark which you see in my forehead"): first try painting it into his existing frames, free. The sealed roll goes into the tray (`KS.waxSeal`). Three leaps for joy is the child's act. Sketch drawn in code, free: `concept/level5-cross-sketch.jpg` (A before dawn with the burden; B after, the sun behind the crossing, the three Shining Ones, him kneeling without the burden). **The hill (owner: "cross should be on more of hill no?", then of three thumbnails: "a blend of 1 and 3 (maybe 3 with slightly higher hill and lighten spots)"):** one LONG SLOPE rising left to right across the picture to a rounded crown in the right third, the cross small on it against the dawn, the sun behind it; a quicker fall on the far side to the hollow where the tomb lies; pale far ridges in layers of haze behind (the game's look), the slope itself DARK (owner: "no i liked the darker one": a lighter slope with haze in its hollows was tried and turned down), with a thin lit rim along the crown, then darker still (owner: "even darker"). `concept/level5-cross-sketch.jpg` now holds the settled pair (owner: "this looks good"): A before dawn, a cold blue-grey sky with only a faint warmth low behind the crown, the low coursed wall of Salvation ending at the left where the slope begins, Christian (his real standing picture, tiny) at its end with the burden, the cross far above; B at dawn, the sun behind the cross, a lit rim along the crown, him kneeling beside it, the burden at the tomb's mouth below. **ONE cross, not three** (owner asked; the book, `{93}`: "upon that place stood a cross"; Bunyan's is the cross itself, empty, with no thieves and no figure on it). Turned down: a smooth round dome with the cross on top ("doesn't look right").

**LEVEL 5 IN THE GAME (build 390; owner: "sounds great" to my plan: everything free but the voices, the lines shown first).** `cross.js`, object `CR`, run by the same engine (captions, `talk`, the tray); reached from the contents (V), the pause menu, the editor's arrows, and "Go on" at the end of the Interpreter's House. **How it goes (`{92}` to `{94}`):** a wide first look at the long dark slope, the wall of Salvation ending at its foot, the sepulchre there, the cross small on the crown against a cold blue-grey sky (`ph` 'look', about 5 s, then the camera comes down to him) → hold to walk up (`walk`; the sky warms a little as he climbs: `dawn`), OPEN said at the start and ASCEND as the cross comes into view → he stops just short of the cross (`STOP_X`), facing it (`cross`, automatic): his standing picture gives way to THE CLIP, the ropes loosen and the burden slips off behind him (LOOSED) → at the clip's frame 41 the bundle becomes its own picture (`bur`) and tumbles all the way back down the slope he climbed, bouncing now and then (`burdenTick`, thumps), while the camera draws back to the whole slope (`wideK`) and he stands tall, his head lifted (the clip's frames 52 to 70, back and forth at its own pace, eased: `clipHold`); TUMBLE as it goes; it rolls into the mouth of the sepulchre and is gone ('tumble', 'gone') → a silence (the low pad has faded out) → the sun rises exactly behind where the beams cross and colour comes back over everything (`goldTo`, `sun`), and **Amazing Grace is played whole and in tune** (`hymn`, `hymnTick`: Level 1's `TUNE`, slower, `BEATT` 34, with `BASS` an octave down and a soft chord on each bar), GLAD and LOOKED said over it → **the three Shining Ones** come down as tall soft figures of light right of the cross (`shining`, drawn with 'lighter', no picture) → `talk(SHINE)`: "Peace be unto thee", "Thy sins be forgiven thee", the new clothes (a light wraps him and his tone turns warmer and lighter: `robe`), the mark (a small light on his forehead: `mark`) and the sealed roll (act `'roll'`: touch it in the third one's hands; it drifts into the tray, `G.roll`), "Look on it as you run ...", they go up into the light (`gone`) → `talk(LEAPS)`: act `'leap'`: the child touches him three times and he leaps (a hop of his standing picture in code, `Ch.hop`, a rising note each, G B D) → end card "The Cross", next: the Hill Difficulty (not yet built). **The land** (`gh(x)`, world units): flat to `SLOPE0` 1180, one long gentle rise of 240 to the crown at `CREST0` 2860, the cross at `CROSS_X` 3040 (196 tall), a quicker fall past `CREST1`; the wall ends at `WALL_END` 860, the sepulchre at `TOMB_X` 1010. Sky and far ridges in screen space (`sky()`, `far()`), the land, wall, tomb and cross in world units (`land()`, `wall()`, `tomb()`, `cross()`). **The clip** (free, Modal, `clips` branch `christian-burden-falls`, 81 frames at 16 a second): frames 0 to 40 with the burden, 41 on his body alone (the bundle and ropes cut away by an opening of the mask, the rope stubs by his heels cleared), cut by `tools/gesture-frames.py --height 372 --to 288 --match christian-stand-nb` into `assets/christian-cross.pack` (`CLIP_META` ax 182.5, ground 299: matched to his standing picture by the feet and the chest's front edge, the same height to a pixel), and the bundle as it lands, `assets/cross-burden.webp` (99 x 118, `BALL`). Its last frames (72 to 80) start a walk, not used yet. **Departures (tell the owner):** the dawn, the wide looks and the music are ours; the Shining Ones are figures of light (the book gives them no look here); "the second stripped him of his rags" told as "took off his rags, and clothed him with new clothes" (his clothes are not yet changed in the pictures: only his tone; a free try at painting a new coat into his frames is next); the third one's charge to look on the roll and give it in at the Celestial Gate is spoken by him (the book reports it); the child takes the roll and makes the three leaps; his song after the leaps is left out. **Voices (build 394, about 20 cents):** all 13 lines recorded; the Shining Ones share one new designed voice (`SO`, `S1`, `S3` in `tools/make-voice.py`, description in HANDOFF.md); `VOICE_V` 11. **Not yet:** the roll in the tray still opens as Evangelist's (the sealed roll, `KS.waxSeal`, is for later); the new coat; a free clip of the leaps and of his walking on without the burden. Nothing spent.

**Build 392: THE SEPULCHRE ON THE FAR SIDE, AS IN THE SKETCH (owner, shown the tomb at the slope's foot: "Why not on other side like in initial sketch?"; my plan, agreed: "Yes!").** He walks up past the cross and stops just beyond it (`STOP_X` = `CROSS_X + 112`), turns round to face it (his book-free turn played backwards, quickly and eased, `Ch.tv`, about 1.5 s; the clip and his standing picture are then drawn mirrored, `Ch.face` -1), and the burden falls off behind him to the right and rolls down the far side into the sepulchre in the hollow (`TOMB_X` 3840; the far side now falls to a hollow 70 above the near foot, rising a little beyond it; the tomb drawn mirrored, its mouth toward the slope). The wide shots are two: the opening over the long slope (`ph` 'look') and the burden's, from the cross to the tomb with the tomb's ground above the words. The Shining Ones come to him on the cross's side (left of it); the roll is held out toward him. TUMBLE is said a little sooner (the roll down is short now, about 3 s). The departure "the sepulchre at the slope's foot" is gone. Nothing spent.

**Build 400: A BIGGER CROSS AND HILL, THE CLIMB TOLD BY A CUT (owner: "cross needs to be bigger, relative to Christian, hill too just don't want it to take a long time to go up ... zoom in on Christian climbing and then fade slightly then unfade and zoom out and he's at top").** The cross is 340 units (was 196; he is about 150), its beam 186 wide and 14 thick (`CROSS_H`, `CROSS_BY`; the sun still rises behind the crossing); the hill rises 430 (`H_TOP`, was 240), so the far slope the burden rolls down is longer. He starts inside the walls (`START` 480, was 120). The climb: from `CLOSE0` the camera comes in close on him (`closeK`, `z` 1.25); at `CUT_X` (`SLOPE0` + 380) the child's hold is let go, he climbs on while the picture dips softly toward dark (`veil`, at most .86, never black), he is set near the top (`SKIP_TO` = `CROSS_X` - 300), and the picture comes up on a wide look from the top, he and the cross high, the hill falling away below (`topK`, `z` .4); "He came to a place somewhat ascending ..." is said there, then the camera comes back down to him and the child walks the last steps to the cross (`climb`, `climbTick()`). `CR.peek()` gives `closeK`, `topK`, `veil`. Nothing spent.

**Build 401: THE HILL TWICE AS HIGH (owner: "Can you make hill twice as big (yes I know dwarfs him, but I want it to be epic)").** `H_TOP` 860 (was 430); the far side falls to the hollow over a longer stretch (`FALL1` 4000, `TOMB_X` 4080); the cut comes a little lower on the slope (`CUT_X` = `SLOPE0` + 300), where it is still gentle underfoot. The two wide shots fit the taller hill: the opening's zoom is held so hill and cross fit (`Math.min` in `camTick`), and the burden's wide shot is fitted from the tomb to the cross's top (`fitT`), the tomb's ground lifted above the words (`gh(TOMB_X) - 90`). Nothing spent.

**Build 402: THE SLOPE AS GENTLE AS BEFORE (owner: "Not necessarily twice as steep - I like the old slope (maybe a tad steeper is ok)").** The hill keeps its 860 but is longer: the crown at `CREST0` 4080 (was 2860), the cross at `CROSS_X` 4260, the far side falling from `CREST1` 4480 to `FALL1` 5400, the tomb at `TOMB_X` 5480; so the rise is about 1.15 times as steep as build 400's. The cut over the climb makes the extra length cost no time; the opening wide look stands back to the whole span (`VW / (CROSS_X - START + 900)`). Nothing spent.

**Build 403: THE SHINING ONES AS DIFFUSE HUMAN SHAPES OF LIGHT (owner: "The shining ones need very diffuse human shape").** Each is a human silhouette (head, neck, shoulders, a long robe to the ground, arms at the sides; the third holds one arm out with the roll) drawn white once (`shineFig()`, `SHF`), blurred smoothly by halving it four or five times and doubling it back step by step (shrinking in one jump left blocky heads), tinted warm white to gold and faded toward the feet; laid over a wide soft glow with 'screen' (with 'lighter' they burned to flat white on the bright dawn sky). Nothing spent.

**Build 407: THE SEALED ROLL GLOWS UNTIL IT IS HANDED TO HIM (owner: "How about the scroll be glowing too and be diffuse until it's handed to Christian").** In the third one's hand the roll is only a soft light (a wide glow, a warm gold halo that breathes, an elongated white core; no parchment shape). A touch starts it on its way (`rollFly`, `ROLL_FLY` 80 ticks): it is carried in a low arc to his hands (in front of him, `Ch.x - 24`), its glow drawing in and the parchment with its red seal coming clear over the second half; a moment there, then it drifts into the tray (`G.roll` from where it is, `rollAt`) and the talk goes on. The ring does not come back while it flies. `CR.peek().fly`. Nothing spent.

**Build 410: THE CROSS AS HEWN TIMBER (owner: "Can you make cross more realistic").** `crossShape()` draws it as two real beams: the upright 22 units at the foot tapering to 17, its sides left rough by the adze (small facets), its top sawn on a slant; the beam 190 by 19, a little thinner at its ends, a hair lower on the left, its ends sawn out of true. One dark tone as before; inside it, baked once (`bakeCross()`, `CRB`, at 3 pixels a unit), faint long grain, two drying splits in the upright and one in the beam, the lap joint (the beam's edges cut into the upright, a shadow under it, two pegs), two knots, the adze's broad facets, and the foot darker with weathering. Its foot is wedged in a low cairn of stones and packed earth on the ground line. At dawn the gold edge follows only its outer outline (`CRB.rim`: the shape less itself shrunk, baked once; a stroke traced the joint and the buried foot). Nothing spent.

**Build 411: THE PLAIN CROSS AGAIN (owner: "I think I prefer the simplicity of the original - can we (no need for rock support)").** Build 410's hewn timber (`crossShape`, `bakeCross`, the cairn) is taken out; `cross()` is build 409's again: two plain dark bars, a little rough at the edges, at build 400's size, no stones at its foot. **Keep the cross plain; do not bring back grain, pegs, splits or a cairn.** Nothing spent.

**Build 412: THE PLAIN CROSS'S EDGES A HAIR UNEVEN (owner: "Maybe ever slight variation to the straight edges to make it look slightly (very slightly) more wooden").** Still two plain dark bars, no texture: each edge wanders very slightly along its length (`wav`: two slow sines and a fine roughness, under a unit either way), the upright narrows by 7% to its top, and the ends are sawn a touch out of true. The dawn's gold edge follows the upright's own sides (`UL`, `UR`) and its top, and no longer crosses the beam or runs below the ground. Nothing spent.

**Build 414: A LITTLE LESS (owner: "Little less variation").** The cross's edges wander about half as much as build 412's (`wav`: .25 and .16, roughness .18) and the upright narrows by 4% to its top (was 7%). Nothing spent.

**Build 424: HE STAYS FACING THE CROSS, THE SEPULCHRE BELOW HIM TO THE LEFT, A BETTER GRAVE (owner: "grave needs to be better - also I'm not sure about switching his orientation, walking past cross is strange, maybe sepulcher can be below him and a little to the left and then burden can roll down and down").** Build 392's turn is gone: he walks up and stops short of the cross, facing it (`STOP_X` = `CROSS_X - 300`; `SKIP_TO` `CROSS_X - 640`), and the burden falls off behind him and rolls back down the slope he climbed (`burdenTick`: leftward) into the sepulchre in a hollow of the hillside (`TOMB_X` 3080, about 1200 units down the slope, 250 below him: the hollow is a level floor sunk 22 into the slope, `hs`/`w` in `gh()`, easing out over 620 uphill and 760 downhill). **The grave** (`tomb()`, `TOMB`, `TOMB_K` 1.45): a low weathered outcrop of rock rising from the hollow, a breath lighter than the land, darker at its foot; a round-headed doorway hewn in it seen straight on (a dressed surround a little lighter, dark within, a worn sill); the great round stone rolled aside on its edge to the left; a thin warm edge on its top at dawn. The burden rolls to the doorway, shrinks and darkens into it and is gone. The burden's wide shot frames the tomb and the cross (`cx` their middle less 50, width their span + 820). The Shining Ones stand between him and the cross, the third (with the roll) nearest him, drawn mirrored so its hand reaches toward him; the roll is carried to his hands in front of him. Nothing spent.

**Build 425: THE HILL AS BEFORE, THE SEPULCHRE ON ITS SLOPE (owner: "Hold up, hill should be shaped same as before, sepulchre can be in middle of screen at ground level but in the middle of the hill").** Build 424's hollow is taken out of `gh()`: the hill is build 402's shape again. The sepulchre stands on the open slope partway up (`TOMB_X` 3000, about 1000 units below the cross along the slope), drawn after the land: the rock's height is measured from the slope under it (`gl()` in `tomb()`), so its foot follows the hill and its two ends run down into it; its shading turned to the slope; the round stone stands on the ground beside the door. The burden's wide shot centres a little right of the tomb (`cx` `TOMB_X + 250`), so the tomb sits near the middle of the picture with the cross on the right. **A `//` comment swallowed the rock's lower edge while building this (a gap showed between rock and hill): comments on their own line.** Nothing spent.

**Build 427: THE SEPULCHRE CUT INTO THE BODY OF THE HILL (owner: "No down on the ground like in the body of hill").** No rock standing on the slope: in the flat side view the dark mass under the slope's outline is the hill's face toward us, and the tomb is hewn into it, below the surface (`tomb()`, `tombSill()`: the sill `DOOR_H` + 34 below the lower of the surface heights either side of the door): a rough cut face a breath lighter than the hill from a narrow ledge up to the slope's outline, ragged at its sides; a round-headed doorway (`DOOR_W` 38, `DOOR_H` 104) with a dressed surround, dark within; the great round stone rolled aside on the ledge. The burden rolls down the slope to above the door, then drops down the cut face onto the sill and into the dark (`bur.st` 'in': falls under gravity to the sill, shrinks, darkens, fades). The wide shot fits from the sill to the cross's top. Nothing spent.

**Build 428: THE SEPULCHRE LOWER AND FURTHER RIGHT (owner: "Better but down at bottom of screen and more to the right").** `TOMB_X` 3550 (was 3000), right under where he comes up after the climb and below him to the left as he stands at the cross; the burden's wide shot is centred just right of it (`cx` `TOMB_X + 80`, fitted to reach the cross) and sets the sill just above the words (`wh` = `tombSill() - 12`), so the tomb sits low and near the middle of the picture with Christian and the cross above on the right. Nothing spent.

**Build 429: THE SEPULCHRE AT THE HILL'S FOOT, BELOW THE CROSS (owner: "At bottom of hill not on slope but on ground level below cross - when zoomed out it should be pretty much below cross (maybe to the left of the point on the zoomed out ground at the base of the cross)").** `TOMB_X` 4100 (160 left of the cross), its sill at the level of the plain (`tombSill()` 6), cut into the foot of the hill's face: a rough hewn patch round the door, ragged all round, its top a low arch (`DOOR_H` + 70), the round stone rolled aside, the ledge. The burden no longer rolls along the slope: from behind him it tumbles down the hill's face toward us (`bur.H`, its own height; 230 ticks, faster and faster, six bounces each with a thump, growing a little as it comes nearer, a little lighter so it reads on the dark hill) to the door, then into the dark. The burden's wide shot stands back to show the cross and the tomb together (`cx` `CROSS_X - 60`, fitted from the sill to the cross's top). Nothing spent.

**Build 432: THE SEPULCHRE A CAVE, THE HILL GIVEN A LITTLE FORM (owner: "sepulchre should be more like a cave and more integrated into hill side and base (which is darker color) (very subtle but beautiful) you could shade hill a slight bit to get sense of its 3d and how pack is rolling down").** (1) **The cave** (`tomb()`, `MOUTH`): no hewn face, surround or ledge: an uneven round-headed dark mouth in the hill's foot, deepest at its top, a soft deeper shadow round it (a radial wash, squashed, no hard edge), the rock's brow over it catching a faint line of sky light, a second faint line of the brow above, and the round stone half sunk at its left in the hill's own darkened tone with a faint lit edge. (2) **The hill's form** (in `land()`, clipped to the land): a touch lighter just under its outline, darker toward its foot (so the cave sits in the darker base); and one soft broad spur coming toward us from where he stands down to the cave, a little lighter down its middle and darker at its flanks, baked once small and drawn stretched (`spur()`, `SPUR`: 25 soft pools along the line widening toward the foot; drawn per frame as gradients it showed grain and was too strong). The burden tumbles down that spur. Nothing spent.

**Build 433: NO LIGHT PATH, THE HILL IN LAYERS OF ROCK (owner: "The light path doesn't look good - maybe give the hill more rock texture (with layers of different shades - subtle but beautiful").** Build 432's spur (`spur()`) is gone. `rockTex()` (`ROCK`, baked once at half the world's size, drawn stretched inside the land's clip): bands of rock lying under the hill's outline, each a shade lighter or darker than the next (light and dark alpha, so they take the hill's own colour through the dawn), their edges wandering; along some a broken dark seam with a faint lit lip above it; a fine grain over all. Also fixed: the gold edge along the crown at dawn showed as a row of beads (short overlapping strokes with round caps): now one stroke whose strength follows a gradient. Nothing spent.

**Build 434: ROCKIER LAYERS, A QUIETER CAVE (owner: "The sepluchre can be more integrated and subtle. Maybe the layers can have a little more rockiness to them so it doesn't stand out (at first glance you might not see it)").** (1) **The layers:** each band's edge breaks in small steps and chips (value noise on 26 and 9 unit cells, `VN`, traced every 4 units), the seams fainter, and joints run across each band now and then with a few faint dark blocks (light blocks were tried and read as pink bricks: taken out). (2) **The cave:** its mouth a little more uneven, dark at its heart and melting into the rock at its edge (the shape laid three times, larger and fainter, then a radial dark), the brow's lit line at a third of its strength and the second line gone, the round stone fainter. Nothing spent.

**Build 436: MORE EDGE AND VARIETY IN THE ROCK (owner: "Can you add a little more edge and variety to rock layers").** In `rockTex()`: the bands now differ much more, thin and thick (18 to about 140 units), smooth and rough (`rough` .4 to 1.9, with a third finer noise of 4-unit cells), some leaning a little (`tilt`), each its own shade (a wider spread); each band's foot sits in a narrow shadow under the next band's lip, so every layer reads as a shelf; the seams longer, darker and more often unbroken, and a faint lit lip on most of them. Still in light and dark alpha, so it takes the hill's colour through the dawn. Nothing spent.

**Build 438: THE HILL A GRADIENT, ITS ROCK SHAPED LIKE LEVEL 3'S CRAGS (owner: "don't like that it looks like strata - should be gradient - light to dark... I like the rock shape of the scenery around Christian in mt scene in level three").** Build 433 to 436's bands, seams and joints are gone. The hill now goes light under its outline to dark at its foot (`land()`: a light .1 at the top easing to a dark .5 at the foot), and `rockTex()` lays four faint rows of Level 3's breaking-wave spurs (`crag()` ported from `hill.js`: a steep left face, a rounded brow, a long fall right), each row deeper under the outline and a touch darker; over them the burning hill's own painted rock (`hill-rock.webp`, the same crop as Level 3's `ROCKTEX`, made grey and mirrored into a tile, `RTEX`/`RPAT`) at .2 in 'overlay', laid large (2.4) so its repeat does not show. Nothing spent.

**Build 440: THE HILL'S ROCK LESS JAGGED (owner: "Maybe not quite so much jaggedness but much better in terms of color").** The colours of build 438 kept; in `rockTex()` the spurs are about twice as wide (`seg` 140 to 300), a quarter lower (130 to 250), their steep left face eased in over a longer stretch (`smooth(-.3, 0, u)`), and the fine roughness along their tops halved and slowed, so the rows read as broad rounded masses. Nothing spent.

**Build 443: HE RUNS UP THE HIGHWAY, AS THE OPENING LINE SAYS (owner: "it says he's running at beginning - we should match that").** The book ({92}): "Up this way, therefore, did burdened Christian run, but not without great difficulty". From the start up to the cut on the slope, holding now runs him in his plain run (`RUNP`, Level 1's, at its video's own pace: `run()` in `cross.js`, `Ch.run`, `Ch.ranim`; a quick blend from his standing picture and back when the finger lifts); the climb's cut keeps him running; after the cut, at the top, he walks the last steps to the cross (the book: "He ran thus till he came at a place somewhat ascending"). The plain run still has the pouch at his belt (as in Level 3). The run to the cut takes about 5 s of holding (was about 18). Nothing spent.

**Build 444: HIS LIFE REMEMBERED IN THE SKY (owner: "in the sky is full of flash back images of his life - like his mind thinking of the providence of God"; then "Stand, 6 memories but quickly almost overlapping in frequency and punch, yes leave out birth and mother voice").** In the silence after the burden is gone, before the sun (`ph` 'gone'), six memories come one after another about a second apart, each overlapping the last, in the upper sky (`MEMS`, `memories()`, `memPic()`, `memVoice()` in `cross.js`): Evangelist pointing and the far light ("Do you see that shining light?"), the run ("Life! Life! Eternal life!"), his wife in the lit door ("Come back! Come home to us!"), Help reaching down ("Give me your hand."), Evangelist raising him as he kneels ("God-speed."), and last Good-will reaching ("Bear your burden a little longer. At the place of deliverance, it will fall from your back of itself."), the promise heard as it comes true; the sun rises during it and Amazing Grace begins once it is said. Each is the game's own pictures (`EVA`, `RUNP`, `wife.webp`, `lift.pack`, `KNEEL`, `STD`, `goodwill-reach.webp`) as dark warm figures in a soft warm oval of light that fades at every edge, comes in with a brief brightening, a soft hiss and a low note, drifts a little and fades; the voices are the existing recordings, played over each other without cutting (`VOICEG`). He stands, as in the book ("as he stood looking and weeping", {94}). **Departure:** the memories are ours (the book: "He looked therefore, and looked again"). The moment is about 11 s longer. Nothing spent.

**Build 447: NO CUT ON THE CLIMB, HE LEANS WITH THE SLOPE (owner: "Since he's running you can probably not cut to top of hill since it won't take very long to go up, also tilt him ever so slightly back so his feet land right as he runs up hill").** Build 400's cut over the climb (the dip toward dark, the jump to `SKIP_TO`, the wide look from the top) and its close-up (`closeK`) are no longer used (`climbTick` kept, not called): holding runs him the whole way from the walls up the slope to the cross (`STOP_X`; about 13 s of holding up the hill), "He came to a place somewhat ascending ..." said as the cross comes into view (`CREST0 - 900`), and the clip begins once his run has settled into his standing picture (`Ch.run <= 0`). While running he is drawn turned back about his feet by .6 of the ground's own angle (`TILT`, `slopeTilt()`, in `figure()`), so his feet land on the slope; upright on the flat and at the cross. Nothing spent.

**Build 448: THE MEMORIES COME ON THE RUN UP THE HILL (owner: "the memories maybe happen as he runs up the hill (too much overlapping dialogue at cross)").** Build 444's six memories (`MEMS`) now begin once he is on the slope and the storyteller's opening line has been said (`memT` starts in the `walk` phase; its clock runs on in `update()` whatever the phase, so the last fades even at the cross), placed in the sky left of and above the rising hill; Good-will's promise comes last, about as he reaches the cross. "He came to a place somewhat ascending ..." waits for them, and the burden loosens only after it (`said.ascend` in the stop condition). At the cross it is quiet again as in build 443: a silence after the burden falls, the sun at once (`t` 150) and the hymn (`t` 210). `?at=cross` skips them. Nothing spent.

**Build 449: HIS TEARS (owner asked for his eyes and tears in the sky; my advice against it, eyes would break the plain-face rule, could unsettle a child, and crowd the sunrise; offered instead, "Sure!!").** On "He looked, and looked again, till the tears ran down his cheeks." (the second LOOKED line, once the caption queue reaches it: `looked`, `tearT`) the camera comes in close on him (`closeK` in the `gone` phase: `z` 1.45, `cx` him + 130, so the cross and the sun behind it stay in the picture) and five small drops of light (`TEARS`, drawn in `drawChr` after the clip with 'lighter', at his cheek: `TEAR_X` 10.5, `TEAR_Y` 124) gather one after another, run a little down his cheek, then fall faster and fade before they reach the ground; the camera draws back as the Shining Ones come. His face stays plain. `CR.peek().tear`. Nothing spent.


**Build 450: SHARPER MEMORIES THAT MELT INTO THE SKY, NO TWO VOICES AT ONCE, THE BURDEN ZIG-ZAGS DOWN (owner: "the resolution of the memories isn't great, also they should maybe blend in a little more with surroundings (edge transistions not great) - finally in this level the dialogue sometimes overlaps if you got too fast, is there way to add choices or sequence things so this doesn't happen (even a rule could work)"; "when his burden falls it should zig zag down the hill to give it depth"; "memories could move locations if sequencing is necessary").** (1) Each memory is baked at three times its size (`memPic`, `Z` 3: 900 x 600, drawn in its old 300 x 200 units), the soft blur over it lighter (.3 from a sixth-size copy) and shown a little larger (.36 of the width); its warm light dimmer (.4) and its edge fading over a long soft stretch (six stops, from .25 of the radius out), laid at .74, its first flash fainter, so it melts into the dusk sky. (2) **THE RULE FOR LEVEL 5: no line is spoken, and no memory speaks, while any voice is still sounding** (`busy()` = `saying()` or a memory's voice still playing, `memUntil` in game ticks from the recording's length). Each memory comes only when the one before has had its time (`MEM_GAP`) and no voice sounds (`memS`, the tick each began); if he runs fast they simply come later, on to the top or at the cross; the storyteller's ascent line waits for the last, and he waits at the cross (the burden does not loosen) until it is said. Every `!saying()` in the update is now `!busy()`. (3) The burden tumbling down the hill's face toward us zig-zags (a turn at each bounce, up to 150 units each side, narrowing near the cave, its spin turning with it) and grows to 1.3 as it comes nearer (`bur.zx`). Nothing spent. **For any level: a line never starts over another voice; hold the child at a stop point instead.**


**Build 451: THE LEVEL OPENS CLOSE ON HIM AT THE WALLS (owner: "at the start of this level can we not show it zoomed out with the cross just the opening wall and him standing there").** The wide first look over the long slope and the cross (`ph` 'look', build 390) is gone: the level comes up on the near camera, Christian standing between the walls of Salvation (`wideK = wideTo = 0` in `start`), the storyteller's first line said, and the child may run after about 2.5 s (was 5.5). The cross is first seen as he climbs. Nothing spent.


**Build 452: FINER MEMORIES, A QUIET BEFORE THE STORYTELLER, A LOOK DOWN AT THE SEPULCHRE, A WALK AT THE TOP (owner: "can we crank up the resolution of the memories (seems a little pixelated) (ok with blurry)"; "can you put a few seconds of silence after last memory before narrator begins"; "when narrator say sepluchre can we zoom down to see it and then back up"; "once he's at the top with the cross he can walk").** (1) Each memory is baked at four times its size (`Z` 4: 1200 x 800), and its soft blur is made by halving three times and doubling back step by step (one big stretch from a small copy showed blocks), laid at .42. (2) The ascent line waits about three seconds of quiet after the last memory's voice ends (`memDone` needs `G.t >= memUntil + 180`). (3) On "... and a little below, a sepulchre." (`sepT`, from the line's start): at 2.5 s the camera draws back and down to the burden's wide shot (`wideTo` 1: the cross above, the sepulchre's mouth low in the hill), holds about 4.5 s, and comes back up to him; he stands still while it looks (`sepLook`), and the burden does not loosen until it is back. (4) He runs up the slope, and from `WALK_X` (`STOP_X - 380`, where the hill flattens at the top) he walks the last steps to the cross (`walk()`, a brief settle from the run). `CR.peek().sep`. Nothing spent.


**Build 454: NO VOICES OVER EACH OTHER, A SHORTER LAST MEMORY, A SLOWER TUMBLE (owner: "still have an issue with overlapping audio - maybe we should make last memories a little shorter"; "the tumbling of burden can take time (audio skips beceause it goes too fast)").** (1) **The likely cause of the overlap:** a memory's recording slow to load on a phone (over 2 s) let the next memory start, then the late one played over it. Now the memory voices are fetched as the level starts (`MEMS.forEach(m => vox(...))` in `start`), nothing else may speak while one loads (`memUntil` = 10 s until it arrives), and a voice that arrives more than 1.5 s late is not played at all. (2) **Good-will's memory is only its second sentence**, "At the place of deliverance, it will fall from your back of itself." (`from: 1`; `memCut(buf)` finds the longest quiet stretch after the first second of the recording, 4.0 s into its 9.1 s, and plays from there), about 5 s instead of 9; no new recording. (3) **The tumble twice as long** (`T` 470 ticks, was 230; eight bounces, was six), so "It began to tumble ... I saw it no more." keeps pace with it; and after it is gone the silence before the sun and the hymn is counted only while no voice sounds (`quietG`), so they never come over the words. Nothing spent.


**Build 456: A SLOWER SUNRISE, A BREATH BEFORE CHRISTIAN SPEAKS (owner: "sun can rise slower and slight delay between burden disappearing and christian speaking").** The sun rises over about 14 s (`sun` +.0012 a tick, was .0028, about 6 s) and the colour comes back about half as fast (`gold` .0032, was .006). Christian's "He hath given me rest by his sorrow ..." (GLAD) now waits for 12 beats of the hymn and at least 2.5 s of unbroken quiet (`qRun`), and LOOKED follows it. Nothing spent.


**Build 457: THE SHINING ONES APPEAR (owner: "the shining ones can just appear (not descend)").** They no longer come down from above or rise when they go: each fades in where it stands, one after another, and fades there when they go (`up` 0 in `shining()`). Nothing spent.


**Build 458: THE SHINING ONES NAMED, PAUSES BETWEEN VOICES (owner: "narrator say the second, but there's no context for this ... let's assume viewers don't always read", then "Yes, just make sure this level has sufficient pauses between clips so persons can be distinguished"; about 1 cent).** (1) A new storyteller line as they appear, from the book ({94}): "As he stood looking and weeping, three Shining Ones came to him." (first line of `SHINE`, recorded first take, `assets/voice/5a25c2d4.mp3`), so "the second" and "the third" make sense by ear. (2) **Pauses in Level 5:** in a talk, about 1.25 s of quiet after each line before the next (`G.autoT` + 45, once per line, `pauseKey`); the storyteller's captions wait a second after the last recording ends (in `index.html`, `+ (CR.on ? 60 : 12)`; other levels unchanged). Spent: about 1 cent.


**Build 460: A MORE NATURAL TUMBLE, A LIGHT ROUND IT, A REAL GOING-IN (owner: "can you make the tumbling more natural maybe extra little bounce in between each turn around point, and maybe some light around it so we can see as it falls down the dark hill... finally the entering the sepluchre could use some work").** In `burdenTick()`/`drawBurden()`: (1) seven legs down the face, each one big hop (most of the leg, crossing from one side to the other) and then a little hop (15 against 50, hardly moving across) before it turns; a thump at each landing, softer for the little ones; it spins quickly in the big hop's air and slowly in the little one, its spin turning with each leg. (2) A soft warm light round it as it falls (`bur.gl`, 'screen', 70 units), and it is drawn a little lighter, so it can be followed down the dark hill; the light fades as it goes in. (3) **The going-in:** it rolls slowly up to the mouth and stops, rocks on the lip (a damped rocking, a small knock), and waits there until the storyteller is about 5.5 s into "It began to tumble ..." (`tumbleT`; at most about 10 s), then tips over backward into the dark: smaller as it goes deeper, rising a little in the mouth, darkening to black, a dull thud from inside and a faint breath of dust at the mouth (`bur.dust`). `CR.peek().bur` gives `a` and `t`. Nothing spent.


**Build 461: THE TUMBLE BY PHYSICS (owner, of build 460's scripted zig-zag: "the tumbling burden is looking worse and worse (need a natural fall - think about physics of fall, it's not too important the path it takes so long as it is natural)").** The zig-zag and the fixed big-and-little hops are gone. `burdenTick()` now simulates it, tick by tick, with a seeded random (`rgen(77)`, so it falls the same way every time): it runs down the hill's face toward us, gathering speed under gravity with a little friction (up to 2.4 units a tick), the face easing off at its foot so it comes to the mouth slower; the rough face throws it up now and then (more often and higher the faster it goes, `vz`); in the air it falls under gravity (.16) and bounces back 38 to 50% of its speed, with a thump as strong as the landing; each bounce knocks it a little sideways (a drift `dx` that wanders and is drawn back, so its path runs nearly straight down with a slight wander) and changes its spin; on the ground it rolls with its speed, in the air it keeps the spin the last bounce gave it; drawn larger as it comes nearer. About 10 s from his back to the mouth. The soft light round it, the rocking on the lip and the going-in (build 460) are unchanged. `CR.peek().bur` gives `H`, `z`, `r`. Nothing spent. **Rule: a falling or bouncing thing is simulated (gravity, a bounce that loses speed, friction), never scripted as a fixed pattern.**


**Build 464: ONE WAY ROUND, A SOFT SACK, THE CAPE CLEANED (owner: "can you make it rotate one direction as it falls, also as it comes off shoulders, there seems to be a cropping problem with cape"; "the burden can change shape as it falls, imagine its a burden of loose items (thought this may not look good)").** (1) The burden turns one way only as it tumbles (`bur.w` kept below -.006; a bounce only adds to it). (2) **A soft sack, not a ball:** it squashes as it lands, by how hard it lands (up to .35), wobbles back on a spring, and stretches a little in the air (`bur.sq`, drawn as a scale before the rotation, its foot kept on the ground); it stays one bundle, since the book has it fall into the sepulchre whole. Loose things scattering was not done. (3) **`christian-cross.pack` cleaned** (`PACK_V` 40): in frames 34 to 40, as the burden slides off, a pale triangle of paper shut in between his back, the ropes and the bundle was cleared (pale pieces over 150 px, with a 2 px margin), and a little paper on the ground under the bundle; in frames 41 to 80 the back edge of his cape, a hard stair-stepped upright cut left where the bundle was cut away, is smoothed (per row, the edge's median over 17 rows then averaged over 9, filled from the cape beside it or cleared, a soft last pixel). Nothing spent.


**Build 466: EACH MEMORY TIED TO A PLACE ON THE HILL (owner: "i'm cool if memories show up in different places, but once initiated you tie memories to locations so that they are sufficiently spaced out and don't overlap with other narrator text").** Each of the six memories now has its own place on the slope (`MEM_X`: `SLOPE0` + 250, 700, 1150, 1600, 2000, and `WALK_X - 80` for Good-will's at the top) and comes only when he has reached it, at least `MEM_GAP` after the one before, and after a full second of quiet since the last voice (`memQ`, ticks with no voice sounding, 60 needed); if a voice is still sounding it waits and comes a little further on. If he stops, they wait for him. Measured in a full run holding all the way (voices loading as in a browser): the storyteller's first line, then the memories at x 1685, 2144, 2835, 3425, 3638, 3734 (about 4 to 7 s apart), the ascent line about 10 s after the last. `CR.peek().mem` gives how many have come. Nothing spent.

A first finish line: **Level 5, the Cross** (the burden carried since the first screen falls off), so Levels 1 to 5 stand as a whole game. Could be about 14 by also joining the Delectable Mountains with Ignorance, and the Enchanted Ground into the last level. Rough size: about $3 a level for pictures, videos and voices, more where there are many new people.
